332A Answer Keys and Explanations

Numbered to match the practice tests in your printed study guide.

Practice Test 1 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (It breaks down synthetic dye molecules so they can be rinsed from the hair.): A colour reducer is formulated specifically to break down artificial colour molecules deposited by a previous service, allowing them to be rinsed out. It targets synthetic dye and generally has little effect on the hair's own natural melanin. Lightening powder, by contrast, works primarily on natural melanin and is a less targeted tool for stripping artificial colour.
  2. The correct answer is D (The perimeter is shortened gradually but is not brought down to bare skin.): A taper is defined by shortening the perimeter gradually while never taking it down to bare skin, which produces the more conservative result the client wants compared with a skin fade. Bringing the perimeter to bare skin would turn the cut into a fade rather than a taper. Keeping this distinction in mind guides which guards and blending technique to select.
  3. The correct answer is D (The exact product and formula, processing time applied, and rod size or application technique.): A proper chemical service record includes the exact product and formula used, including brand and strength, the processing time actually applied, and the rod size and wrapping pattern for a perm or the application technique for a relaxer, along with the hair's condition beforehand. Recording only the client's name and price leaves out the chemical detail future services depend on. A stylist's general opinion of the result is not a substitute for this specific technical record. Appointment length alone does not capture what product or technique was actually used.
  4. The correct answer is A (A mousse, which delivers light to medium hold and lift without weighing hair down.): Mousse is a foam, a liquid formula that has been aerated so it distributes lightly and evenly through the hair, and it generally provides light to medium hold with added volume and lift. Applied at the root it builds height without the weighting effect of a heavier cream or gel. A firm gel would give strong hold but dry heavier, and a silicone serum is a finishing product for shine rather than volume.
  5. The correct answer is B (Sanitize the bowl to remove the remaining residue before applying any disinfectant.): Any dried product residue left on a surface can shield microorganisms and interfere with a disinfectant's ability to work correctly. Sanitizing the bowl to physically remove the streaked residue is the required first step, performed before disinfectant is applied. Applying disinfectant over residue, or skipping cleaning altogether, does not achieve the intended result on a mixing tool that contacts the client's scalp. A visibly clean bowl at the start of the process supports an effective disinfection outcome.
  6. The correct answer is A (Early scalp conditions are often seen at close range during a routine shampoo.): A consistent shampoo protocol gives the stylist a built-in chance to re-examine the scalp at close range each visit, which is often how early signs of a developing condition are first noticed. Clients rarely examine their own scalp closely, so the stylist is often the first to spot a change. Catching a condition early allows a referral before service. This is a screening observation, not a diagnosis.
  7. The correct answer is A (That certificate may only be automatically recognized within the issuing province.): A Certificate of Qualification without the Red Seal endorsement may only be automatically recognized within the province or territory that issued it, since the endorsement is specifically what supports interprovincial labour mobility. It is not invalid everywhere, it simply may not transfer automatically to another jurisdiction without further steps. A stylist without the endorsement can still be employed, particularly in a voluntary certification jurisdiction. The certificate does not expire automatically after a fixed one-year period.
  8. The correct answer is D (Explain each upcoming step clearly and confirm the client's comfort before proceeding.): A first-time client genuinely benefits from a clear explanation of each step in the process, including preparation steps like the hot towel and lather, before the actual shave begins. This helps the client relax and builds real trust in the service being provided. Rushing through the steps or skipping preparation entirely would not address the client's clearly stated nervousness about the appointment.
  9. The correct answer is C (Work it through evenly with a brush and bowl, then monitor it visually and rinse.): A toner is applied after lightening to neutralize warmth and deposit the desired tone. Working it through evenly, monitoring the deposit visually, and rinsing when it reaches the tone keeps the result clean. Following only the clock or leaving thick uneven sections risks patchy, muddy tone.
  10. The correct answer is B (The cutting line chosen has to serve the client's actual features and stated preference.): Consultation and technical planning are treated as one connected process, since the cutting line chosen has to serve the client's actual features and stated preference, such as using a diagonal forward line to soften angular features. It is not true that cutting line has no relationship to face shape, or that face shape only matters after the cut is finished. Different face shapes are addressed with different cutting line strategies, not one uniform approach.
  11. The correct answer is A (It breaks bonds in hair whose structure has already been altered, weakening it further.): Previously relaxed hair has already had its internal structure altered once, and overlapping fresh relaxer onto it breaks bonds that have no healthy structure left to protect, which weakens the hair and often causes visible breakage. Already-straightened hair does still absorb additional relaxer, which is exactly what makes overlapping dangerous rather than inert. The risk is to the hair's physical strength, not its colour, and overlapping does not reverse or undo previous straightening, it compounds the chemical damage instead.
  12. The correct answer is A (Place them half off base or fully off base, angled down and away from the scalp.): Rollers placed half off base or fully off base are angled down and away from the scalp, which reduces root lift while still producing curl through the length of the hair. A roller set directly on base and wound straight up produces maximum volume and lift at the scalp. This placement is the right choice when a softer, less bouffant finish is wanted near the crown.
  13. The correct answer is C (Sustained hunched posture increases strain on the neck, shoulders, and lower back over time.): Sustained hunched posture increases strain on the neck, shoulders, and lower back, and this strain accumulates across a shift and across a career rather than resolving on its own. Hunching is not a recommended posture for any length of service, and posture related strain applies to haircutting just as much as to chemical services. Focusing only on the finished result while ignoring posture overlooks a real occupational health risk. Adjusting chair height and periodically straightening the back reduces this specific strain.
  14. The correct answer is B (Choose caution, postpone the service, and make a tactful referral framed around client wellbeing.): A stylist unsure whether a presentation is manageable or needs medical attention should always choose caution. Declining or postponing a service is a professional decision, not a failure, and it protects both the client's health and the salon's liability. A tactful referral framed around client wellbeing is the correct response. The exam expects referral as the correct action for an unclear presentation.
  15. The correct answer is B (Whether the stylist personally remits their own income tax and CPP contributions.): The clearest financial difference between booth rental and commission employment is who handles tax remittance. A booth renter is self-employed and must personally track income, remit their own Canada Pension Plan contributions, and file as a small business. A commission employee has those deductions withheld automatically by the salon. Personal preferences in service type, building location, and commuting method do not reflect this structural difference.
  16. The correct answer is B (Take a careful hairline template before her remaining hair loss progresses further.): For a client fitting a wig for medical reasons, taking a template of the hairline early, before hair loss progresses further, helps ensure the finished piece matches her original hairline as closely as possible. Waiting until all of her hair is gone removes the exact reference point the fitting depends on. Careful, early measurement directly supports this client's stated goal for the finished wig.
  17. The correct answer is D (It grabs colour unevenly, taking it up fast at the damaged points and releasing it quickly.): Porous hair accepts colour unevenly and often too fast, grabbing it heavily at the most damaged points along the strand. It then releases that colour just as quickly, which is why direct application produces patchy results. A target shade needs an evened-out base before it can act consistently.
  18. The correct answer is C (Clipper-over-comb technique, blending along the edge of the comb.): Clipper-over-comb technique combines a comb held against the head with the clipper blade run along the comb's edge, allowing a stylist to blend and taper hair to a length no fixed guard provides. A single fixed guard would leave a visible line rather than a gradient. A stationary guideline and a horizontal line at zero elevation are shear concepts, not clipper blending tools.
  19. The correct answer is A (A no-lye relaxer with a strand test first, since it balances gentleness against a strong result.): A no-lye relaxer offers a reasonable middle ground for this client, generally gentler on a sensitive scalp than a lye formula while still producing a genuinely straight, long-lasting result, and a strand test adds a direct check given the hair's porosity from prior colour treatment. Choosing a full-strength lye relaxer ignores the documented scalp sensitivity entirely. Doubling a thio relaxer past its recommended strength increases risk without a proven benefit to straightness. Cost alone is not a safe basis for a chemical recommendation when real differences in scalp gentleness and result exist between formula types.
  20. The correct answer is B (A paddle brush that is flat and wide for a smooth result.): A paddle brush is flat and wide and is chosen when the goal is maximum smoothness on long hair with minimal directional bending. A round brush bends and curls, while a vent brush preserves texture and movement. Brush choice works alongside angle and tension to control the outcome.
  21. The correct answer is D (A government inspector may be brought in to make a final determination on the concern.): When an employer's investigation does not resolve a reported safety concern, the defined process allows a government inspector to be called in to make a final determination. The concern is not automatically dismissed simply because time has passed, and repairing the hazard is the employer's responsibility, not the worker's. Completing an investigation does not by itself close the matter if the underlying hazard remains unresolved. This escalation step exists specifically for situations where the initial investigation does not settle the issue.
  22. The correct answer is D (Postpone the perm and stabilize the hair with reconstructive treatment before further chemicals.): Hair that stretches very little before snapping, or that stretches and does not spring back, has compromised elasticity pointing to weakened internal bonds from over-processing. It is a poor candidate for further chemical services until stabilized with reconstructive treatment. A gentler solution does not solve the underlying weakened cortex, and protein after a service is not a replacement for condition assessment. Postponing protects the hair from further damage.
  23. The correct answer is C (It still grants interprovincial recognition if the stylist later relocates elsewhere.): Even in a province where certification is voluntary, completing the Red Seal exam still grants the interprovincial recognition that supports labour mobility if the stylist later relocates to a different province or territory. It does not become an unnecessary formality locally, since it also allows the stylist to use protected titles and demonstrate a verified skill standard to employers and clients. Passing the exam does not change the province's own regulatory classification. Its value is not limited only to future salon ownership.
  24. The correct answer is D (Oil applied near the tape bonds can loosen them and shorten wear time.): Heavy oils applied close to tape wefts can loosen the adhesive bond over time, causing the extensions to slip loose or fail well earlier than expected. This is precisely why clients are generally advised to keep oil-based products away from the attachment area itself. Explaining this connection clearly helps the client adjust her at-home care routine going forward.
  25. The correct answer is C (It checks for an allergic reaction to the colour product's chemical ingredients.): A predisposition test, also called a patch test, is performed to detect whether a client will have an allergic reaction to ingredients in an oxidative colour product. A small amount of the mixed product is applied to the skin and observed for a waiting period before the service proceeds. Skipping this step risks a serious reaction during the actual colour application.
  26. The correct answer is B (Open, exposed tools left on a station can cause injury.): Tools should never be left open or exposed on a station where they could cause injury, which is exactly the risk created by leaving open shears within reach. Leaving shears open has no effect on how sharp the edge is. Both shears and clippers need to be handled and stored safely between uses, not just one or the other.
  27. The correct answer is C (To act as a barrier that keeps the strong alkaline chemical off the client's skin.): The protective base cream applied to the scalp before a lye relaxer service is a barrier, not a treatment, and its only job is to keep the strong alkaline chemical off the skin during processing given how quickly and aggressively lye relaxers can act. It is not intended to speed up processing, add moisture to the hair itself, or neutralize the relaxer's alkalinity, since neutralization happens later through the neutralizing shampoo step. Because lye relaxers act so quickly and aggressively, the base cream provides the skin protection that makes the service safer for the client.
  28. The correct answer is A (A large barrel round brush, which wraps the hair into a looser curve.): A large barrel round brush wraps the hair into a looser bend, which is why it suits longer hair or a soft wave result. A small diameter brush wraps the hair into a tighter curve and is better for short hair or defined curl. A vent brush preserves volume and movement instead of giving a smooth finish, and a paddle brush keeps hair straight rather than adding a wave.
  29. The correct answer is A (Ergonomics, which focuses on reducing physical strain through posture, tools, and task variation.): Ergonomics is the practice of arranging the work, the tools, and the stylist's own posture to reduce physical strain rather than working through it. This is distinct from triage, which relates to prioritizing care in an emergency, and from consultation and sterilization, which address entirely different parts of salon practice. Applying ergonomic principles to chair height, tool selection, and task variation is what protects a stylist's hands and body over a long career. Recognizing this term and what it covers is foundational to the topic.
  30. The correct answer is C (Decline or postpone the service and refer, since open sore and broken skin are contraindications.): General contraindications include any open sore, active infection, unexplained bleeding, or broken and severely inflamed skin on the scalp. A chemical service in this area could introduce irritation or infection and cause pain or injury. Declining or postponing is a professional decision, not a failure, and a tactful referral protects the client and the salon. Proceeding with the colour is not appropriate.
  31. The correct answer is B (Health-related personal information deserves discretion and should not be shared without consent.): Health-related details such as an allergy or skin condition are sensitive personal information, and clients generally expect that information to stay confidential. Sharing it with staff who have no reason to know is a breach of that reasonable expectation and can undermine client trust. There is no rule against coworkers knowing a client's name, and salon software is fully capable of storing such notes securely. Records also are not required to be destroyed after every visit.
  32. The correct answer is D (Whether the scrub could meaningfully disturb the freshly deposited colour molecules.): An exfoliating scalp scrub applied fairly soon after a colour service could genuinely disturb freshly deposited colour molecules, particularly close to the scalp where they were most recently applied. Assuming there is no interaction at all overlooks this real, practical concern for a client in this specific situation. Reprocessing the colour, or deliberately removing it, are not appropriate goals for what is meant to be a simple scalp treatment.
  33. The correct answer is C (Green results from mixing equal parts of yellow and blue.): Green is a secondary colour formed by combining yellow and blue in equal proportion. It sits opposite red on the colour wheel, which is why green based products are used to neutralize unwanted red or red-orange tones. Recognizing this relationship is central to corrective colour work.
  34. The correct answer is C (A diagonal back cutting line, shorter at front, longer toward the back.): A diagonal back cutting line angles the opposite way from diagonal forward, shorter at the front and longer toward the back, commonly used to build increasing length toward the nape and direct weight away from the face. A diagonal forward line does the reverse, adding length toward the front instead. A horizontal or vertical line does not create this directional weight shift.
  35. The correct answer is A (Small-diameter rods, since a tighter wrapping arc produces a smaller resulting curl.): Rod diameter is the main variable that controls curl size, and small-diameter rods wrap the hair through a tighter arc, which produces the small, tight curl this client wants. Large-diameter rods produce the opposite result, a loose wave rather than a tight curl. Rod choice is not interchangeable or arbitrary, and extending processing time on a medium rod changes how fully the curl forms, not the underlying size the rod itself is capable of producing.
  36. The correct answer is B (A fishtail braid, also known as a herringbone braid.): A fishtail, also called a herringbone braid, uses only two large sections of hair. Small pieces are crossed over alternately from each side, producing a fine woven texture. Because no new hair is picked up, it can start anywhere on a length of hair rather than only at the scalp.
  37. The correct answer is A (Chronic delays and rushed services can directly reduce how likely clients are to rebook.): Punctuality and reliable time management affect client retention directly, since clients who experience chronic delays or rushed services are less likely to rebook. This pattern shows a real connection between scheduling practices and whether clients return, not just the technical quality of the finished style. Dismissing scheduling as irrelevant to retention overlooks a documented and predictable consequence of poor time management. Building in realistic time estimates and reasonable buffers helps prevent this kind of client loss.
  38. The correct answer is B (A moisture treatment, which adds and holds water to improve flexibility.): Dry, stiff, brittle hair without stretch or breakage typically needs moisture rather than protein. Moisture-based treatments add and hold water within the cortex, improving flexibility and softness. Adding protein to already stiff, moisture-poor hair can leave it snapping. Reading elasticity and porosity first is what separates the right choice.
  39. The correct answer is D (Proof the stylist passed the national interprovincial exam for trade code 332A.): The Red Seal endorsement is granted only after a candidate passes the interprovincial Red Seal examination for their trade. It is stamped onto the provincial or territorial Certificate of Qualification and signals that the holder met one common national standard. This is what allows the credential to be recognized in other provinces and territories under labour mobility rules. A safety course, business registration, or a manufacturer class does not carry the same national recognition.
  40. The correct answer is A (Postpone the entire service until her irritation has fully and visibly resolved.): Active scalp irritation is a genuine reason to postpone an extension service rather than proceed as scheduled, since the fusion process still involves heat and close handling near the scalp area. Working carefully around one small section does not address the underlying irritation present across the rest of her scalp. Waiting until the reaction has fully resolved protects the client from further avoidable discomfort or complications.
  41. The correct answer is C (A blue based toner, because blue is complementary to orange on the wheel.): A blue based toner neutralises orange, which is the residual warmth typically seen at the lower to mid lightening levels. The colour wheel governs which toner corrects which warmth, because complementary colours opposite each other cancel each other out. Using a violet toner on strongly orange hair will not fully neutralise it and can leave a muddy tone.
  42. The correct answer is D (The straight razor, which tapers as it shaves through hair.): The straight razor naturally thins and softens the line as it cuts because it removes hair progressively along a beveled edge, making it useful for movement and soft, piece-y finishes. Shears at zero elevation produce a blunt, weight-heavy line, the opposite effect. Clippers and a comb alone do not create this tapered texture.
  43. The correct answer is D (Smaller subsections take longer but generally give a more defined, uniform curl result.): Smaller subsections wrapped with more rods hold the hair more precisely against each rod, which generally produces a more defined and uniform curl, but this takes more time to wrap than larger subsections with fewer rods. Subsection size does affect definition regardless of how evenly neutralizer is applied. Larger subsections tend to leave a looser, less defined pattern rather than a more defined one, and reducing rod count does not reliably cut appointment time by any fixed proportion.
  44. The correct answer is A (Perm rods, which give a firm well-defined curl pattern.): Perm rods come in the same range of diameters as rollers but have a more rigid, ridged construction. On wet, undamaged hair without a chemical service they create a firm, well-defined curl pattern, particularly on naturally textured or coily hair. A standard roller set does not achieve the same reliable separation and spring.
  45. The correct answer is B (Nitrile resists a broad range of chemicals and avoids the latex allergy risk that latex carries.): Nitrile gloves are the standard choice for chemical services because they resist a broad range of salon chemicals and do not carry the latex allergy risk that latex gloves present to some clients and stylists. Cost comparisons vary by supplier and are not the primary reason for the preference, and latex gloves remain legal to sell and use in Canada. Gloves of any material are disposable protective equipment and are not meant to be reused indefinitely. The chemical resistance and allergy consideration are what actually drive the standard recommendation.
  46. The correct answer is B (Dandruff, a manageable scalp condition that responds to regular anti-dandruff shampoo.): Dandruff, or pityriasis capitis, is an increased rate of skin cell turnover that produces flakes. It is generally cosmetic and manageable rather than a disease. Regular anti-dandruff shampoo improves it for many clients. The other conditions involve redness, greasy scale, or raised plaques that point elsewhere.
  47. The correct answer is D (Briefly acknowledge the concern, involve a manager, and arrange proper correction time.): Briefly acknowledging the client's concern, looping in a manager, and arranging a proper correction appointment addresses the complaint seriously without abandoning the client currently in the chair. Interrupting an in-progress service is unfair to the client already being served and does not actually provide the focused attention a correction needs. Telling the upset client to return whenever a slot opens dismisses the urgency they are expressing. Criticizing a colleague's work to validate the client is unprofessional and does not solve the underlying scheduling and correction problem.
  48. The correct answer is B (It temporarily straightens hair using heat, and it reverts once wet.): A silk press is a temporary straightening technique achieved entirely through blow-drying and flat-ironing rather than any chemical process. Because no chemical bond in the hair is permanently broken, the natural curl pattern returns as soon as the hair is exposed to moisture again. This makes it fundamentally different from a chemical relaxer, which produces a lasting structural change to the hair.
  49. The correct answer is C (Apply to the mid-lengths and ends first, then work the colour up to the scalp last.): The intact cuticle of untreated hair processes more slowly at the dense lower portions. Body heat at the scalp speeds development, so colour placed on the scalp first would finish darker than the ends. Working from the mid-lengths up to the scalp last keeps the level consistent.
  50. The correct answer is B (Zero degrees of elevation, holding hair straight down.): Cutting at zero degrees of elevation produces a blunt, weight-heavy line that keeps the maximum weight and density at the perimeter, which suits one-length and low-graduation shapes with a strong, solid outline. Ninety degrees and beyond remove more weight and build graduation or layering instead. Forty-five degrees builds moderate graduation rather than preserving maximum perimeter weight.
  51. The correct answer is D (A small fan alone does not match proper ventilation for chemical texture services.): A small desk fan near one station is not the same as a properly ventilated service area, so relying on it alone does not match the standard of good airflow expected for chemical texture services generally. Assuming one small fan is fully sufficient regardless of service volume overlooks how ventilation needs can scale with how much chemical work is happening. Fan placement and airflow quality genuinely matter beyond simply having a fan turned on somewhere in the room. Ventilation matters across all chemical texture services, not only smoothing treatments.
  52. The correct answer is C (The stem, which is the hair left free between the base and the clip.): The amount of hair left free between the base of the curl and the clip is called the stem, and it controls how much mobility the finished curl will have. A curl with no stem sits closest to the head and moves the least, while a full-stem curl has more give and produces a softer, more mobile finish. Pin curls do not rely on heat, so tool temperature plays no role in their result.
  53. The correct answer is D (Disinfecting the clipper blades to the same sanitation standard as any other tool used.): Clipper blades should be brushed free of hair clippings after every use, lubricated regularly, and disinfected according to the same sanitation standard as any other tool that contacts a client's hair and scalp. Brushing away visible hair alone only accomplishes the sanitation step, not disinfection, so something is genuinely missing here. Disinfection applies to clippers regardless of whether the service involved a chemical product. There is no exemption for clippers based on how they are built, and skipping disinfection leaves the same infection control gap as skipping it on a comb or shears.
  54. The correct answer is C (Androgenetic alopecia, which thins the crown and often preserves the front hairline.): Androgenetic alopecia is a genetically influenced, hormone-related progressive thinning. In women it tends to present as diffuse thinning over the crown while the front hairline is preserved. It is not caused by styling choices and it progresses slowly. None of the other patterns match this crown-centred, hairline-sparing description.
  55. The correct answer is A (It shows implements are cleaned and disinfected consistently between clients as required.): A disinfection log demonstrates that implements are being cleaned and disinfected consistently between clients, which is a core part of what a public health inspection is checking for. It has nothing to do with retail sales figures, appointment counts, or apprenticeship enrollment. Sanitation records like this help a salon show compliance and avoid a correction order or fine. Inspectors focus on infection control practices rather than unrelated business metrics.
  56. The correct answer is D (A different attachment method that is genuinely better suited to short, fine hair.): A sew-in installation relies on a secure braided foundation, which unusually short or fine hair may not be able to hold safely in place at all. Recommending a different method that does not depend on cornrows is a genuinely more appropriate choice given her hair's current condition. Compensating with tighter braiding or added thread tension only increases stress on hair that is already too short or fine to anchor a foundation properly.
  57. The correct answer is A (Recommend a more conservative approach or multiple sessions rather than maximum volume.): Noticing signs of previous overprocessing should lead the stylist to recommend a more conservative developer strength or a multi-session plan rather than reaching for the strongest available volume to satisfy a dramatic request in one visit. Prioritizing hair integrity over speed protects the client from further damage. This kind of judgment call is part of responsible, ethical formulation.
  58. The correct answer is D (Small notches are opened into the ends rather than one continuous straight line.): Holding the shears vertically and cutting into the ends opens small notches into the hair rather than producing one clean, continuous line the way a horizontal cut would. This is what softens a blunt edge and adds texture without significant length loss. The vertical blade position is the defining mechanical difference in point cutting.
  59. The correct answer is B (A predisposition test for the new product line, since sensitivity can be product specific.): A predisposition test should be repeated whenever a client is introduced to a new product or formula, since sensitivity can be specific to a particular chemical rather than to chemical services in general, so prior tolerance of a different brand does not carry over automatically. Skipping the test because of past tolerance ignores this product-specific risk. A strand test alone does not address the allergic reaction concern a predisposition test is designed to catch, and this requirement applies to any first use of a new line, not only to clients entirely new to chemical services.
  60. The correct answer is C (Break it up gently by hand once the hair is fully dry.): A gel-set style often dries with a stiff outer layer called a cast, meant to be broken up gently by hand once fully dry rather than styled while still wet. Combing through a damp cast would disturb the curl shape, and reapplying a mousse adds product rather than softening the existing set. Breaking the cast with the hands reveals the soft, defined curl underneath.
  61. The correct answer is C (Perform a strand test and confirm details with the client before deciding how to proceed.): When a record contains genuinely conflicting chemical history and the client cannot fully clarify it, performing a strand test alongside further discussion with the client is the responsible way to verify what the hair can actually tolerate before proceeding. Moving ahead with a standard formula despite the conflict ignores a real risk that combining an undocumented relaxer with a lightening service could cause serious damage. Picking one note over the other without verification is a guess rather than a resolution. Cancelling permanently is an overcorrection when a strand test and further questions can reasonably resolve the uncertainty.
  62. The correct answer is A (About 4.5 to 5.5, which is slightly acidic and keeps the cuticle closed.): Healthy hair and scalp sit at roughly 4.5 to 5.5 on the pH scale, slightly acidic. This acidity keeps the cuticle lying flat and tight, supporting shine and limiting moisture loss. Products close to that range are gentler on the cuticle. More alkaline products tend to lift the cuticle and leave hair more porous.
  63. The correct answer is C (Respect the client's choice while explaining why that information helps ensure safety.): Respecting a client's choice not to disclose certain information while explaining why it helps the stylist perform a safer service is a reasonable, professional approach. Refusing service entirely over one declined question is an overreaction unless the missing information is essential to a specific service being requested. Assuming an answer based on appearance risks inaccurate documentation and a false record. There is no reason to report a declined intake question to a public health inspector.
  64. The correct answer is B (Micro-link extensions secured mechanically with crimped silicone-lined beads only.): Micro-link extensions attach through mechanical compression of a silicone-lined bead rather than through any heat or adhesive chemicals, making the method genuinely suitable for a client with known sensitivities to both categories. Fusion relies on heat and tape-in relies on adhesive, so neither option fits her stated requirement here. This mechanical attachment is the specific advantage that sets micro-link apart from the other three methods available.
  65. The correct answer is D (Visual monitoring of the hair as it processes, since porosity and history change the speed.): Porosity, natural level, and previous chemical history all change how fast a given head lifts. Relying only on the expected time ignores those differences and risks overprocessing. Visual monitoring lets the stylist stop when the hair actually reaches the target level.
  66. The correct answer is B (A dry, styled check confirming the cut looks even the way it will be worn.): A cut can look even while wet or under tension and still reveal unevenness once it dries and is styled the way the client actually wears it, so a dry, styled check is the final step before the service is complete. Assuming a wet check guarantees a dry result skips an important verification. This final look confirms the client is leaving with the shape they actually asked for.
  67. The correct answer is D (Disulfide bonds between sulfur atoms on neighbouring protein chains in the cortex.): The reducing agent used in a permanent wave, typically ammonium thioglycolate, breaks disulfide bonds by adding hydrogen to the sulfur groups within the cortex. This softens the hair's internal structure so it can be reshaped around a rod. Hydrogen bonds and salt bonds are weaker, temporary bonds affected mainly by water and styling, not by perm chemistry. Peptide bonds hold the protein chain itself together and are not the target of a perm's reducing solution.
  68. The correct answer is A (The irregular shaft shape makes it harder for natural sebum to travel down the length.): Because the hair shaft on curly and coily textures is irregular rather than round, natural sebum produced at the scalp has a harder path to travel down the length of the hair. This leaves curly and coily hair naturally drier than straighter textures and makes moisture focused products a core part of styling. The difference comes from the shape of the shaft, not from a lower amount of sebum produced.
  69. The correct answer is C (Decline, since performing services outside the trade's recognized scope is a professional conduct concern.): Practising within the scope of the trade is part of professional conduct expectations, and performing a service outside that recognized scope is a concern regardless of the relationship between the stylist and the client. A personal friendship does not change what falls within the hairstyling trade's actual scope of practice. An informal waiver or a request for confidentiality does not address the underlying scope of practice issue. Declining the request and directing the friend to an appropriately qualified professional is the correct response.
  70. The correct answer is B (High porosity, so it processes fast and may need reduced product strength.): High porosity hair has a lifted or worn cuticle that lets moisture and colour enter quickly and unevenly. It processes faster than expected and can grab colour too dark. For the next service the stylist would reduce processing strength or shorten the timing. A tight cuticle would indicate low porosity instead.
  71. The correct answer is A (Starting the consultation for the next scheduled client on the book.): Using processing time to begin the next client's consultation keeps the schedule moving without compromising either client's service. Leaving the floor entirely wastes productive time that could reduce lateness later in the day. Extending processing time beyond what the formula requires risks an uneven or damaged result. Rebooking future appointments before checking the current colour also misplaces priority ahead of monitoring the service already underway.
  72. The correct answer is A (Each strand of hair is individually hand-tied to a sheer mesh base.): A monofilament cap is built by hand-tying each individual hair to a sheer mesh base material rather than sewing machine-made wefts. This construction lets the hair be parted in any direction while appearing to grow straight from the scalp. It is considerably more time-consuming and costly to produce than a wefted cap.
  73. The correct answer is D (The formulas, developer volumes, and processing times used at each stage.): Documentation matters as much as technique in corrective work. Recording the formulas, developer volumes, and processing times used at each stage gives any colourist who works on that client later a reliable record to build on. This avoids having to guess at the client's history the next time a service goes wrong.
  74. The correct answer is A (A shorter shear, roughly five to five and a half inches.): Shorter shears, typically in the five to five and a half inch range, give more control for precision work close to the head, including scissor-over-comb technique. Longer shears are generally better suited to reach and to slicing larger sections. Thinning and texturizing shears are not the correct tool for a precision straight-edge task.
  75. The correct answer is C (Processing may need more time nearer the scalp before the whole section is ready.): A pattern that looks developed on top but still mostly straight near the scalp suggests that area has not processed as far along yet, so more time is likely needed there before treating the whole section as ready. Declaring the section fully processed when part of it clearly is not would risk locking in an incomplete curl at the scalp. This kind of difference is worth monitoring rather than ignoring, and changing rod diameter addresses curl size, not an uneven processing rate within one section.
  76. The correct answer is D (Lift the style up off the shoulders so the neckline stays visible and clean.): A high, structured neckline generally pairs well with an updo that keeps the neck and shoulders visible, so lifting the style off the shoulders is the adjustment that best balances the gown. Adding volume at the shoulders would hide the neckline, and loose hair around the face would cover it rather than work with it. The change also makes the style easier to hold than a heavy, low mass.
  77. The correct answer is A (Explain the risk, describe a safer alternative path, and get informed agreement before proceeding.): When a client's hair condition makes their desired result unsafe or unachievable in one visit, the professional response is to explain why in plain language, describe a safer path to the result, and get the client's informed agreement before proceeding. Simply deferring to the client's own sense of their hair's condition ignores the physical assessment findings that are part of every consultation. Refusing service entirely skips the conversation that could lead to a safe, agreed alternative, and silently changing the plan without telling the client removes their ability to give informed consent. Clear communication protects both the hair and the relationship with the client.
  78. The correct answer is B (This looks like alopecia areata, an autoimmune loss that is not caused by her colour service.): Alopecia areata is an autoimmune condition producing sudden, well-defined, often round patches of hair loss with an unpredictable course. It is not caused by a colour service and is not contagious. A stylist should recognize the pattern and refer the client rather than reassure her that the colour was responsible. Traction alopecia, seborrheic dermatitis, and normal shedding present differently.
  79. The correct answer is B (A correction order or follow-up inspection until the issue is properly fixed.): A sanitation violation like improper sharps disposal typically results in a correction order or a follow-up inspection to confirm the issue has been fixed. It does not revoke individual stylist certifications, which is governed by the separate apprenticeship authority rather than a health inspector. Sharps disposal is a genuine safety and sanitation requirement, not a matter of personal preference. Chemical services would not be banned outright over this specific infraction.
  80. The correct answer is C (The client's actual natural growth pattern rather than a fixed straight template.): Reading the client's own natural growth pattern before any cutting begins is essential, since forcing a shape against the grain of growth tends to produce uneven results as the beard fills back in. A rigid straight line, or a template copied without regard for actual growth, ignores how the finished result will look once the hair regrows. Working with the real pattern gives the most natural, lasting outcome for this client.
  81. The correct answer is B (Remove artificial colour, then fill the porosity, then handle the banding, then tone.): A corrective service is sequenced to fix the foundation before the finish, so the stylist removes unwanted artificial colour, corrects porosity with a filler, softens the regrowth line, and only then tones and deposits the final shade. Skipping ahead, such as toning before the underlying problems are dealt with, compounds the original issue. Testing at each stage keeps the service controlled.
  82. The correct answer is D (A moderate to high elevation, higher than the low elevation of the perimeter.): Interior sections in a square layered shape are typically elevated to a moderate or high angle to remove weight from underneath the solid, low-elevation perimeter without disturbing its crisp outline. Using the same low elevation as the perimeter would not create the intended interior movement. This contrast in elevation between perimeter and interior defines the technique.
  83. The correct answer is D (The level of ventilation and precautions needed based on what the formula actually contains.): Checking a smoothing formula's formaldehyde content is meant to guide the stylist's preparation for ventilation and other precautions appropriate to what that specific product actually contains, since not every smoothing formula carries the same risk. This check does not determine service pricing. Keratin and chemical smoothing treatments are not the same as a permanent relaxer, and formaldehyde content does not determine that distinction. It also has no established connection to how soon a client might need a follow-up haircut.
  84. The correct answer is C (A firm backcombed foundation with evenly placed pinning and strong-hold finish.): Durability techniques matter more in special occasion work than in routine styling. A firm backcombed foundation, secure and evenly placed pinning, and a strong-hold finishing product extend how long a special occasion style holds its shape. Humidity resistant products and extra pins are reasonable contingencies for an outdoor event whose conditions cannot be controlled.
  85. The correct answer is C (Sustained overhead arm positioning increases strain on the shoulders over the course of a service.): Sustained overhead arm positioning increases strain on the shoulders, and this kind of posture is exactly what chair height adjustment and stance changes are meant to reduce during a service. Overhead positioning is not the safest posture available, since lowering the chair or adjusting the client's position reduces the strain considerably. Shoulder strain is relevant to blow drying just as much as to any other repetitive salon task, not only chemical services. Focusing solely on the finished result while ignoring posture overlooks a real and preventable strain risk.
  86. The correct answer is A (Frequent clarifying shampoo is drying her scalp and worsening the dryness and irritation.): Clarifying shampoo uses stronger surfactants to remove heavy buildup and is typically used periodically rather than daily, since frequent use can be drying. On an already dry, sensitized scalp, overuse can worsen dryness and irritation and increase flakes. A moisturizing shampoo with gentler surfactants is the better regular choice here. The change is a product-selection problem rather than a new condition.
  87. The correct answer is B (Whether their self-employed revenue crosses the threshold that requires registration.): A self-employed booth renter must register for GST or HST once their revenue crosses the threshold that requires it, since they are running their own small business rather than working through the salon's registration. The salon owner's own tax registration for the building does not cover an independent contractor's separate business income. Personal preference does not determine a legal tax obligation, and a client requesting a receipt is unrelated to registration requirements.
  88. The correct answer is A (The client's own individual head shape combined with her natural hair density.): The foundation braid pattern should genuinely be mapped to the individual client's own head shape and natural hair density so the finished installation lies flat and distributes weight evenly across the scalp. Applying one generic pattern equally to every client ignores real differences in head shape and density that meaningfully affect the finished result. Planning carefully around this client's own head, rather than a default habit, produces a noticeably better outcome overall.
  89. The correct answer is D (A very pale yellow stage is most likely at the lightest levels near level 10.): At the very lightest levels, the underlying pigment progression has moved through red, orange, and orange-yellow to arrive at pale yellow, which is the stage typically encountered near level 10. This predictable sequence lets the stylist select a toner suited to that final pale yellow stage. Skipping ahead without anticipating this stage risks an uneven or unexpected finished tone.
  90. The correct answer is C (The finished cut may end up far shorter than intended once curl reforms.): Cutting curly hair while it is pulled straight or heavily stretched risks leaving the finished length far shorter than intended once the curl pattern springs back to its resting shape. This is a direct consequence of shrinkage on tightly coiled hair. Cutting closer to the hair's natural, unstretched state helps avoid this outcome.
  91. The correct answer is A (Together, these findings point strongly toward overprocessing as the most likely explanation.): When breakage at multiple points and a loss of elasticity appear together right after a chemical service, this combination points strongly toward overprocessing as the most likely explanation, since both are recognized signs of the same underlying structural damage. Treating them as unrelated ignores how they commonly occur together for the same reason. Breakage is itself a clear, serious sign of overprocessing on its own, not one that only matters when paired with elasticity loss. This combination is not a normal, expected result of a properly processed chemical service.
  92. The correct answer is D (Reduce the tension to just enough to keep the section smooth while it dries.): Consistent tension turns a wet hair section into a smooth, controlled one, but excessive tension on fragile or chemically treated hair increases the risk of breakage where the brush grips the section. This client's hair is already vulnerable from colour treatment, so pulling harder would worsen the fraying. Reducing the tension to just enough to keep the hair smooth delivers a sleek result with less stress on the shaft.
  93. The correct answer is B (Eye protection, since strong relaxers carry a real risk of splash during mixing and application.): Eye protection matters most during mixing and application of products that can splash, and strong relaxers are specifically named among the products that carry this risk. Doubling up on gloves does not address the separate hazard of a splash reaching the eyes, and ear protection is not relevant to a chemical splash risk. Assuming gloves alone cover every hazard overlooks the specific splash risk this product presents. Selecting protection based on the actual hazard, rather than a single default choice, is the correct approach.
  94. The correct answer is A (Use it periodically rather than daily because frequent use can be drying.): Clarifying shampoo uses stronger surfactants to remove heavy buildup and mineral deposits. Frequent use can be drying because it strips more than a gentle shampoo would. It is best used periodically, such as when a client needs a fresh start. For a dry or sensitized scalp, a stylist would avoid clarifying on every visit.
  95. The correct answer is A (So the client can clearly see exactly what each part of the visit cost.): Itemizing add-on services separately lets the client see clearly what each part of their visit cost, which supports transparent and honest billing. It has nothing to do with avoiding tax reporting, and legitimate salon revenue is reported regardless of how a receipt is itemized. A receptionist removing charges without telling the client would be dishonest, not a normal billing practice. Add-on services are performed by whichever certified stylist is providing the appointment, not restricted to uncertified staff.
  96. The correct answer is A (The creative brief provided directly by the photographer and the art director.): Editorial styling is guided by the creative brief or mood board supplied directly by the photographer or art director, since the finished work is produced specifically for that shoot rather than for the model's own everyday taste. Techniques may be deliberately exaggerated because the final result is judged through a camera lens under studio lighting. The stylist collaborates closely with the creative team rather than defaulting to a personal preference.
  97. The correct answer is D (Introducing a small amount of warmth can help offset the flat, dull cast.): A small, measured addition of warmth can counterbalance an overly violet, flat result and restore some brightness to the finished blonde. Simply adding more violet toner would deepen the dullness rather than correct it. Adjusting tone thoughtfully, rather than repeating the same approach, is what resolves this kind of complaint.
  98. The correct answer is C (Assess and cut the layers with the curl pattern's natural fall and shrinkage in mind.): Accounting for the curl pattern's natural fall and shrinkage while cutting layers helps ensure the finished shape looks even once the hair dries and the curl reforms, addressing the client's specific concern directly. Cutting under heavy tension risks the opposite problem, an uneven result once the stretch releases. Layering is still possible on curly hair when the technique accounts for how the curl actually behaves.
  99. The correct answer is C (Perform a new strand test, since hair condition and product formulas can change over time.): A new strand test should be performed for a new service being planned today, since hair condition can change meaningfully over six months, and the product or method being used now may differ from what was tested previously. Reusing an old result assumes hair condition stays constant, which is not a safe assumption. One prior result does not cover all future services indefinitely, and hair length is not the relevant factor in deciding whether an old strand test result still applies.
  100. The correct answer is B (A paddle brush, which is wide and flat for maximum smoothness on long hair.): A paddle brush is flat and wide and is chosen when the goal is maximum smoothness on long hair with minimal directional bending. It gives the straight, sleek blowout the client wants without adding a wave or curl. A round brush would introduce a bend, and a vent brush would preserve movement instead of a smooth finish.
  101. The correct answer is B (Processing time is a fixed wait, so it can reasonably be used for a brief task.): Processing time for colour or chemical services adds a fixed waiting period that cannot be rushed without compromising the result, and this predictable window is often exactly when a stylist can reasonably attend to a brief additional task, such as a quick consultation for another client. Assuming the time must sit entirely unused overstates the constraint, while assuming the wait itself can be shortened by scheduling something else misunderstands what the fixed processing time actually represents. Ignoring processing time when planning the schedule risks creating unnecessary delays elsewhere. Thoughtful scheduling around this fixed window supports a smoother overall day.
  102. The correct answer is C (This pattern fits dandruff, which is manageable and often improves with regular anti-dandruff shampoo use.): Dandruff, or pityriasis capitis, involves faster skin cell turnover that produces white or grey flakes with mild itching, and it is generally a manageable cosmetic condition. It often improves with regular anti-dandruff shampoo, so salon shampoo plus home maintenance is a reasonable path. Seborrheic dermatitis produces redness, greasy yellowish scale, and more persistent itching and often needs a medical approach. Psoriasis presents as thick, bordered, silvery plaques and warrants referral.
  103. The correct answer is B (The provincial or territorial apprenticeship authority that oversees their registration.): The apprenticeship authority in the apprentice's province or territory tracks logged hours and determines exam eligibility, making it the correct source for this question. A product representative or colour manufacturer has no role in apprenticeship tracking. A certified stylist in an unrelated specialty may not have accurate or current information either. Going directly to the regulating authority avoids relying on secondhand or outdated information about hour requirements.
  104. The correct answer is D (Limit the shave to a single pass performed with the grain only.): For a client prone to irritation, limiting the shave to a single pass in the direction of hair growth reduces friction compared with adding a second pass across or against the grain. Skipping pre-shave preparation such as the oil step would increase irritation risk rather than reduce it for this client. Adjusting technique to match the client's known skin sensitivity reflects sound professional judgment here.
  105. The correct answer is B (Past use of henna or metallic dye, which can react with peroxide.): No colour or corrective service should begin without a thorough consultation about the client's chemical history. Henna and metallic dye are the key risks, because they can react unpredictably with permanent colour or lightener and generate heat. Asking about them is a safety step that protects the client from breakage and scalp injury.
  106. The correct answer is C (The crown often has a whorling growth pattern that complicates blending.): The crown is the area surrounding the apex where the hair growth pattern often whorls in a circular direction, which commonly requires extra attention because hair grows outward from this point in a way that can make an evenly blended line harder to achieve. It does not require an entirely different shear, and it still responds to elevation like other areas, just with added difficulty. The crown is not inherently the shortest section on every head.
  107. The correct answer is B (Every product is tested and formulated for a specific concentration and processing window.): Manufacturer timing instructions reflect the specific concentration and processing window a product was actually tested and formulated to work within, which is why following them exactly, rather than treating them as a rough suggestion, matters for both safety and predictable results. Describing timing as mainly a legal formality misstates its real chemical basis. Exact timing genuinely matters across perms, relaxers, and smoothing treatments alike, not only for one category, and its importance does not depend on whether a client has had prior chemical services.
  108. The correct answer is C (Use a diffuser, cupping sections and holding the tool still.): A diffuser spreads the airflow across a wide, low-velocity area instead of concentrating it through a narrow nozzle. Cupping the sections and holding the tool still lets curls dry in their natural clumped formation, which reduces frizz. A direct high-velocity nozzle would separate the curl clumps and roughen the cuticle.
  109. The correct answer is A (Report the observation through the normal process rather than deciding alone whether it is dangerous.): The right to know and the right to participate exist so that a worker does not have to single-handedly judge whether a hazard is serious before it can be looked into. Reporting the observation through the normal process, rather than dismissing it or escalating to an unannounced walkout, allows a supervisor or committee to assess ventilation and product handling properly. Ignoring a genuine odour concern risks missing a real ventilation problem, while confronting a coworker publicly does not address the underlying workplace issue. Using the established reporting channel is the balanced and correct response to an uncertain but plausible hazard.
  110. The correct answer is A (Postpone the service and refer the client for medical assessment.): Signs of a possible contagious condition, such as unusual scaling with visible movement or live insects, call for referral rather than continued service. Working in the salon could spread the condition or expose the stylist. Postponing and referring is a professional decision that protects the client and limits liability. The stylist should be tactful and frame the referral around client wellbeing.
  111. The correct answer is A (Complex corrective work is priced after consultation since time and product vary.): Complex or unpredictable services such as colour correction are typically quoted only after a consultation, because the actual time and product needed cannot be known until the stylist assesses the hair. A menu price tied only to weekday mornings or left to reception with no professional input misrepresents how service pricing actually works. The listed menu price is also not meaningless, it simply does not fit a service whose scope depends on the hair's current condition.
  112. The correct answer is D (Adjust the lineup to work naturally with the scar rather than straight through it.): A scar that disrupts normal hair growth genuinely needs to be considered carefully when planning the lineup, so the stylist adjusts the shape to work with the irregular growth rather than cutting a straight line as though it were not there. Avoiding the entire side of the face is an unnecessary overcorrection that this specific situation does not actually require. Thoughtful adjustment around the scar produces a cleaner, more natural-looking final result overall.
  113. The correct answer is D (Ratio directly affects how strongly a formula deposits and processes overall.): An unintended extra amount of developer changed the working ratio and produced a sheerer, lighter deposit than the manufacturer's chart predicted, showing that ratio and deposit strength are directly connected. This reinforces why following the specified ratio matters even for a toning step. Mixing ratio, developer volume, and processing time function together as one interconnected system.
  114. The correct answer is C (Keep the elevation low so the stack builds tight and heavy.): A low graduation angle keeps the stacked layers overlapping tightly, producing the strong, blunt-looking wedge the client wants at the nape. Raising elevation would soften the stack and blend it more gradually rather than build a tight effect. Consistent low elevation throughout the nape section achieves the requested heavy stack.
  115. The correct answer is A (The relaxer's shampoo mainly normalizes pH, while a perm neutralizer reforms disulfide bonds.): A perm neutralizer is an oxidizing solution whose main role is reforming disulfide bonds, while a relaxer's neutralizing shampoo mainly works to normalize pH and rinse out residual alkaline chemical, since much of the bond reformation in a relaxer service already happens as the product is rinsed and the hair repositioned straight. Describing both as working identically, or reversing which product does which job, misstates how these two differently named but distinct products actually function. Both do have a real chemical function beyond simple rinsing.
  116. The correct answer is C (Wet hair is more elastic and more easily damaged by repeated combing.): Backcombing should be done on completely dry hair, since wet hair is more elastic and more easily damaged by repeated combing. The technique tangles the hair at its base to create a cushion of volume that supports the smoother top layer. Working on damp hair increases the risk of breakage and produces an unstable foundation for the updo.
  117. The correct answer is B (Wiping alone skips disinfection, so the shears are not yet ready for another client.): Wiping an implement clean of visible hair is only the sanitation step, and it does not destroy the microorganisms that disinfection is designed to remove. Shears must go through wet immersion or another approved disinfecting process for the manufacturer's stated contact time before returning to general use. Believing that a wipe is sufficient, or that disinfection can wait until the end of the day, leaves every client in between exposed to an incompletely processed tool. The senior stylist is right to correct this before the shears are used again.
  118. The correct answer is D (This resembles seborrheic dermatitis, so she should get a medicated treatment beyond a salon shampoo.): Seborrheic dermatitis is an inflammatory condition producing redness, greasy yellowish scale, and persistent itching, often extending to the eyebrows and sides of the nose. It often benefits from a medicated approach beyond what salon shampoo alone provides. Dandruff produces dry white flakes with milder itching and is more manageable. Since the pattern extends beyond the scalp and is inflammatory, a referral or medicated treatment is appropriate.
  119. The correct answer is C (Explain honestly what the salon's own pricing reflects without disparaging the competitor.): Explaining honestly what the salon's own pricing reflects, such as experience, product quality, or service time, without making unfounded claims about a competitor is the professional approach. Suggesting without evidence that a competitor must be operating illegally is an unfair and likely false accusation. A stylist generally does not have authority to unilaterally change salon pricing without involving ownership. Declining to discuss the topic at all leaves a reasonable client question unanswered.
  120. The correct answer is A (The overall cap fit may have changed and genuinely needs reassessment now.): A cap that once fit well can genuinely loosen over time due to general wear, gradual adjustment of internal straps, or real changes in the client's own remaining hair volume underneath it. This looseness is not an inevitable outcome for every monofilament cap regardless of how it is treated. Gentle brushing on its own would not cause the actual cap structure to loosen over time.
  121. The correct answer is B (One part colour to one part developer is the most common default ratio.): A one to one ratio of colour to developer is the most widely used default across professional colour lines and is the basis for most published processing times. Some product categories such as high lift blondes call for more developer. A stylist should always confirm the exact ratio on the manufacturer's own chart.
  122. The correct answer is B (Texturizing worked through the mid-shaft area to remove interior bulk.): Texturizing worked mid-shaft removes bulk from the interior of the section, allowing the hair to move more freely while preserving the overall length. Graduation or a blunt re-cut would change the shape and likely the length rather than simply softening movement. Clipper-over-comb is a different tool suited to close, graduated perimeter work, not mid-shaft texturizing.
  123. The correct answer is D (A neutralizing shampoo formulated for relaxer services should be applied per its directions.): After thoroughly rinsing a relaxer with water, a neutralizing shampoo specifically formulated for relaxer services should be applied according to its directions, since this step removes remaining alkaline residue and helps stabilize the hair's pH. A standard clarifying shampoo unrelated to relaxer chemistry does not serve this specific stabilizing function. Skipping straight to styling would leave residual alkaline chemical unaddressed. A perm neutralizer is a different product working through a different mechanism and is not the correct product for a relaxer service.
  124. The correct answer is B (A vent brush, which allows air through to preserve texture and movement.): A vent brush has widely spaced bristles and open slots in its base, which allows air to pass directly through the section for faster rough-drying while preserving natural texture and movement. A paddle brush gives maximum smoothness, and round brushes produce a bend or curl. The client wants preserved wave, so a vent brush fits the request.
  125. The correct answer is C (An apron or protective cape worn over the stylist's own clothing during mixing.): An apron or protective cape reduces skin and clothing contact with product during mixing and application, which directly addresses the concern about splashing onto the stylist's own clothes. A face shield rated for machinery, steel-toed footwear, and an industrial respirator are designed for entirely different work environments and are not part of standard salon PPE for this task. Selecting protection that matches the actual task, rather than borrowing equipment from an unrelated field, is the correct approach. A simple apron addresses this specific, common concern.

Practice Test 2 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (Test it against the stylist's own wrist, then confirm verbally with the client.): Water temperature should be tested against the stylist's own wrist before it touches the client. The stylist should then check verbally once contact begins. Water that feels fine to the stylist may feel too warm to the client. This confirms comfort and prevents a painful rinse.
  2. The correct answer is B (Explain the record contents and the salon's general storage and access practices.): A client is entitled to a reasonable explanation of what information is kept about them and how it is generally stored and accessed, which supports transparency and trust. Refusing to answer misunderstands confidentiality, which protects a client's information from unauthorized third parties, not from the client themselves. Salons do keep records, whether digital or paper, contrary to a blanket denial. Transferring the call elsewhere does not address a reasonable question the salon itself should be able to answer.
  3. The correct answer is B (Ongoing sun and heat exposure has gradually dried out the hair's cuticle.): Direct sun and prolonged heat exposure gradually dries out and weakens the cuticle of human hair, whether that hair is growing naturally or styled into a finished wig. This is exactly why wigs should be kept away from direct sun and other heat sources whenever they are not being worn. Melting is specifically a synthetic fibre concern rather than a human hair issue, and dryness from sun exposure is not automatically a defect from manufacturing.
  4. The correct answer is D (Rinse each foil section with the foils in place, keeping lightener off the untreated hair.): Foils isolate the treated strands so the product stays off the untouched hair beside them. Rinsing with the foils still in place keeps that separation while the lightener is washed away. Removing all the foils and shampooing the whole head together lets the product spread onto the untreated sections.
  5. The correct answer is A (Short regrowth will stand up, creating a spiky or fuzzy hairline area.): Texturizing too close to the scalp leaves very short pieces that stand up as they regrow, producing an unwanted spiky or fuzzy look at the hairline. This is why texturizing is typically worked well away from the root. Correcting it requires waiting for the regrowth to grow out enough to blend.
  6. The correct answer is D (How the hair responds to the exact product and method planned for the full service.): A strand test uses the actual product and processing method planned for the service on a small strand of hair, evaluating how the hair itself responds, such as its resulting straightness or condition, before the full head is committed. Allergic reaction is the specific concern a predisposition test addresses instead, using a skin site rather than a hair strand. A strand test has no connection to customer service satisfaction or to pricing the appointment, both of which are unrelated business matters.
  7. The correct answer is C (Apply heat protectant before every thermal tool use and avoid repeated passes.): Prevention of thermal damage starts with applying heat protectant before every thermal tool use, choosing the lowest temperature that will still achieve the result, and avoiding repeated passes over the same section. Working at the lowest effective temperature and moving smoothly in clean sections limits the cumulative stress. A worn iron creates hot spots, so it does not spread heat more evenly.
  8. The correct answer is C (Stop the service, apply pressure with a clean single use item, and offer a bandage.): The correct response to a nick is to stop the service immediately, apply pressure with a clean, single use item until bleeding stops, and offer the client a bandage. Continuing the haircut before addressing the bleeding, reusing an unprocessed tool, or using a reusable cape against an open wound all fail to follow the proper exposure protocol. Any tool involved is then set aside for cleaning, sanitizing, and, where blood contact occurred, sterilizing before it touches another client. Prompt, correct first response protects both the client and the stylist.
  9. The correct answer is A (Its shingle-like cells lie flat and tight, reflecting light evenly and controlling moisture entry.): The cuticle is a single-direction layer of overlapping, translucent scale cells that lie flat like shingles. When it is smooth and tight it reflects light evenly, so hair looks shiny and feels smooth, and it controls how easily moisture and chemicals enter. Dull, tangled hair often indicates a lifted or roughened cuticle rather than a need for clarifying. Clarifying shampoo removes buildup, but shine depends mostly on a smooth cuticle.
  10. The correct answer is A (The salon owner sets pricing and policy since the stylist is an employee.): In a commission arrangement, the stylist is an employee, and the salon owner sets service pricing and business policy in exchange for supplying the space, product, and support staff. There is no national law fixing identical prices across salons, and pricing varies widely by market and business. Commission stylists are not barred from discussing price with clients, that is a normal part of the consultation. Retail manufacturers also have no role in setting a salon's service pricing.
  11. The correct answer is A (A lightweight clip-in topper or crown hair addition piece for that area.): A lightweight topper or crown addition is designed specifically for diffuse thinning and adds volume through clips or a light adhesive strip rather than attaching under load to individual natural strands. Full extension methods place significant sustained weight on the hair that fragile, thinning hair often cannot safely support long term. This makes a lightweight addition the more appropriate recommendation in this specific situation.
  12. The correct answer is D (No, semi-permanent products deposit pigment only and cannot lift the level.): Semi-permanent colour deposits pigment onto the hair without a developer reaction and is not capable of lifting the natural level, so it cannot noticeably lighten naturally dark hair. Achieving real lift requires a permanent, oxidative formula instead. Explaining this clearly during consultation prevents the client from expecting a result the product cannot deliver.
  13. The correct answer is C (In the interior sections, elevated above the solid perimeter length.): Interior sections are elevated, usually to a moderate or high angle, and layered to remove bulk from underneath the perimeter while the solid outer edge stays undisturbed. Cutting directly along the perimeter at low elevation would alter the crisp outline itself. Square layered shapes are specifically designed to combine a solid perimeter with a mobile interior.
  14. The correct answer is B (Gloves and product should stay properly managed and not left where anyone could contact them.): Even a brief interruption calls for managing gloves and open chemical product responsibly, since leaving them where a client, coworker, or passerby could make unintended contact creates an avoidable safety risk. Assuming a short absence removes any concern ignores how quickly accidental contact can happen. Leaving an open container uncapped increases both spill risk and unnecessary fume exposure in the meantime. The length of the interruption does not remove the basic responsibility to keep chemical products and protective equipment properly managed.
  15. The correct answer is D (Comb the section toward the scalp in short repeated strokes on fully dry hair.): Backcombing combs a small section of hair back toward the scalp in short, repeated strokes, which tangles the hair at its base and creates a cushion of volume that supports the smoother layer over it. The process should be done on fully dry hair, since wet hair is more elastic and more easily damaged by repeated combing. The tension should be firm but controlled, because overly aggressive backcombing at the scalp causes unnecessary breakage.
  16. The correct answer is C (Without a recorded formula, the exact result cannot be reliably repeated at the next visit.): Recording the exact products, strengths, and timing used on each visit is what allows a service to be reliably repeated or safely adjusted at a client's next appointment. Without that documentation, even the original stylist cannot be certain of reproducing the same result from memory alone months later. Booking the same stylist again does not eliminate the need for a written record, since memory naturally fades over time. An accurate formula record is exactly what repeat colour services depend on.
  17. The correct answer is B (Coarse hair needs more processing time, since its larger diameter and stronger cuticle resist the colour.): Coarse hair has the largest diameter and often a stronger cuticle layer, so it generally requires more processing time or stronger product strength for the same result. Fine hair has the smallest diameter, less strength per strand, and tends to process faster. Medium texture is the baseline most manufacturers use for standard timing. Treating coarse like fine would under-process the colour.
  18. The correct answer is A (Pricing often reflects a stylist's experience, speed, consistency, and reputation.): It is common for a salon menu to price services higher for a more experienced stylist, reflecting their speed, consistency, and reputation built over time. There is no provincial trade law mandating a specific price difference between experience levels. A higher listed price is a service charge, not an automatic tip, which is a separate matter entirely. Newer stylists are not barred from performing highlighting services within their scope of certification.
  19. The correct answer is C (Describe the treatment as cosmetic care and suggest seeing a dermatologist too.): Scalp treatments should be described honestly to clients as a cosmetic and comfort service rather than as a guaranteed medical cure for an ongoing condition. Persistent dandruff or scalp irritation that does not respond to normal cosmetic care may genuinely warrant a referral to a dermatologist for further evaluation. Promising a permanent cure for a medical-sounding condition oversteps a cosmetologist's actual scope of practice.
  20. The correct answer is D (Mixing ratios vary by manufacturer and product line, so charts must be confirmed.): Because mixing ratios differ across manufacturers and even across product categories within the same brand, checking the technical chart before mixing protects against using an outdated or incorrect ratio from memory. This habit supports consistent, predictable results across every product line a salon carries. Experience does not remove the need to confirm details that vary by product.
  21.
