The correct answer is A (Dry hair offers more resistance and increases the risk of catching and pulling.): Dry hair resists the blade more than damp hair does, which increases the chance of the razor catching and pulling rather than slicing cleanly through the strand. Cutting on damp hair allows the diagonal blade to move smoothly along the shaft. This is a standard technical reason razor work is almost always done on damp hair.
  22. The correct answer is A (Writing it immediately after the service, while the specific details are still fresh.): Writing the record immediately after finishing a service, while the specific product, timing, and any observations are still fresh, best protects the accuracy of the details that future planning will rely on. Waiting until the end of a busy day with several other clients seen in between risks details blurring together or being forgotten. Relying on memory alone instead of a written record removes the whole benefit records are meant to provide. Writing only a brief summary weeks later, once details have already faded, defeats the purpose of accurate, timely documentation.
  23. The correct answer is B (A mousse, which distributes lightly and gives light to medium hold with volume.): Mousse is a foam, a liquid formula that has been aerated so it distributes lightly and evenly through the hair. It generally provides light to medium hold with added volume and lift, particularly when applied at the root, without weighing the hair down the way a heavier cream or gel might. A firm gel gives stronger hold and a hard cast, which is more than this client wants for everyday body.
  24. The correct answer is D (Use a separate, already processed comb for the waiting client instead of the set aside one.): A tool that has contacted blood must go through sterilization before it can be used on another client, and equipment being temporarily busy does not lower that requirement. The correct response is to continue the next client's service with a separate implement that has already been properly processed, while the exposed comb waits its turn for sterilization. Rinsing under water does not meet the sterilization standard, and asking a client to supply their own tool is not a substitute for the salon's own protocol. Having enough backup implements on hand avoids exactly this kind of gap.
  25. A silvery, raised patch suggests psoriasis, which a stylist should not treat or diagnose, and itchy red bumps point to folliculitis. Together these findings make a chemical wave unsafe, so the service should be postponed and the client referred. Proceeding around the patches ignores the open and infected areas.

Apprenticeship authorities do not send ongoing technique updates, since their role is registration and certification rather than continuing training. Relying solely on original apprenticeship training risks falling behind as the industry changes. Client descriptions are not a reliable substitute for structured, hands-on training.

Referral to a physician, dermatologist, or trichologist is the appropriate response rather than attempting a cosmetic treatment over it. A cosmetology license does not extend to diagnosing or treating medical scalp conditions.
  28. Recommending a purple or blue toned shampoo and a maintenance toning schedule addresses this cause. Reformulating the whole service would not help.
  29. It appears far more often in men's design cutting than in most women's work. This is distinct from graduation or fading, which are built specifically to avoid any visible line.
  30. This is not a normal, expected finding right after any chemical service, and a simple leave-in conditioner does not address genuine structural bond loss of this kind. Underprocessed hair would typically show a weak or barely formed result rather than this pattern of poor elasticity and easy breakage.
  31. The correct answer is A (A Dutch braid with the outer strands crossed underneath the centre strand.): A Dutch braid uses the same principle of picking up new hair from the sides, but the outer strands are crossed underneath the centre strand instead of over it. This causes the braid to sit up and stand out from the head rather than lying flat. It is sometimes taught as an inverted or reverse French braid.
  32. The correct answer is C (A disconnected detector leaves that area without an early warning system for an actual fire.): A disconnected smoke detector leaves that specific area without an early warning system in the event of an actual fire, which is a meaningful gap rather than a negligible one. The correct response to recurring false alarms is addressing their actual cause, such as improved ventilation or detector placement, rather than simply disabling the detector. Assuming a single disconnected detector has no real effect overlooks that this may be exactly the area, near heat tools and chemical products, where early detection matters most. A functioning detector near a mixing station is part of a salon's basic fire safety expectations, not an optional feature.
  33. The correct answer is B (Daily shedding is normal since about 10 to 15 percent of hair rests in telogen.): Telogen is the resting phase during which the old hair is shed, and it is normal for 10 to 15 percent of scalp hair to be in telogen at any time. Ordinary daily shedding is not pathological hair loss. A stylist should recognize when heavier, sustained shedding is a sign that referral is appropriate. Bulbs detach in catagen, not anagen.

A retail seminar, owning personal tools, or having worked at multiple salons locally does not carry that interprovincial recognition. Labour mobility depends on the national standard the endorsement represents. The stylist may still need to complete minor jurisdiction-specific registration steps.
  35. The stylist should explain this real tradeoff clearly rather than simply raising the setting just to save time today. A setting too cool to melt the keratin properly would also fail to form a proper bond, so the correct temperature range matters either way here.

A bowl and brush lets the stylist lay the formula precisely along the clean partings and control saturation. An applicator bottle floods the area more quickly but gives less control at the boundary, which is where overlap happens.
  37. Simply covering the area with product does not fix the underlying technical issue. This targeted blending approach addresses the specific spot where the inconsistency occurred.
  38. New furniture does not address a chemical safety concern at all. Relying on stylists to personally recall what an unlabelled bottle contains does not protect a different stylist, client, or emergency responder who might need that information later. Adding more kits without fixing the labelling problem leaves the core safety gap unresolved.
  39. The correct answer is D (A leave-in conditioner, which adds moisture and slip for easier detangling.): A leave-in conditioner is applied after washing and not rinsed out. It supplies ongoing moisture and slip, which makes detangling easier and reduces the mechanical stress that causes breakage on curly and coily textures. A curl-defining cream is heavier and carries styling hold, so it comes afterward to coat curl clumps and reduce frizz.
  40. The correct answer is C (The employee should be shown extinguisher locations, the evacuation route, and the meeting point promptly.): Every stylist should know the salon's evacuation route and meeting point, along with where fire extinguishers are located and how to use one, and a new employee missing this information should be shown it promptly rather than left uninformed. Limiting this knowledge to only the owner or manager leaves every other staff member unprepared during an actual emergency. Waiting until a fire actually occurs to explain evacuation routes defeats the purpose of preparation entirely. Ensuring every staff member knows this information in advance is part of basic salon fire safety.

Gentle heat helps open the cuticle enough to allow the conditioning agents to reach the cortex. It is not used to seal the cuticle; a properly formulated closing step does that. Disulfide bonds are not rebuilt by heat.
  42. Ordering more product without addressing rotation would only add to the pile of neglected older stock. Moving stock out of sight behind the register does not solve the underlying rotation problem. Developer is a backbar item used in services, not something typically sold directly to retail clients.

Cutting well below the natural line, or ignoring the growth pattern entirely, can produce an unnatural or genuinely short-lived result. A brief, respectful conversation about this tradeoff is the appropriate professional step to take here.
  44. Performing the test just before the service would not allow enough time to observe a delayed response before the actual colour is applied. This is why the test must be scheduled in advance rather than done the same day.

Growth pattern, blade grind, and guideline type are less likely explanations for this specific angular versus rounded shape difference. Switching to a vertical cutting line would better follow the head's natural curve.

Chair height and appointment day have no chemical or physical bearing on curl formation. Shampoo brand affects cleansing, not the geometry of how the hair is held during processing.
  47. The correct answer is A (Heat protectant is needed, because thermal damage builds with repeated exposure over time.): Heat protectant is not optional on chemically treated, lightened, or otherwise compromised hair. Even a brief contact adds to the cumulative thermal damage a client's hair experiences over time, rather than only causing damage through one long exposure. The product deposits a coating that cushions the shaft against the sudden heat and slows moisture loss from inside the hair.
  48. Assuming fine hair can never be cut cleanly ignores that well maintained shears are specifically expected to handle it well. Increasing pressure to compensate mirrors the same problem a dull shear causes and risks crushing the hair shaft. Dismissing the issue simply because sharpening recently occurred overlooks a persistent performance problem worth investigating further.
  49. It can grab colour too dark and may need reduced processing strength or a gentler formula. Low porosity resists absorption, and normal porosity processes evenly. Rough, grabby ends on previously lifted hair point to the cuticle, not to the cortex measure of elasticity.
  50. It is not the same as a liability waiver, which does not remove a stylist's professional responsibility. It also has nothing to do with a retail purchase or booking a future appointment. Genuine consent requires an honest consultation before work begins.
  51. Substituting an improvised household tool, or pulling the beads free by hand, risks damaging the natural hair or injuring the client during removal. Rescheduling until the correct professional tool is available on hand is the safer choice for everyone involved.
  52. Using this ratio as the starting point is appropriate when the manufacturer has not indicated a different requirement. Confirming against the specific product line remains good practice whenever it is available.
  53. Sections becoming shorter than earlier ones would indicate an error rather than a correct outcome. Consistent geometry across the whole perimeter is the goal of this technique.
  54. Treating the whole head with one uniform time based on the roots risks overprocessing the ends before the roots have caught up. Leaving the ends untreated is not a real option if a perm result is wanted throughout the length, and using the shortest time everywhere would leave the more resistant roots underprocessed.
  55. The correct answer is C (Thermal damage, with split ends and mid-strand breakage as visible signs.): Split ends, where the outer layer of the shaft has separated at the tip, are one of the most visible signs of thermal damage, along with breakage partway along a strand rather than at the end. Recognising these signs early protects both the client's hair and the ongoing relationship. The pattern is not normal shedding, which occurs from the root, or a scalp condition, and it is distinct from a simple colour result.
  56. The correct answer is C (The new stylist must receive WHMIS training before working with or near those hazardous products.): Every employee who works with or near hazardous products must receive WHMIS training before using them, not at some later or more convenient point. Prior experience at a different salon does not remove this obligation, since training must cover the specific products actually used in the new workplace. A probation period or a flexible multi month window does not satisfy the requirement that training occur before exposure begins. Completing this training up front protects both the new stylist and every client whose service involves those products.
  57. The correct answer is D (The cuticle has lifted and roughened, so light scatters instead of reflecting.): A smooth, tight cuticle reflects light evenly and feels silky. When the cuticle is lifted or roughened, the surface scatters light and looks dull. Raised scales also catch against each other, which causes tangling. The cortex and medulla sit below or within the shaft and do not control surface shine.
  58. Logging a sale does not by itself change a commission rate, which is set separately by salon policy. It also does not eliminate the eventual need to reorder that product as stock is used. Sales logging and public health inspection are unrelated processes serving different purposes.
  59. The stylist should clearly explain this tradeoff rather than simply granting the request as asked. Balanced, appropriate tension protects the client's natural hair across repeated future installations over time.
  60. The correct answer is B (Semi-permanent colour sits in the cuticle and fades, while permanent colour lasts.): Semi-permanent colour sits only in the outer cuticle layer and gradually fades with each shampoo, while permanent colour penetrates and chemically alters pigment inside the hair shaft, producing a result that lasts until new growth appears. This structural difference explains why one fades evenly and the other shows a visible regrowth line. Understanding this helps the stylist set accurate expectations for maintenance.
  61. The correct answer is A (A razor drawn through the ends at a shallow diagonal angle.): A razor cuts diagonally along the hair shaft, tapering each strand to a fine point and producing the soft, feathered movement the client wants. Horizontal shear work at zero elevation would produce a blunt line instead. Clipper guards and a stationary blunt guide are not suited to this particular textured, feathered effect.
  62. The correct answer is C (It removes the ability to quickly check timing, concentration, or first aid information.): Transferring a chemical product into an unlabelled container removes the ability to quickly check timing, concentration, or first aid information if something goes wrong, which is why chemicals should never be moved out of clearly labelled containers. This is a safety concern, not a claim about the product losing effectiveness. Relying on personal memory does not address the risk to anyone else who might handle that container, and the concern applies broadly to how any chemical is stored and identified, not narrowly to the original manufacturer bottle alone.
  63. The correct answer is C (Lift the brush up and away from the scalp while drying the base.): The angle of the brush against the head controls the amount of root lift in the finished style. Lifting the brush up and away from the scalp while drying the base builds volume. Directing the brush down and close to the head flattens the root and produces a smoother silhouette.
  64. The correct answer is B (A structure through which workers exercise their right to participate in workplace safety.): A joint health and safety committee is one of the structures through which the right to participate is exercised, allowing workers to take part in identifying and resolving workplace safety concerns. Flagging a genuine ventilation issue and recommending a fix is exactly the kind of function this committee is meant to serve. It is not a social club, and its authority is not limited to payroll or scheduling matters. This committee's recommendation should be taken seriously as part of the salon's safety obligations.
  65. The correct answer is D (Recognize likely folliculitis and modify or postpone the service on the affected area.): Folliculitis is an inflammation or infection of the hair follicle seen as small red bumps or pustules around individual follicles, sometimes with tenderness. It can be bacterial, caused by tight styles, or from ingrown hairs, and active folliculitis means the service should be modified or postponed on that area. An allergic reaction and alopecia areata have different signs, and alopecia areata presents as smooth patches of loss rather than pustules. Treating the bumps is outside salon scope.

Continuing education is valuable in general, but the salon's own data shows technique complaints are rare, making it a lower priority for this quarter's limited funds. Assuming either problem resolves without action ignores the real operational cost of repeated stock shortages. Splitting funds evenly without regard to which problem is more pressing is a less effective use of limited resources than targeting the documented issue.
  67. The correct answer is D (Micro-link extensions, also called micro-bead or cold fusion extensions.): Micro-link extensions use a small silicone-lined bead compressed shut with dedicated pliers, clamping natural hair and extension hair together through friction alone. No heat tool or bonding chemical takes part in the attachment itself. This makes micro-link a common recommendation for clients who cannot tolerate heat or adhesive products near the scalp.
  68. Correcting it means softening the hard line rather than erasing it in one step. Root smudging with a demi-permanent shade close to the surrounding colour blends the transition zone.
  69. Ignoring face shape entirely would miss this part of the client's stated goal for the consultation.
  70. The correct answer is C (It either barely stretches before breaking or stretches excessively without any springback.): Overprocessed hair typically either barely stretches before breaking or stretches excessively without any springback, both patterns indicating the cortex has lost the structural bonds that normally give hair its elastic quality. Healthy hair, by contrast, stretches a small, predictable amount and returns close to its original length, which is a different pattern than either extreme seen in overprocessed hair. Elasticity checks are in fact a useful, objective way to distinguish overprocessed hair from healthy hair rather than being meaningless for this purpose.
  71. The correct answer is B (It deposits a coating that cushions the shaft and slows moisture loss.): Lightened hair is more porous and has less structural reserve, so it absorbs heat quickly and is more vulnerable to thermal damage. A heat protectant deposits silicones or other film forming ingredients onto the cuticle, cushioning the shaft against sudden heat and slowing moisture loss. Thermal damage accumulates with repeated exposure over time, so a brief use of the tool does not make the protectant optional on this hair.
  72. The correct answer is B (Refer the client for medical attention rather than continuing to treat it in the salon.): If bleeding does not stop with reasonable, sustained pressure, the client should be referred for medical attention rather than continuing to be treated further in the salon. Concealing the area with makeup ignores the underlying issue, and continuing pressure indefinitely without a plan is not an appropriate response to bleeding that will not stop. Using an implement grade antiseptic on skin is inappropriate and not part of basic salon first aid. Recognizing when an injury exceeds what salon first aid can manage is part of responding correctly.
  73. The correct answer is A (The internal bonds are weakened, so further chemical work is unwise until rebuilt.): Hair that stretches and fails to spring back has lost elasticity and internal strength. The cortex is no longer holding shape the way it should. Continuing to lighten would stress already weakened bonds and risk breakage. A reconstructive treatment to rebuild strength should come before further chemical services.

A senior position, an early certificate, or an owner's personal endorsement do not involve the standardized, judged format a competition provides. These competitions exist specifically to showcase and benchmark trade skill. They are a recognized path within the skilled trades system generally.

A second pass across or against the grain may follow once the skin has acclimated to the first pass. Starting against the grain on unprepared skin significantly raises the risk of razor burn.

This lets the stylist calm the warmth without pushing the colour toward an unrelated tone. Recognizing gold's position on the wheel is what makes this correction predictable.

This softens a blunt perimeter and adds texture without removing significant length. It is commonly used as a finishing step after a blunt or graduated base cut.

This client's lightened and glossed hair is in that category. The stylist should start at the lowest temperature that will still achieve the result and raise it only if needed.

This is why standard precautions treat every client's blood and body fluids as potentially infectious rather than relying on appearance or self disclosure. Head lice, scabies, and foodborne illness spread through entirely different routes and are not the basis for this specific protocol. Applying the same precautions to every client is what makes the protocol reliable.

Protein-based treatments temporarily fill gaps along the shaft and add strength and firmness. This is the better match for over-processed, breaking hair. Moisture treatments would improve softness but not the weakened structure.

Retail recommendation is a normal and expected part of a stylist's professional role.

Synthetic-specific shampoo is meant specifically for synthetic fibre wigs rather than for human hair pieces. An ordinary, potentially harsher shampoo is not automatically appropriate simply because the product is meant for hair in general.
  84. The angle also changes how the pattern reads on the client and how it softens as new growth appears. This is why placement is planned with the client's intended style and regrowth in mind.
  85. Texturizing is generally done farther out from the scalp, depending on the desired effect. It does not produce a softer outline, a stronger weight line, or a smoother blend when done this close in.
  86. The correct answer is A (Move promptly to neutralizer to protect the already sensitive section, accepting a softer curl overall.): Mixed findings like this call for prioritizing hair safety, so moving promptly to neutralizer protects the section already showing elasticity loss from progressing to actual breakage, even if it means accepting a somewhat softer curl on the rest of the head than originally planned. Continuing to the full planned time risks turning an early warning sign into real damage. Extending time further specifically on the already weakening section directly increases the risk that concerns the stylist in the first place. Ignoring a genuine early warning sign because other parts of the head need more time sets aside a real safety concern.
  87. The correct answer is C (A large barrel round brush, which produces a looser bend or soft wave.): Brush choice works alongside tension and angle to control the outcome. A large barrel round brush produces a looser bend or soft wave and suits longer hair, while a small diameter round brush wraps hair into a tight curve for short to medium hair or tight curl definition. Since the client asked for a soft loose wave rather than defined curl, the large barrel is the correct selection.
  88. The correct answer is D (A missing consent form leaves the salon without documentation that informed consent was obtained.): A missing consent form means the salon lacks documentation showing the client was informed of the risks and agreed to proceed, which is exactly the protection this kind of form is meant to provide for both parties. Assuming there is no consequence as long as the outcome was fine overlooks the ongoing dispute the client is now raising. Consent documentation is useful at the time of service, not only if a problem later arises. Relying on verbal agreement alone, without a written form, leaves this exact kind of gap when a disagreement occurs later.
  89. The correct answer is D (Ask about comfort once the water touches the client, since the wrist test is preliminary.): Water temperature should be tested against the stylist's own wrist before it touches the client, then checked verbally once contact begins. Water that feels fine to the stylist may feel too warm to the client, so the client's response is the final guide. Continuing without a verbal check risks discomfort or a burn. The wrist test is a screening step, not a substitute for asking.
  90. The correct answer is B (The booth renter typically arranges their own liability insurance coverage.): A stylist operating under booth rental is generally responsible for arranging their own liability insurance, since they function as an independent business rather than an employee automatically covered by the salon's policy. The salon's own insurance typically does not extend to cover an independent contractor's separate business activity. Liability insurance is available to independent hairstylists and is a normal part of operating a small business. The apprenticeship authority regulates certification, not insurance coverage.
  91. The correct answer is D (It blends naturally with existing hair while covering only the thin area.): An integration piece is woven directly through the client's existing hair at the thinning area so her natural hair blends with the piece and effectively disguises the smaller base underneath. This makes it well suited to localized crown thinning rather than to more extensive overall hair loss across the scalp. It is also a considerably lighter-weight option than a full wig cap or a bonded extension method.
  92. The correct answer is A (Individual hair can vary somewhat in exactly how the pigment stages appear.): While the general progression of underlying pigment is predictable, individual factors such as hair history, porosity, and natural pigment concentration can cause some variation in exactly how a stage appears between clients. The overall sequence still holds as a reliable planning tool even with this individual variation. This is why a strand test remains useful even when the general theory is well understood.
  93. The correct answer is C (A diagonal back line, since it builds rounded graduation while directing weight backward in one pass.): A diagonal back cutting line angles shorter at the front and longer toward the back, which builds increasing length and graduation toward the nape while directing weight backward and away from the face in a single pass, closely matching both stated goals. A horizontal line does not inherently direct weight in any particular direction, and a vertical line's rounding effect does not itself push weight toward the back. A diagonal forward line moves length and weight toward the face, the opposite of what is wanted here.
  94. The correct answer is B (It gives a strong, evidence based starting point, adjusted for any changes since then.): A detailed record of rod size, wrap pattern, processing time, and result gives a genuinely useful, evidence based starting point for planning today's service, though it should still be adjusted for any changes in the client's hair condition or history since that visit. Disregarding it entirely because of the time gap wastes real, relevant information. Hair condition can change over eighteen months, so following the old plan with no adjustment at all is not the safest approach. The record's value does not depend on whether the exact same physical rods are still on hand, since it is the technique information that matters.
  95. The correct answer is A (A larger roller makes a looser, wider wave with more volume and less definition.): The hair can only curl as tightly as the object it is wrapped around, so a large roller forms a loose, wide wave with more volume and less defined curl. A small roller forms a tight, well defined curl of a smaller diameter. The selection should match the intended curl size before the set is wound.
  96. The correct answer is B (Taking brief breaks to stretch and alternating techniques rather than repeating one motion for hours.): Taking brief breaks to stretch the hands and shoulders, and alternating techniques rather than repeating one identical motion for hours, reduces cumulative strain from repetitive tasks like detailed foiling. Working through the stiffness without pausing, or increasing pace, adds more repetition rather than relieving it. Dismissing a connection between repeated motion and stiffness overlooks a well established ergonomic pattern. Building brief, regular breaks into a long repetitive task is the correct preventive habit.
  97. The correct answer is A (Approximately 4.5 to 5.5, a slightly acidic range that keeps the cuticle flat and tight.): Healthy hair and scalp sit on the slightly acidic side, commonly cited at about 4.5 to 5.5. This acidity keeps the cuticle lying flat, which supports shine and limits moisture loss. A neutral or alkaline product would swell and lift the cuticle and leave hair more porous unless followed by a closing step. Formulating close to the natural range is gentler on the cuticle.
  98. The correct answer is B (Sending confirmation reminders while reserving deposits for higher-cost or longer services.): Sending confirmation reminders for all appointments while reserving deposits specifically for longer or higher-cost services balances reducing no-shows against not discouraging new clients from booking. Requiring full prepayment from everyone can feel like an unnecessary barrier for a routine service. Sending no reminders at all would likely increase no-shows rather than reduce them. Charging a fee before an appointment has even occurred does not match a fair no-show policy, since the client has not yet missed anything.
  99. The correct answer is B (Whether her overall density can safely support the added weight of the beads.): Low overall density across the whole head is a genuine safety consideration for any bonded or beaded extension method, since each individual attachment point places real weight on only a small section of natural hair. Colour matching and bead style are purely cosmetic decisions that matter only once the underlying safety question has been properly settled first. Appointment length is simply a scheduling detail, not the primary factor that should guide whether this service proceeds at all.
  100. The correct answer is D (The level 3 client needs more lift and will pass through more warm stages.): The client starting at level 3 must lift further to reach the same level 8 target, so they pass through more, and stronger, warm underlying pigment stages along the way. This generally calls for a more careful lightening strategy, possibly across multiple sessions. The level 6 client has a shorter distance to travel and a simpler path to the same target.
  101. The correct answer is B (The extra strand diameter resists the shallow blade angle needed for clean cutting.): Coarse, resistant hair has greater strand diameter, which resists the shallow angle a razor needs to cut cleanly and can make the technique less effective or harder to control. This does not mean razor cutting is impossible, but it does require extra care and awareness. Recognizing this challenge helps the stylist decide how and where to apply razor work.
  102. The correct answer is B (No, formaldehyde-free formulas can still release irritating fumes when heated with a flat iron.): Even formulas marketed as formaldehyde-free can release other irritating fumes when heated with a flat iron, so good ventilation remains a standard precaution for the whole category of smoothing treatments, not just formulas known to contain formaldehyde. Assuming no fumes at all under any heat condition is not accurate for this category. Ventilation is a general precaution across smoothing treatments rather than one limited only to products that list formaldehyde specifically, and the reasoning about extra heat is not an accurate explanation of why ventilation still matters.
  103. The correct answer is C (Right over centre, then left over centre, alternating down the length.): The three-strand braid is the foundation of every other braid. The hair is divided into three even sections, and the outer strands are alternately crossed over the centre strand, right over centre, then left over centre, repeating down the length. Crossing strands underneath produces a Dutch braid, which is a different technique.
  104. The correct answer is D (Oiling the pivot point, since stiffness often points to a lubrication issue rather than dullness.): A shear that feels stiff or gritty when opened and closed typically needs oil at the pivot point before it needs sharpening, since periodic oiling prevents the metal to metal friction that causes this kind of feeling. Jumping straight to professional sharpening, or assuming the shear must be replaced, treats a lubrication issue as though it were a blade condition issue. Ignoring the stiffness does not address a problem that can affect cutting quality and comfort over time. Addressing pivot maintenance first is the correct initial step for this specific symptom.
  105. The correct answer is C (It oxidizes the reduced bonds and reforms them in the new configuration.): The alkaline relaxer breaks the disulfide bonds so the hair can be reshaped. The neutralizer is an oxidizing formula that reforms those bonds in the new position. This locks the new shape into the cortex and makes the change lasting. Without neutralization the bonds stay reduced and the hair reverts to its original form.
  106. The correct answer is C (Requiring a deposit at booking for longer or higher-cost services like extensions.): A deposit collected at the time of booking directly offsets the financial loss caused by a late cancellation on a long or costly service. Refusing all future bookings is an extreme response that does not address the underlying scheduling risk for other clients. Raising prices for every extension client punishes people who show up reliably. Removing the cancellation policy entirely would make the problem worse, not better.
  107. The correct answer is A (Avoid exfoliating directly over broken skin and reassess the overall service plan.): Exfoliating directly over broken or already irritated skin can genuinely worsen the condition and increase both discomfort and infection risk for the client. The stylist should modify or postpone that particular step and reassess whether the full treatment remains appropriate given her current scalp condition today. Persistent itchiness combined with broken skin may also warrant a broader referral discussion with the client.
  108. The correct answer is A (A violet based toner, because violet sits opposite yellow on the colour wheel.): A violet based toner neutralises yellow, because violet and yellow are complementary colours that sit opposite each other on the colour wheel. Pale yellow is the residual warmth left at the highest levels of lift, which is why most pale blonde toners lean violet or violet ash. A blue toner would target orange instead, and a green or red base would not cancel the gold present here.
  109. The correct answer is D (Graduation builds and shapes weight, while layering removes weight and builds movement.): Graduation builds and shapes weight, producing a rounded, beveled silhouette, while layering removes weight and builds movement, producing an evenly distributed, textured silhouette, which is a clear way to summarize the distinction. The two are related but are not the same technique with two names. Neither claim about guideline type or exclusive razor use accurately describes graduation or layering.
  110. The correct answer is A (Extra time to ensure adequate airflow is running before the smoothing treatment begins.): Because good ventilation is a standard precaution for chemical smoothing treatments, scheduling should allow extra time to ensure adequate airflow is already running before the treatment begins rather than starting it the moment the client sits down. Ventilation is a standard safety practice rather than something offered only when a client happens to ask for it. It is the smoothing treatment, given the fumes it can release when heated, that specifically calls for this extra scheduling consideration, not the unrelated haircut appointment before it.
  111. The correct answer is C (A strong hold hairspray with film forming resins.): Hairspray is a finishing product applied at the end of a style to hold the finished shape in place, working through film-forming resins suspended in a carrier that flash off and leave a light polymer film. Strong hold sprays withstand humidity and hours of wear and are most often used to finish an updo. Lighter holds are meant to be brushed through and allow movement.
  112. The correct answer is C (Discard the used neck strip and use a new one for the next client.): Single use items such as neck strips are designed to be discarded after contact with one client, regardless of how clean they appear afterward. Visible cleanliness does not indicate the absence of microorganisms, and these items cannot be effectively sanitized or disinfected once used. Wiping, lightly rinsing, or judging reuse based on familiarity with the client does not change this requirement. A fresh single use item is required for every client without exception.
  113. The correct answer is B (Use clarifying periodically to lift buildup, then a moisturizing shampoo for regular visits.): Clarifying shampoo uses stronger surfactants to remove heavy buildup such as styling residue and hard-water minerals. It is meant to be used periodically, since frequent use can be drying, especially on colour-treated, dry hair. A moisturizing shampoo with gentler surfactants is better for regular use. This client needs clarifying to manage buildup and moisturizing on other visits to protect her dry ends.
  114. The correct answer is D (A compulsory jurisdiction requires certification or registered apprenticeship to legally perform the trade.): In a compulsory jurisdiction, a person must be a registered apprentice or certified journeyperson to legally work in the trade for pay. In a voluntary jurisdiction, someone can work in a salon without formal certification, though only a certified stylist can use protected titles or claim the Red Seal endorsement. This distinction applies to employees generally, not only owners. Requirements differ by province and territory, so a stylist should confirm the rule where they intend to work.
  115. The correct answer is B (Follow the creative brief closely, even though it departs from her usual styling.): Editorial work is guided directly by the creative brief supplied by the photographer or art director, even in cases where the requested look departs significantly from a model's everyday personal style. Substituting the model's personal preference instead, or refusing the direction outright, would not genuinely serve the actual purpose of the scheduled shoot. Following the brief closely, including unconventional or unfamiliar requests, is simply part of working within an editorial context like this one.
  116. The correct answer is C (A predisposition test must be performed and checked before the service.): Because this is the client's first ever colour service, a predisposition test must be performed and its result checked before the actual permanent colour is applied. This step is a required safety measure, not an optional courtesy, and protects against an unexpected allergic reaction. Skipping it before a first time service is a serious professional lapse.
  117. The correct answer is A (A stationary guideline that stays fixed as each section is cut.): A stationary guideline stays fixed in one place, and each new subsection is combed back to that same fixed point and cut to meet it, producing a haircut where the perimeter length stays consistent all the way around. A traveling guideline instead allows length to change gradually, which is not the goal for a one-length bob. Cutting without any guideline removes the reference needed for a consistent perimeter.
  118. The correct answer is D (A station with strong, moving airflow directed away from where the flat iron is used.): A station with strong, moving airflow directed away from the flat-ironing area best supports the goal of reducing fume exposure during a chemical smoothing treatment, since good ventilation is a standard precaution for this category of service. Placing the station in the smallest, most enclosed room works against this goal rather than supporting it. Assuming fumes disperse on their own without any ventilation changes ignores the established need for airflow during this step, and opening a window only after the service ends does nothing to reduce exposure while the fumes are actually being released.
  119. The correct answer is D (Regular deep conditioning to help replace lost moisture and support integrity.): Regular deep conditioning helps replace lost moisture and support the hair's structural integrity between services, which counteracts the drying effects of cumulative heat exposure. A clarifying shampoo is for removing build-up rather than rebuilding condition, and a silicone serum coats the surface rather than supplying moisture. Deep conditioning is the recovery step alongside heat protectant and lower heat.
  120. The correct answer is D (An unreviewed record can miss a chemical treatment that makes combining processes unsafe.): Combining certain chemical processes, such as applying a strong relaxer over hair that already carries another chemical treatment, can cause serious damage that a stylist working from memory rather than the actual record might not anticipate. Relying on memory instead of the documented history introduces a real risk of missing a detail that a written record would have captured. This risk exists whether or not the same stylist performed the earlier service, since even the original stylist's memory can be incomplete months later. Chemical history from months earlier remains directly relevant to what can safely be done today.
  121. The correct answer is A (Use the pads of the fingers in slow circular movements that move the scalp.): Effective scalp massage uses the pads of the fingers, not the nails, applying firm but comfortable pressure in slow circular or gliding movements. The motion moves the scalp over the underlying skull rather than sliding over the hair surface. Nails or a comb can scrape the scalp and cause irritation. Service usually begins at the front hairline and works toward the crown and nape.
  122. The correct answer is D (Worker and employer representatives meet to identify hazards and recommend corrections.): A joint health and safety committee brings together worker and employer representatives to identify workplace hazards, investigate incidents, and recommend improvements. It is a shared responsibility rather than a decision made unilaterally by ownership. It has no role in inspecting other businesses or involving clients in equipment purchases. This structure reflects the general right of workers to know about and participate in addressing hazards.
  123. The correct answer is B (Working efficiently while still delivering a precise, camera-ready finished result.): Editorial shoots typically run on tight production schedules, so the stylist needs to work efficiently while still producing a precise, camera-ready result that genuinely matches the creative brief for each look. Slowing down to a normal salon pace, or abandoning the brief altogether, would not meet the demands of the production. Efficiency and precision both matter equally under real shoot conditions like these.
  124. The correct answer is A (It applies the product to a patch of skin so a reaction can surface early.): A patch test is done in advance of the service by applying a small amount of the product to a patch of skin, usually behind the ear, so any reaction can be noticed before the hair is processed. It must not be skipped when the client's history is uncertain. This is a safety step that protects the client from an unexpected reaction during the service.
  125. The correct answer is C (The occipital bone, where the head curves toward the neck.): The occipital bone is the bony protrusion at the base of the skull where the head curves from the back down toward the neck, and locating it correctly is necessary before blending toward it. The apex, parietal ridge, and crown are landmarks located elsewhere on the head. Following an instruction like blending into the occipital only makes sense once this landmark can be found by touch.

Practice Test 3 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Rewrap the section and allow more processing time before checking it again.): A barely visible curl pattern means processing is not yet far enough along, so the correct response is to rewrap the section and allow more time before checking again, continuing to monitor rather than proceeding on insufficient evidence. Moving to neutralizer on a faint pattern would lock in an underdeveloped curl. A single check result should not be treated as final proof for the rest of the head, and switching products mid-test introduces a variable that defeats the purpose of the check.
  2. The correct answer is C (A standing barrel pin curl built for height and fullness.): A pin curl can be sculpted to lie flat against the scalp for a smooth, close curl, or built with a standing barrel shape for more height and a fuller curl once released. The barrel shape gives a more dimensional result. The stem left free then controls how much the finished curl moves.
  3. The correct answer is A (Assess whether the fire stays small and contained, while keeping the client's safe evacuation ready.): A small, clearly contained fire, such as one confined to a waste bin, is the situation where using a nearby extinguisher is appropriate, but this should be done while remaining ready to evacuate the client immediately if the fire grows or does not respond to the extinguisher. Evacuating without ever assessing whether the fire is genuinely small and containable can be an overreaction in some cases, but safety must still take priority the moment the fire shows any sign of spreading. Using an extinguisher without watching for whether the fire is actually staying contained risks missing the moment evacuation becomes necessary instead. Leaving this safety decision to the client rather than the trained stylist does not reflect the stylist's actual responsibility in the moment.
  4. The correct answer is C (Avoid vigorous manipulation over that tender area and keep the massage gentle there.): A stylist should avoid vigorous manipulation over any area with a scalp condition, a recent chemical service, or reported tenderness. Pressure should be adjusted to the client's comfort. Massaging firmly over a tender, irritated area from a tight style could worsen the irritation. Gentle work elsewhere while avoiding that spot is the appropriate adjustment.
  5. The correct answer is B (The booth renter is an independent contractor rather than an employee of the salon.): A stylist who rents a chair or booth is generally self-employed and operates as an independent contractor, not as an employee of the salon owner. This means they handle their own income tax filing, Canada Pension Plan contributions, and any required sales tax registration. They are not part owners of the business and typically must arrange their own liability insurance. A commission-based stylist, by contrast, is a true employee with deductions withheld by the employer.
  6. The correct answer is D (A canvas block preserves the cap shape and prevents matting or creasing.): Storing a wig on a canvas block or stand holds the shape of the cap and prevents the hair from matting or creasing the way folded drawer storage often does. This recommendation applies to both human hair and synthetic wigs, not to one fibre type alone. Proper block storage extends the useful life of the piece and cuts down on styling time before each wear.
  7. The correct answer is B (20 volume developer is generally appropriate for roughly two levels of lift.): 20 volume developer is the commonly used general purpose strength capable of lifting the natural level by roughly two levels while also depositing colour. It is the strength most stylists reach for when a moderate lightening change is requested alongside a new shade. Choosing a much weaker or stronger volume would not match this specific two level goal.
  8. The correct answer is D (The sharp contrast itself is the intended design, unlike a fade which avoids any visible line.): Disconnection is defined by a deliberate, sharp contrast between two lengths or textures with no gradual blend, which is the opposite goal of a fade or taper that specifically works to eliminate any visible line. Understanding this difference helps the stylist execute the design intentionally rather than accidentally blending an area meant to stay sharply defined. This distinct technique is a common design choice in men's clipper work.
  9. The correct answer is C (A standard starting estimate with a test curl check, since low porosity resists solution.): Smooth, slightly resistant hair with no damage or chemical history typically indicates a healthy, low-porosity cuticle, which resists solution and takes longer to process than porous hair, supporting a standard starting estimate confirmed with a test curl check rather than a shortened one. Skipping a progress check and going straight to the maximum time removes the direct evidence a stylist needs about how this specific hair is actually responding. Reaching for a stronger solution is not warranted just because hair feels healthy and resistant.
  10. The correct answer is C (Lifted up and away from the scalp while drying the root area of the section.): The angle of the brush against the head controls the amount of root lift in the finished style. Lifting the brush up and away from the scalp while drying the base builds volume. Directing the brush down and close to the head flattens the root and produces a smoother silhouette.
  11. The correct answer is A (Wash the area immediately with soap and water and follow the exposure reporting procedure.): If a stylist's own broken skin contacts a client's blood, the correct response is to wash the area immediately with soap and water and then follow the employer's exposure reporting procedure. Simply monitoring for symptoms later, using only hand sanitizer, or bandaging without washing first skips the immediate cleaning step that reduces exposure risk. This response applies to the stylist's own exposure just as consistently as it applies to protocols for the client. Prompt washing and reporting is the standard response to this kind of incident.
  12. The correct answer is D (The cortex, the bulk of the shaft containing the pigment granules that chemical colour alters.): The cortex makes up the bulk of the hair shaft and is where melanin granules give hair its natural colour. Permanent colour and chemical texture services work by altering the cortex, which is why these services carry the greatest risk if timing or strength is wrong. The cuticle sits above and controls penetration, while the medulla is a loose core absent from many strands. The papilla nourishes the follicle and has no role in pigment storage.
  13. The correct answer is C (The right to refuse work the stylist believes is genuinely unsafe to perform.): A worker generally has the right to refuse work they believe is genuinely unsafe, which applies directly to using equipment with a suspected electrical hazard. Continuing to use damaged equipment until a client complains ignores the actual safety risk rather than addressing it. Selling faulty equipment to another business would simply pass the hazard along. Disabling a fire alarm has nothing to do with the situation and would itself create a serious safety violation.
  14. The correct answer is D (Very little time remains to adjust the style if real changes are needed.): A trial run should ideally happen well before the actual event date so there is enough time to make meaningful adjustments if the client is not satisfied with the first attempted result. Booking the only trial just two days out leaves very little room to change course before the wedding itself. This is exactly why event styling is best planned around an earlier, well-spaced trial appointment.
  15. The correct answer is B (A blue-green based toner should be selected to neutralize the red-orange stage.): Combining the two principles, a blue-green based toner is the correct complementary choice to neutralize a red-orange underlying stage exposed during lifting. Selecting a toner that matches rather than opposes the underlying warmth would fail to correct it. This is a practical example of applying both the level pigment sequence and the colour wheel together.
  16. The correct answer is C (The nape, below the occipital bone at the hairline.): The nape is the area below the occipital bone, covering the back of the neck up to the hairline, and it is often the finest, most delicate hair on the head. It is typically cut with the most control, frequently using clippers or precise shear work close to the skin. The apex, parietal ridge, and crown are located higher on the head and are not described this way.
  17. The correct answer is B (The hair is ready for neutralizer, and this timing can guide the full service.): A well-defined curl matching the rod's diameter within the expected window indicates the hair is ready for neutralizer, and this result gives the stylist a reliable timing estimate to guide the full head service. A clear, well-formed result like this is conclusive rather than something requiring an unrelated repeat test. It does not suggest the hair cannot tolerate the rod, and neutralizer is still required to actually lock the reformed bonds into the new curled shape.
  18. The correct answer is A (Leave no stem so the curl sits closest to the head.): The amount of hair free between the base of the curl and the clip is called the stem, and it controls how much mobility the finished curl will have. A curl with no stem sits closest to the head and moves the least. A full stem curl has more give and produces a softer, more mobile finish.
  19. The correct answer is A (Switch to nitrile gloves, which avoid the latex allergy risk the client has described.): Nitrile gloves are the standard alternative when latex allergy is a concern, since they provide the same chemical resistance without the allergy risk latex presents. Reasoning that the allergy only affects the client, and not the gloves themselves, misunderstands that latex proteins can transfer through touch during the service and trigger a reaction. Skipping gloves altogether removes protection the stylist still needs for the chemical service. Switching glove type is the straightforward and correct response to this information.
  20. The correct answer is D (Compromised elasticity means weakened bonds, so this hair is a poor candidate for further chemical services.): Healthy wet hair stretches roughly 40 to 50 percent beyond its length and still returns, because wet hair is naturally more elastic. A strand that snaps after only a short stretch, or that stretches without springing back, has compromised elasticity. This reflects weakened internal bonds from over-processing and makes the hair a poor candidate for further chemical services. Stabilizing it with reconstructive treatment is the appropriate next step.
  21. The correct answer is B (The workspace, backbar product, and often front desk and marketing support.): In exchange for a commission percentage, a salon typically provides the workspace, backbar product, and often front desk and marketing support that help generate client traffic. This does not include a guaranteed minimum number of weekly clients, since bookings depend on demand and the stylist's own clientele. It also does not include eventual ownership of the building, which remains a separate business matter. A commission stylist still follows salon scheduling policy as an employee rather than being exempt from it.
  22. The correct answer is C (Discuss available cosmetic options and suggest she also see a physician.): Sudden, diffuse shedding linked to a recent illness can have a genuine underlying medical cause, which places diagnosis and treatment outside a cosmetologist's scope of practice. The stylist can still discuss reasonable cosmetic options like additions or camouflage techniques while gently suggesting the client also see a physician. Ignoring the topic entirely, or promising a specific medical outcome, are both inappropriate responses here.
  23. The correct answer is B (Mix a fresh, correctly ratioed batch rather than diluting the current formula.): Rather than stretching a formula by adding extra developer beyond its correct ratio, the more sound approach is to mix an additional, correctly ratioed batch to complete the service. Diluting the ratio risks an inconsistent level and tone across the head. Maintaining the correct ratio throughout the entire application protects an even, predictable result.
  24. The correct answer is A (The difference between a curl's stretched length and its dry resting length.): Shrinkage describes the difference between how long a curl measures when stretched out and how long it actually sits once released and dry. On tightly coiled hair this difference can be substantial, sometimes drawing the hair up to nearly half its stretched length. Understanding shrinkage helps the stylist predict the true finished length of a curly cut.
  25. The correct answer is D (Residual relaxer may keep acting chemically on the hair and scalp until fully removed.): Any relaxer left on the scalp or hair continues to act chemically until it is fully removed, so a shortened rinse before the neutralizing shampoo risks leaving residual product that keeps working on hair and scalp longer than intended. Neutralizing shampoo does not instantly clear away all remaining relaxer on contact, which is why thorough rinsing beforehand matters just as much as the shampoo step. This risk is chemical rather than purely cosmetic, and cutting the rinse short does not speed up how the hair's pH actually stabilizes.
  26. The correct answer is B (A vent brush with open slots in its base.): A vent brush has widely spaced bristles and open slots in its base that let air pass directly through the section. This makes it an efficient choice for rough-drying while preserving natural texture and movement. A paddle brush and round brushes are selected when a smooth or directional finish is the goal.
  27. The correct answer is B (Build in reasonable buffer time around the service with the variable processing window.): Building in reasonable buffer time between appointments, particularly around chemical services with variable processing times, is a mark of professional scheduling judgment rather than simple overcaution. Scheduling the next client to start the exact instant a variable service is expected to end leaves no room for the process running longer than planned. Assuming the shortest possible outcome will always occur ignores the variability the process actually has. A buffer protects the schedule from the ripple effect that a single delayed service can otherwise create.
  28. The correct answer is A (That no water, product, or chemical can reach the skin at the neckline.): The towel and shampoo cape should be positioned so no water, product, or chemical reaches the client's skin or clothing at the neckline. A secure drape protects the client and presents a professional standard. The drape should be checked again if the client repositions. Draping is also the chance to ask about allergies and recent chemical services.
  29. The correct answer is A (Meet regularly to identify hazards and recommend workplace safety improvements.): A joint health and safety committee exists to identify hazards, investigate incidents, and recommend safety improvements, with both worker and employer representatives involved. It has no authority over retail pricing, the service menu, client bookings, or commission structure, which are separate business decisions. Its focus stays specifically on workplace health and safety matters. This structure reflects the general worker right to participate in identifying and correcting hazards.
  30. The correct answer is A (Avoid shaving directly over the irritated area, or reschedule the entire service.): Shaving directly over active cuts or already irritated skin meaningfully increases the risk of further injury and possible infection for the client. The stylist should avoid working over that specific area, or reschedule the entire service until the skin has had time to heal properly. A hot towel softens the beard and opens pores, but it does nothing to treat skin that is already broken.
  31. The correct answer is C (Feather the product so it is heaviest at the ends and lightest near the root.): Balayage relies on hand control to distribute the product rather than a foil boundary. Feathering so it is heaviest at the ends and lightest near the root produces a soft, gradual transition. Loading the crown heavily would create a hard line that reads as a distinct band.
  32. The correct answer is D (Assess each area's natural fall and adjust technique so the layers blend evenly.): Mixed textures across one head can behave differently under the same elevation, so assessing each area's natural fall and adjusting the approach helps the finished layers blend evenly despite the texture variation. Applying one identical elevation everywhere without regard for texture could produce an inconsistent result once the hair settles. This kind of texture-aware adjustment is part of adapting general cutting principles to an individual client's hair.
  33. The correct answer is D (A slightly shorter processing time with frequent test curl checks rather than a fixed full-strength timing.): Fine, moderately porous hair processes faster than average and carries a higher risk of overprocessing if a fixed maximum timing is used without checking progress. A shorter starting estimate combined with frequent test curl checks lets the stylist stop as soon as the curl has actually formed, balancing speed against the hair's real condition. Applying a fixed maximum timing, increasing chemical strength, or copying a previous client's timing all ignore this client's specific porosity and texture.
  34. The correct answer is A (Work in a clean section and move in one smooth pass at the right setting.): Repeated passes of a hot tool over the same section multiply the risk of damage far more than a single slower pass at a slightly higher temperature. Working in clean, appropriately sized sections and moving smoothly in one pass is safer. Dragging a tool back and forth concentrates heat and strain on the shaft.
  35. The correct answer is B (Loose metal tools stored together can nick and damage each other's cutting edges.): Shears kept loose in a drawer with other metal tools can nick their own edges through contact with those other tools, which is why a dedicated case or guard is standard practice. This storage habit is the opposite of what protects a sharp edge, not a recommended method for preserving it. Storage method does have a real effect on how long an edge stays in good condition, contrary to assuming it makes no difference. Using a dedicated case regardless of current condition prevents damage before it happens rather than reacting after the fact.
  36. The correct answer is D (The dermal papilla, which supplies blood to the hair bulb below.): At the base of the hair bulb sits the dermal papilla, the structure that carries the blood supply nourishing actively growing hair. It sits within the follicle, which extends from the epidermis down into the dermis. Damage to the papilla or follicle from repeated tension can stop that follicle producing hair again. This is why some hair loss is irreversible.
  37. The correct answer is A (They document that a consultation and any required testing actually occurred.): Accurate records document that a proper consultation and any required testing, such as a strand or patch test, actually took place before a chemical service, which protects both the client and the stylist. Records are not a pricing mechanism and do not exist to justify a higher charge. They are relevant regardless of whether a client buys retail product that day. While a salon might use aggregate data for other purposes, that is not the primary reason individual client documentation matters.
  38. The correct answer is B (A humidity-resistant hold with pins anchored securely into a genuinely solid base.): Outdoor humidity followed immediately by hours of active indoor wear demands a style genuinely built for durability, with pins anchored securely into a solid base and product specifically chosen to resist humidity throughout the day. A minimal-hold or unfinished style is unlikely to survive both the outdoor conditions and the sustained physical activity that follows it. Planning deliberately for the full combined day, not just one single environment, reflects good event styling judgment.
  39. The correct answer is D (Permanent oxidative colour is capable of both lifting and depositing coverage.): Permanent, oxidative colour is the only category that both lifts the natural pigment and deposits new colour with lasting durability, which is why it is chosen when a client wants both noticeable lightening and full grey coverage. The other categories can deposit tone but cannot reliably lift the natural level. Selecting the right category up front avoids a mismatch between the client's goal and the product's real capability.
  40. The correct answer is C (It gives an accurate reference for where the hemline should actually sit.): Combing the hair down to its natural fall before cutting gives the stylist an accurate reference point for where the hemline should sit once the client is finished and styled normally. Skipping this step risks cutting the hemline in a position that does not match how the hair actually falls day to day. This preparation step supports the precision one-length cutting requires.
  41. The correct answer is C (Detailed records still make every future service faster and safer to plan accurately.): Detailed records continue to make every future service faster and safer to plan, since the stylist is not guessing at what was used before or how the hair responded, and this value does not disappear simply because a client's history has been trouble free so far. Assuming records stop mattering after years without incident overlooks how useful accurate history remains for planning new formulas or techniques. Records are valuable for every client's ongoing care, not only those considered higher risk. A trouble free history is not a reason to stop documenting future services.
  42. The correct answer is B (Press the index and middle fingers on either side while combing the opposite direction.): The ridge in a finger wave is shaped by pressing the index and middle finger against the scalp on either side of the section while combing the hair in the opposite direction. This pushes a crest of hair up between the fingers. Each completed wave is secured with clips until the setting lotion is fully dry.
  43. The correct answer is A (The product is corrosive and can damage skin, eyes, or materials on contact.): The pictogram showing a test tube pouring onto a hand and a surface is the corrosion symbol, and it is commonly seen on strong alkaline products such as relaxers. It warns that the product can cause serious skin or eye damage, or damage certain materials, on direct contact. A flammability warning uses a flame symbol, and a milder irritant is marked with an exclamation point rather than the corrosion symbol. Recognizing this pictogram tells a stylist to handle the product with the level of caution its label and safety data sheet describe.
  44. The correct answer is D (Seborrheic dermatitis, which often benefits from a medicated treatment beyond salon shampoo.): Seborrheic dermatitis is a more inflammatory cousin of dandruff with redness, greasy yellowish scale, and persistent itching. It often extends to the eyebrows or the sides of the nose. These clients frequently need a medicated approach beyond a salon shampoo. A stylist should recommend referral or a medicated product rather than treating it as simple dandruff.
  45. The correct answer is C (It marks the minimum stock quantity that should trigger reordering that product.): A par level is the minimum quantity of a product that a salon wants to always have on hand. Once stock falls to that level, it signals staff to reorder before the item runs out completely. It has nothing to do with retail pricing, staff authorization to sell, or charitable donations. Tracking par levels helps a salon avoid both running out of product and overordering stock that sits unused.
  46. The correct answer is B (A lightweight clip-in hair addition worn temporarily just for volume purposes.): Given her short ten day timeline, chemically weakened hair, and clear heat sensitivity, a temporary clip-in addition adds real volume for the wedding without adding lasting weight, heat exposure, or long-term tension to already compromised hair. Fusion is ruled out entirely by her stated heat sensitivity, and both micro-link and sew-in add sustained weight that fine, weakened hair may not safely carry for very long. A genuinely temporary option matches her short timeline and current hair condition together at once.
  47. The correct answer is D (Lightening diffuses natural melanin, while a colour reducer targets synthetic dye.): Lightening powder works primarily on natural melanin, diffusing it to lift the hair's natural level. A colour reducer is formulated to break down artificial dye deposited by a previous service. The two target different things, which is why they are not interchangeable.
  48. The correct answer is A (Move the neckline slightly higher, just above the natural growth break.): Placing the neckline just above the natural break where growth stops creates a crisp, intentional-looking edge rather than an uneven blend into the neck. Moving it well past that natural point would remove more of the beard than the client likely wants. This adjustment corrects the specific issue of an unclear or uneven neckline.
  49. The correct answer is C (Reading the actual ingredients and following the specific precautions each formula requires.): The correct approach is reading the actual ingredients on each product and following the specific precautions that formula requires, since smoothing products in the same general category can differ meaningfully in what they release when heated. Assuming both are equally safe simply because they serve the same purpose overlooks real differences between formulas. An unlisted formaldehyde ingredient does not guarantee a product is free of other fume-producing ingredients when heated. Ingredient differences directly affect real safety planning and should not be set aside in favour of price alone.
  50. The correct answer is D (A large roller, which gives a loose wide wave with more volume and less defined curl.): Roller diameter directly determines the diameter of the resulting curl. A large roller produces a loose, wide wave with more volume and less defined curl, while a small roller produces a tight, well-defined curl with a smaller diameter. Because this client asked for loose waves rather than defined curls, the large roller is the correct choice.
  51. The correct answer is C (The apprentice's right to know about workplace hazards before being exposed to them.): The right to know requires that a worker be informed of known workplace hazards and given the training to work safely around them before that exposure occurs, including on a first day of work. Assigning an apprentice to assist with a chemical service without this information overlooks a fundamental protection rather than a minor courtesy. Uniform choice, mentor assignment, and advance scheduling are unrelated workplace matters. Providing hazard information and training before the task begins is what this right actually requires.
  52. The correct answer is D (The dermis, the thicker layer containing vessels, glands, and the follicle base.): The dermis lies beneath the epidermis and is thicker and more complex. It contains the blood vessels, nerve endings, sebaceous glands, and the lower portion of the hair follicle. Blood vessels here supply the nutrients and oxygen the hair bulb needs during growth. The epidermis is the thin outer layer that renews itself.

A Certificate of Qualification does not typically expire and require renewal through continuing education in that sense. Manufacturers offer training but do not have legal authority to mandate annual attendance from every stylist. Continuing education in technique also does not replace separate, ongoing safety training obligations.
  54. The correct answer is B (Extended wear well past eight weeks risks matting, buildup, and root tension.): Protective styles such as box braids are generally recommended for no more than about six to eight weeks of continuous wear before removal or a fresh reinstallation. Leaving the style in for several months meaningfully increases the risk of matting, product buildup, and unnecessary tension building up at the root. The stylist should explain a realistic maintenance timeline rather than approve the client's extended wear plan.
  55. The correct answer is A (Place a strand in water and peroxide with ammonia and watch for discolour or heat.): Because metallic dye is not always disclosed, a metallic salt test is a standard precaution. A small strand is placed in water with peroxide and a few drops of ammonia, and hair containing metallic salts will discolour, heat up, or bubble quickly. If that happens, the service must not proceed.
  56. The correct answer is C (A vertical cutting line, running up and down the head.): A vertical cutting line runs up and down rather than side to side, creating even graduation or layering that follows the curve of the head from the nape to the crown and producing a rounded silhouette. A horizontal line instead produces a level, blunt shape. Diagonal forward and diagonal back lines move length toward the face or toward the back rather than around the head's curve.
  57. The correct answer is C (The neutralizing shampoo is formulated specifically to address residual alkaline chemical from the service.): The neutralizing shampoo used right after a relaxer service is formulated specifically to address residual alkaline chemical and help stabilize the hair's pH, which is a different job than a routine shampoo performs at a later, ordinary wash. Any shampoo in the salon is not interchangeable here given this specific chemical function. Its selection is not based on scent, and routine shampoo is not the stronger or more appropriate option for use directly after a relaxer service.
  58. The correct answer is B (Start at the lowest temperature that achieves the result and raise only if needed.): Colour-treated and porous hair tolerates the lowest range of thermal styling heat because it has less structural reserve to absorb heat safely. When in doubt the stylist should start at the lowest temperature that will realistically achieve the result and raise it only if needed. Repeating short passes increases cumulative risk, while a very high setting risks immediate scorching on already compromised hair.
  59. The correct answer is A (Tool weight affects the strain placed on the hand and forearm during repetitive use.): Tool weight is part of the ergonomic picture, and a heavier clipper increases the physical demand on the hand and forearm during repetitive cutting motions compared with a lighter model. Claiming tool weight makes no difference contradicts the direct experience the stylist is describing and the underlying mechanics involved. Improved precision, if any, does not eliminate the added physical strain the heavier tool places on the body. Choosing appropriately weighted tools is one practical way to manage this kind of fatigue.
  60.   61. The correct answer is B (The certificate is issued after training and exam requirements are fully met.): Apprenticeship registration happens when a person begins their training under the apprenticeship authority, while the Certificate of Qualification is issued afterward, once the required hours, technical training, and exam have all been completed. The registration comes first, not after the certificate. Neither document is issued automatically on day one, since both require the apprentice to progress through defined requirements. The certificate applies to a certified stylist regardless of whether they later work on commission, salary, or booth rental.
  62. The correct answer is A (Pulling risks breaking natural hair, and a proper bond remover is needed.): Fusion bonds are made of solid keratin and will not release safely through pulling or combing alone, which risks pulling natural hair strands out along with the bond itself. A dedicated keratin bond remover solution softens the bond so it can be gently crushed and slid free without damage. Attempting removal by force at home is unsafe and should be discouraged during the consultation.
  63. Completely omitting any supportive base risks weak, uneven grey coverage. Thoughtful formulation, not an all or nothing choice, is what reconciles these two goals.
  64. The correct answer is B (Elevation carried past ninety degrees, often combined with over-direction.): Elevation carried past ninety degrees, sometimes combined with over-direction, produces the shortest interior layers and the most dramatic weight removal, often used for very short or disconnected shapes. Zero degrees would instead preserve maximum weight rather than removing it dramatically. A stationary guideline and forty-five degree elevation are associated with more moderate, connected results.
  65.
