Extending time well past normal guidelines on already porous hair increases the risk of damage rather than improving the result. Porosity from prior chemical treatment is directly relevant to perm timing, and a colour service's timing has no bearing on how a completely different chemical process should be timed.
  66. The correct answer is B (A fishtail braid, which uses two large sections with small pieces crossed over alternately.): The fishtail, also called a herringbone braid, uses only two large sections of hair rather than three. A small piece is taken from the outer edge of one section and crossed over to join the opposite section, then repeated from the other side. Because no new hair is picked up as it progresses, a fishtail can be started anywhere on a length of hair, and the small repeated crossings produce the fine woven texture.
  67. The correct answer is D (Consult the product's safety data sheet, particularly its first aid section, for guidance.): The safety data sheet for a specific product includes a dedicated first aid section describing the correct response to skin contact, and stylists are expected to know where these sheets are kept and how to read them. Dismissing the reaction as minor without checking, delaying any response until later, or applying an unrelated chemical to the skin are all inappropriate reactions to an active exposure. Following the documented first aid guidance for that exact product is the safest and most accurate course of action. This is exactly the situation safety data sheets are designed to address.
  68. The correct answer is C (Traction alopecia, which can reverse if tension on that area is reduced.): Traction alopecia comes from sustained tension from tight braids, ponytails, or extensions over the same area. It shows most often along the front hairline. Because it is mechanical, a stylist can help cause or prevent it through styling choices. Caught early, before the follicle scars, reducing tension can often reverse it.

Adding a charge to the bill without ever mentioning it is dishonest and could reasonably be seen as a billing dispute.
  70. This method attaches extension hair one strand at a time rather than in a weft or bead. It gives very natural movement but takes far longer to install than the other three methods.

  72. The comb sets the angle and the clippers cut cleanly along its teeth. This makes it the appropriate choice for the client's specific request.

Improved grip is not the primary reason gloves are required for this purpose. Preventing hand oils from reaching the client's hair is not the established rationale for glove use during a chemical service. Hand temperature during rinsing is unrelated to the core safety reason gloves are worn.

Choosing the stem is part of controlling the final movement of the style.

This is not simply standard practice that falls outside professional standards, and it is a concern regardless of whether a client happens to complain about it. Advertising claims are part of professional conduct precisely because they shape a client's expectations before the service even begins. Accurate advertising about pricing and inclusions is expected as part of the trade's professional standards.

Only after that does the stylist tone and deposit the final target shade. Skipping ahead, such as toning before the warmth is neutralized, turns a correctable problem into a redo.

Using the same elevation consistently, by contrast, is what produces even layering rather than causing unevenness. Correcting the error involves combing the whole head to natural fall and re-cutting the sections that sit too long.

It does not grant an automatic business operating licence, set pricing across the industry, or exempt a stylist from provincial safety legislation, since licensing to practise and workplace safety rules remain provincial responsibilities. The endorsement specifically addresses interprovincial recognition of the tradesperson's own qualification. This is the core purpose the Red Seal system is designed to serve.

Applying a filler to redeposit missing pigment and even out porosity first is the standard corrective sequence. Skipping it and going straight to the target shade tends to give a patchy result that fades unevenly.

  89. Testing the same day right before the service does not allow that time to pass.   90. Cupping sections inside the bowl and holding the tool still on low or medium heat preserves the curl pattern and cuts down on frizz. A narrow nozzle or a raking motion would separate the clumps and roughen the cuticle.
  91. The correct answer is A (Begin flushing the eye continuously with clean, lukewarm water right away.): A chemical splash to the eye calls for immediate, continuous flushing with clean, lukewarm water for a full fifteen minutes, and this response should begin right away rather than waiting to see if discomfort passes on its own. A dry towel wipe does not remove chemical from the eye's surface, and a cold compress over a closed eyelid does not flush the chemical out either. Delaying the flush allows the chemical more time to cause damage. Starting the flush immediately, then seeking medical attention afterward, is the correct sequence.
  92. It is not contagious, but it is a medical condition a stylist should not attempt to treat or diagnose. The service should be adapted to avoid aggravating active plaques, and referral to a physician or dermatologist is appropriate. Scrubbing would worsen the plaques and is not within salon scope.

It does not reliably shorten the total service time, since correcting a misunderstanding later often takes longer. Most clients value being properly understood rather than preferring a shorter conversation. A rushed consultation also has no automatic effect on the final price of the service.
  94. A separate, genuinely lower-risk cosmetic option, such as a concealer or careful strategic styling, can still reasonably address her immediate event need at the same time. Treating both very different issues identically, or tightly styling directly over active patches, both risk worsening the actual underlying condition further.
  95. The correct answer is C (A strand test on a small section to confirm the colour and timing.): A strand test on a small section confirms how the hair will accept the colour, including resistant grey, before the full service is done. Different starting levels in different areas of the same head are a common cause of uneven results, which testing catches early. A thorough consultation and strand test prevent many corrective visits.
  96. The correct answer is D (A wedge-shaped or stacked silhouette with a defined perimeter line.): Graduation refers to a build of weight that increases gradually from a shorter interior to a longer exterior perimeter, creating a wedge-shaped or stacked silhouette that still has a defined, weighted perimeter line. Layering, by contrast, removes that connected weight line and produces an evenly distributed, textured silhouette instead. Graduation and layering are related but produce visibly different results, so they are not identical.
  97. The correct answer is C (Keratin treatments generally coat and bond a protein layer rather than permanently reforming bonds.): A keratin smoothing treatment generally works by coating and bonding a protein layer to the hair shaft and then sealing it with heat, rather than permanently reforming the hair's internal disulfide bonds the way a traditional relaxer does. Describing both services as working identically, or reversing which one reforms bonds versus coats the surface, misstates the actual difference between these two service types. The difference is chemical in nature, not simply a matter of how long each service happens to take.
  98. The correct answer is A (Most silicones are not water soluble and need a clarifying sulfate shampoo.): Silicones coat the outside of the hair shaft, sealing down the cuticle and filling in surface irregularities, which reduces frizz, adds shine, and lowers porosity. Because most silicones used in these products are not water soluble, build-up over repeated use generally needs a clarifying, sulfate-based shampoo to fully remove. A regular gentle shampoo alone leaves that film behind over time.
  99. The correct answer is D (Establishing a consistent maintenance routine early, which extends a tool's working life.): Treating maintenance as routine upkeep from the very beginning, rather than waiting for a problem to appear, is what extends the working life of an expensive tool like a new pair of shears. Assuming new shears need no attention for months overlooks that residue and friction begin affecting a tool from its very first uses. Waiting until a shear already feels dull skips the preventive stage where maintenance is most effective. This habit applies to any implement that sees regular use, not only tools involved in chemical services.
  100. The correct answer is C (A properly formulated conditioner or rinse that helps close the cuticle back down.): Natural hair and scalp are slightly acidic, around 4.5 to 5.5, and this acidity keeps the cuticle lying flat. Alkaline products swell and lift the cuticle, leaving hair more porous unless a step such as a properly formulated conditioner or rinse follows. A clarifying shampoo is too drying and does not close the cuticle. Additional perm solution would only further chemically alter the hair.
  101. The correct answer is B (Workers directly experience workplace hazards and contribute practical safety input.): Including worker representatives reflects that the people performing the daily tasks directly experience workplace hazards and can offer practical, firsthand input on identifying and correcting them. Employer representatives are not forbidden from discussing safety, they sit on the same committee working alongside worker representatives. Approving an annual budget is unrelated to the committee's health and safety mandate. Worker involvement is a substantive part of the process, not a symbolic gesture with no real function.
  102. The correct answer is C (A full lace cap, which allows styling and parting in any direction.): A full lace cap extends hand-tied lace construction across the entire base, allowing the hair to be parted and pulled straight back in any direction while the hairline stays undetectable. A wefted cap tends to reveal visible rows once the hair is pulled fully away from the face. This makes full lace the appropriate recommendation for a client who wants daily off-face styling.
  103. The correct answer is A (A colour reducer, because it targets synthetic dye rather than melanin.): Colour that will not lift despite a lightener that should be strong enough is a strong signal of underlying artificial tint, because permanent colour resists further lightening. A colour reducer is formulated to break down artificial colour molecules and generally has little effect on natural melanin. Recognising naturally dark hair from artificially tinted hair is a core corrective colour skill.
  104. The correct answer is D (Checking the length at multiple points during the drying process.): For curly and coily textures, many stylists check the length and shape at multiple points during drying specifically to control for shrinkage before removing more length than intended. Elevation choice, heavy thinning, and guideline type do not by themselves address the shrinkage that occurs as curl contracts on drying. This checking habit distinguishes texture-aware practice from a purely mechanical approach.
  105. The correct answer is B (Clear, specific detail about what was used and how the hair responded, not vague notes.): A genuinely useful record prioritizes clear, specific detail about what product and technique was used and how the hair actually responded, so that another stylist unfamiliar with the client can rely on it later. Reducing every entry to a single word sacrifices the detail that makes a record useful in the first place. Personal shorthand defeats the purpose of a record meant to be read by others. Leaving out anything that did not go exactly as planned removes exactly the information, such as a reaction or unexpected result, that matters most for future safety.
  106. The correct answer is A (A small diameter round brush that wraps the hair into a tight curve.): A small diameter round brush wraps hair into a tight curve and is used for short to medium hair or for tight curl definition. A large barrel round brush produces a looser bend or soft wave and suits longer hair. Brush size directly matches the bend the stylist wants.
  107. The correct answer is A (It penalizes a worker for exercising their protected right to refuse unsafe work in good faith.): Workers who refuse unsafe work in good faith, following the proper reporting process, are protected from discipline or reprisal for doing so. Reducing a stylist's shifts specifically because they raised and acted on a ventilation concern is exactly the kind of retaliation this protection exists to prevent. Whether the reduction is framed as temporary, or whether a lawsuit is eventually filed, does not change that the underlying action is a prohibited response to a protected right. Employers are expected to address the hazard, not penalize the worker who identified it.
  108. The correct answer is B (A protein-based treatment, since the hair is breaking from weakened internal bonds that need filling.): Protein-based treatments temporarily fill gaps along the hair shaft, adding strength and firmness, and are most useful for hair that is overly elastic or breaking from weakened bonds. Over-processed hair that snaps without springing back is a candidate for a protein treatment. Moisture-only treatment can leave such hair soft but still weak. Choosing protein here addresses the internal strength issue.
  109. The correct answer is D (Record it clearly in the client file and perform a patch test before applying colour.): A previous reaction to colour product is exactly the kind of detail that belongs in a client's record, along with a patch test performed before the next chemical service. Dismissing it because it occurred elsewhere ignores a real safety concern the client raised in good faith. Salons are fully able to record relevant health information as part of legitimate service documentation. Posting about client allergies publicly would also breach the client's reasonable expectation of privacy.
  110. The correct answer is A (Carefully assess the hair's current condition before applying any heat styling.): Recently relaxed hair has already gone through a significant chemical process that can leave it noticeably more fragile than usual, so the stylist should assess its current condition before adding heat on top of that recent treatment. Proceeding without checking risks compounding damage from two intensive services performed in close succession. Adjusting the plan, or postponing the silk press briefly, may be the safer professional choice here.
  111. The correct answer is D (Henna is not reliably removed, and the reaction with chemicals can generate heat.): Henna is a plant based dye that coats the hair shaft and can also penetrate the cuticle, and it is not reliably removed by a normal colour or lightening service. Applying a permanent oxidative colour or a lightener over henna can produce unpredictable results, and the interaction can generate heat. A client with any henna history needs a strand test before a chemical service proceeds.
  112. The correct answer is A (It helps melt guard lengths together rather than leaving visible banding.): Short, flicking, over-comb style motions worked through each transition zone blend adjacent guard lengths into one another rather than leaving them as distinct stacked bands. A single long steady stroke would not achieve the same gradual melting effect between lengths. This motion is central to producing a seamless fade or taper.
  113. The correct answer is D (Perform and document a predisposition test before proceeding with the next chemical service.): Even with a history of prior services, a missing predisposition test record is a gap that should be corrected by performing and documenting the test before the next chemical service, since sensitization can develop over time and testing is a standard safety practice tied to specific products rather than a one-time formality. Assuming years of prior tolerance rules out any future reaction is not a safe conclusion. The responsibility to initiate this safety step rests with the stylist rather than waiting for the client to raise it.
  114. The correct answer is A (A French braid, which picks up new hair from each side as it descends.): A French braid follows the same crossing pattern as a three strand braid but begins close to the scalp and gradually incorporates new hair from each side into the outer strands as it progresses downward. This produces a braid that appears to grow directly out of the scalp along its entire path. A Dutch braid uses the same pickup but crosses under the centre, so it stands out from the head.
  115. The correct answer is B (Shears not sized appropriately for the hand increase the grip force needed for cutting.): Shears sized appropriately for the stylist's hand reduce the grip force needed for repetitive cutting motions, and using an oversized pair increases that grip force, which can lead to noticeable fatigue over a shift. Tool size does have a measurable ergonomic effect, so assuming it makes no difference is incorrect. Fatigue caused by a poorly fitting tool does not simply resolve within a single day of continued use. Returning to a properly sized shear reduces the strain the stylist is currently experiencing.
  116. The correct answer is A (Low porosity means a tight cuticle that resists moisture and chemicals, so it needs longer processing.): Low porosity hair has a tight, compact cuticle that resists penetration, so colour and chemicals take longer to work. It often benefits from gentle heat or a longer development time rather than a stronger formula. High porosity has a lifted cuticle and processes faster, while normal porosity processes within standard timing. Elasticity is a separate measure of the cortex and does not describe cuticle resistance.
  117. The correct answer is C (It provides predictable income that does not depend directly on client volume.): An hourly wage or fixed salary gives a stylist steady, predictable pay regardless of how full their book is in a given week. This suits a newer stylist who is still building a clientele and cannot yet count on high commission earnings. It does not exempt the stylist from following salon policy or conduct standards, nor does it grant any ownership stake. The main tradeoff is that pay does not rise directly with individual productivity.
  118. The correct answer is D (A product chosen specifically to suit the exact scalp condition just observed.): Product selection for a scalp treatment should genuinely follow the consultation and match the specific condition actually observed, such as oiliness combined with flaking, rather than defaulting to one standard product for every client. Choosing a product meant only for a dry scalp would not properly address what this particular client is experiencing today. Flaking on its own does not automatically rule out an appropriate cosmetic treatment for her.
  119. The correct answer is C (Feather the product so it is heaviest at the ends and lightest near the root.): Balayage saturation is controlled by hand, feathering the product so it is heaviest at the ends and lightest near the root, producing a soft, gradual transition rather than a hard line. Consistent brush pressure and disciplined section size matter even more than in foiling. Grabbing sections too large or painting too fast is a frequent cause of uneven lift.
  120. The correct answer is C (Overstretching pulled the section past its natural resting point before cutting.): Overstretching a section before cutting pulls it beyond where it will actually rest once released, so the cut length appears too short compared with neighbouring sections. This is a common tension error in long layered cutting. Checking tension carefully and comparing each section to its neighbour before cutting helps prevent it.
  121. The correct answer is C (Carefully section and examine the hair closely to locate the growth line before starting.): When the growth line is not immediately obvious, the correct response is to section and examine the hair closely, feeling for texture changes between new and previously treated growth, before applying any product, since accurately locating that line is what prevents overlap. Treating the whole head as virgin hair would apply fresh relaxer onto already-straightened lengths regardless of the difficulty seeing the line. Rescheduling is unnecessary once the line can be found through careful examination, and applying product only to the ends would miss the actual new growth that needs treatment.
  122. The correct answer is B (Apply heat protectant because damage accumulates with repeated exposure.): Heat protectant is not optional on chemically treated, lightened, or otherwise compromised hair. These products deposit a coating of silicones or film-forming ingredients that cushion the shaft against the sudden heat of a plate and slow moisture loss. Thermal damage accumulates with repeated exposure over time, not only during one prolonged pass.
  123. The correct answer is D (Evacuate the salon and call emergency services rather than attempting to fight the spreading fire.): A stylist should never attempt to fight a fire that is spreading rather than small and contained, and the correct action once a fire is clearly spreading is to evacuate and call emergency services. Attempting to fight a fire that has already grown beyond a small, contained size puts the stylist and others at unnecessary risk. Pausing to unplug tools before deciding whether to evacuate wastes critical time once the fire is already spreading. Continuing client services nearby while the fire is monitored from a distance ignores the urgency the situation actually calls for.
  124. The correct answer is B (Anagen, the active growth phase that continues for roughly two to seven years.): Anagen is the active growth phase, the longest part of the cycle and where the vast majority of scalp hair sits at any time. Catagen is a brief transitional phase and telogen is the dormant, shedding phase. The dermal papilla nourishes the bulb but is not a growth-cycle phase. Ordinary shedding is normal and not a sign of pathological loss on its own.
  125. The correct answer is B (Explain honestly what is realistic and that an exact outcome cannot be promised.): Being honest about what is realistically achievable, without guaranteeing a specific outcome, reflects sound professional ethics, especially with unpredictable corrective colour work involving previous box colour. Promising an exact result to avoid an uncomfortable conversation sets up the client for disappointment and the stylist for a harder complaint later. Agreeing to a guarantee while privately planning to explain a shortfall afterward is misleading. Declining to discuss the service at all fails to give the client any useful information to make a decision.

Practice Test 4 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (A root and scalp concealer that reduces contrast between scalp and hair.): A root and scalp concealer powder or spray reduces the visual contrast between the scalp and surrounding hair, offering a quick, effective cosmetic solution suited to a single upcoming event. Diagnosing the underlying cause of the thinning sits outside a stylist's scope of practice, and a relaxer would not meaningfully address this visual concern at all. Concealer products are a common, genuinely appropriate salon tool for exactly this kind of situation.
  2. The correct answer is A (Discuss non-oxidative or alternative product options that do not trigger the reaction.): After a positive predisposition test, the responsible response is to discuss safer alternatives with the client, such as a non-oxidative colour category or a different product line the client has not reacted to. Proceeding with the original product despite the positive result puts the client at real risk regardless of their preference. A positive result on one product does not necessarily rule out every colour option available.
  3. The correct answer is C (Elevate every following section to the same ninety degree angle.): Uniform layering depends on holding the same elevation for every section so that volume distributes evenly rather than building weight in one area. Varying the elevation between sections would introduce graduation instead of uniform layering. Consistency in both elevation and comparison length is what defines this technique.
  4. The correct answer is B (Lye relaxers are highly alkaline and fast acting, raising the risk of scalp burns.): The base cream requirement tracks directly with how alkaline and fast acting a formula is, and lye relaxers, sitting at a high pH, carry a real risk of scalp burns if left in contact with skin, which is why the barrier is specifically emphasized for them. Base cream serves a genuine protective, not cosmetic, purpose. Gentler formulas can still reach the scalp during application even if the burn risk is lower. Protective needs vary by formula strength rather than being identical across every chemical service.
  5. The correct answer is A (The application method disturbs the curl pattern and encourages frizz.): Rubbing the hands together vigorously or scrunching product in roughly, rather than smoothing it down in one controlled motion, tends to disturb the curl pattern and increase frizz rather than reduce it. The correct product for the texture does not fix a poor application method. The praying hands method, pressing the palms together and smoothing down the section, encourages the strands to clump into a defined shape.
  6. The correct answer is D (Eye protection, since this procedure involves debris that might travel toward the eyes.): Eye protection is appropriate for procedures where debris might travel, and certain clipper work is specifically named among the tasks where this can occur. Chemical resistant gloves and a mixing apron are designed for chemical service tasks and do not address an airborne debris concern from clipper work. Assuming no PPE is ever needed for this task overlooks the actual hazard the debris presents. Matching the protection to the specific hazard, rather than defaulting to either extreme, is the correct approach.
  7. The correct answer is C (From the front hairline back toward the crown and nape.): Massage movement begins at the front hairline and works backward toward the crown and nape. This covers the whole scalp systematically and avoids missing areas. Working the scalp over the skull in one consistent direction is the recommended pattern. It helps the client relax and supports circulation.
  8. The correct answer is A (Par levels and ordering are not matched to actual client demand.): A shelf full of slow-selling product alongside frequent shortages of a requested item points to par levels and ordering that are not aligned with what clients actually buy. This is an inventory management issue, not a deliberate strategy to lower revenue. Clients are free to request specific products, and there is no such restriction. The unsold stock is not necessarily defective, it is simply not matched to current demand and should prompt a review of ordering decisions.
  9. The correct answer is B (Remove the wefts carefully and inspect the scalp for tension damage.): Tenderness and tightness reported near the hairline are warning signs of ongoing tension on the natural hair and scalp beneath a sew-in foundation. The wefts should be removed and the scalp examined carefully before any further extension work is allowed to continue. Tightening the foundation further or dismissing the discomfort as ordinary risks progressing toward genuine traction alopecia at the hairline.
  10. The correct answer is A (A colour reducer, which breaks down the artificial colour molecules.): A colour reducer is formulated to break down artificial colour molecules deposited by a previous service, so they can be rinsed out. It targets synthetic dye and generally has little effect on the hair's own natural melanin. Lightening powder, by contrast, works primarily on natural melanin and only lightens artificial colour incidentally.
  11. The correct answer is D (Wipe them clean of hair and product, then oil the pivot.): Shears should be wiped clean of hair and product after every client and have the pivot point oiled regularly as part of routine maintenance. This keeps the blades gliding smoothly and extends the life of the cutting edge. Leaving shears open, skipping cleaning, or over-tightening the pivot are all poor practice.
  12. The correct answer is B (Smooth, controlled strokes that guide the hair while the chemical does the straightening.): The correct smoothing technique uses smooth, controlled strokes that guide the hair into a straight position while the chemical itself does the work of breaking the bonds, rather than forcing straightness through mechanical tension. Aggressive or repeated combing and firm pulling can cause breakage on hair that is not yet chemically ready to be moved that far. Skipping combing entirely would leave the stylist unable to guide the hair evenly during processing, which the technique is specifically meant to provide.
  13. The correct answer is C (A large roller gives a loose wave with less defined curl.): The hair can only curl as tightly as the object it was wrapped around, so roller diameter directly sets the diameter of the resulting curl. Larger rollers produce a wide, loose wave with more volume and less defined curl. Smaller rollers produce a tighter, more defined curl with a smaller diameter.
  14. The correct answer is B (A missed check-in during processing broke the ongoing communication a consultation is meant to maintain.): Clear communication throughout the service, including checking in during a chemical process, closes the consultation loop and helps catch a problem like scalp discomfort before it becomes more serious. Treating this check-in as optional overlooks its actual purpose, and placing the full burden on the client to interrupt an ongoing service ignores the stylist's role in maintaining communication. Discomfort during processing is not something to simply expect and leave unaddressed. A regular check-in habit during longer chemical services helps catch concerns early.
  15. The correct answer is B (Roughly 1.25 centimetres per month during the anagen growth phase.): Scalp hair growth during anagen is commonly cited at roughly 1.25 centimetres per month. This varies by individual, scalp area, and factors such as age and health. The anagen phase itself is the active growth phase lasting two to seven years. Growth does not happen during the resting phases.
  16. The correct answer is D (How much income stability the stylist needs while building their own client base.): A new stylist with no existing clientele carries real income risk under commission, since pay depends directly on client volume they have not yet built. An hourly role at a newer salon offers predictable income while they develop skills and a following, even if the salon itself is less established. Interior decor, nearby restaurants, and a shorter commute are minor conveniences that do not address the core financial tradeoff. The decision should weigh income stability against long-term earning potential given the stylist's actual starting position.
  17. The correct answer is D (Reducing daily friction and manipulation that leads to breakage over time.): Protective styles are worn mainly to reduce the daily friction, tension, and manipulation that coily hair experiences, since it tends to break most easily at the point where the curl bends back on itself. They are not intended to permanently change the natural curl pattern underneath. The scalp still needs regular moisture and periodic cleansing while any protective style is being worn.
  18. The correct answer is A (The direction of the foils, choosing horizontal, diagonal, or vertical placement.): Foil placement, whether horizontal, diagonal, or vertical, changes how the highlight falls in the finished style and how it grows out. Varying the placement is what lets the colour work with the client's everyday style. Developer volume, brand, or foil size do not control the direction the highlight falls.
  19. The correct answer is A (The crown or front hairline, blending shorter sections up to that point.): Establishing the first guideline at the crown or front hairline gives the stylist a clear reference point to blend shorter interior sections into as the cut progresses toward the perimeter. Starting from the ends before any elevation is applied would not provide a reliable reference for layering. This is standard practice for building a long layered shape.
  20. The correct answer is C (Future stylists cannot accurately judge how this hair may respond to a similar service.): Missing the specific product brand and strength leaves future stylists without the detail needed to accurately judge how this client's hair may respond to a similar service, since different formulas and strengths genuinely affect processing and risk. Brand and strength are meaningfully connected to how hair responds, not an unimportant detail. This gap matters for any client's ongoing chemical history, not only those who have already had a reaction. Recording the date alone does not make up for missing the chemical specifics that actually guide safe planning.
  21. The correct answer is B (Ceramic or tourmaline coated plates that distribute heat more consistently.): Well-maintained tools with accurate temperature displays and evenly heating plates, such as ceramic or tourmaline coated surfaces, distribute heat more consistently than a worn or lower quality tool. They reduce hot spots that scorch a section of hair. A wider barrel or a higher temperature setting does not address uneven heat distribution, and a metal surface can worsen the problem.
  22. The correct answer is A (Add a workplace label showing the product name, safe handling information, and an SDS reference.): Any hazardous product transferred into a secondary container, such as a spray bottle used for daily convenience, still requires a workplace label under WHMIS 2015. That label must show at minimum the product name, safe handling information, and a reference to the full safety data sheet. Staying inside the immediate work area does not remove this requirement, and personal initials or a purchase date do not meet the standard. Proper labelling ensures anyone who picks up the bottle, not only the original stylist, knows what it contains and how to handle it safely.
  23. The correct answer is C (The hair is fine texture with high density, so it processes fast and still looks full.): Texture refers to the diameter of an individual strand and is judged by feel, classified as fine, medium, or coarse. Density is a separate measure of how many hairs grow per square centimetre of scalp. A client can be fine textured yet high density, which affects how much bulk to remove versus how long a chemical needs to process. Fine strands process faster, which explains her colour timing.
  24. The correct answer is C (Decline to comment on another stylist's client work outside of a proper consultation.): Declining to comment on another stylist's client work outside of a real consultation respects both client privacy and professional courtesy toward a colleague. Giving a detailed critique based only on a casual photo ignores that the stylist does not know the client's history or what was actually discussed. Sharing the photo further would compound a privacy concern involving someone who is not even the current stylist's own client. Agreeing with a negative comment without context is also unprofessional.
  25. The correct answer is A (Fine, hand-knotted lace appears only along the front hairline edge.): A lace front cap uses a strip of fine, hand-knotted lace only along the front hairline, creating an undetectable edge that can be styled away from the face. A full lace cap extends that same hand-tied lace construction across the entire base of the wig. Because it uses far less lace, a lace front cap is generally more durable and less expensive than full lace.
  26. The correct answer is D (Address the scalp condition and reconsider proceeding with the service today.): Cuts or visible irritation on the scalp are a real safety concern for an oxidative colour service, since the chemical formula can cause additional discomfort or complications on compromised skin. A responsible stylist addresses this finding and reconsiders whether to proceed with the service that day. Checking scalp condition is a standard part of pre-service consultation, not an optional step.
  27. The correct answer is A (Thinning shears used to blend the two lengths together.): Thinning shears are used to blend the transition between two different lengths so the line does not read as a hard step. A dry razor risks damaging the hair, and a short clipper guard would remove far too much length. A comb alone cannot change the shape of the existing cut.
  28. The correct answer is D (That alkaline residue has been addressed and the hair's pH is reasonably stabilized.): The final check after neutralizing shampoo is meant to confirm that alkaline residue has been properly addressed and the hair's pH is reasonably stabilized, which is the specific job of the rinse and neutralizing shampoo steps. A relaxer service does not change natural hair colour, so that is not what this check is for. The service is meant to remove curl, not restore it, so regaining the original curl pattern is not a goal being checked here, and trimming is a separate service unrelated to this chemical check.
  29. The correct answer is B (Removing clips while the wave is wet will collapse the ridge.): Each completed wave is secured with clips while the setting lotion dries, and the clips are only removed once the hair has fully set. Disturbing a wet wave before it dries will collapse the ridge. This keeps the molded S-shape intact until the set is finished.
  30. The correct answer is B (Ask where the salon's safety data sheet binder or file is kept and review it.): Employers must keep safety data sheets accessible to every worker, and a stylist unfamiliar with a product should locate and review the relevant sheet before first use rather than relying on memory or guesswork. The supplier label provides a summary, but the full data sheet contains the detailed handling, storage, and first aid information a stylist needs. Waiting until a problem occurs defeats the purpose of reviewing this information in advance. Confirming the correct handling steps beforehand is the responsible approach to a new product.
  31. The correct answer is D (The strand stretches about half its length beyond and springs back.): Wet hair in good condition stretches about 40 to 50 percent beyond its original length and still returns. Dry hair stretches only a modest amount. Hair that breaks with little stretch or fails to spring back has weakened internal bonds. That finding points to over-processing and poor candidacy for further chemical services.
  32. The correct answer is C (Each province and territory through its own designated apprenticeship authority.): Apprenticeship training and trade certification in Canada are regulated provincially and territorially rather than federally. Each province and territory has its own apprenticeship authority that registers apprentices, tracks required hours, and administers the Certificate of Qualification exam. The federal government supports the Red Seal Program but does not itself issue provincial certification. Salon owners and municipalities have no authority to set or grant trade certification.
  33. The correct answer is D (Red and red orange, then orange and gold, then pale yellow at the top.): Lightened hair passes through a predictable sequence of underlying pigments as it lifts, moving from red and red orange at the lower levels, through orange and gold, into pale yellow at the highest levels. Knowing this sequence lets the stylist choose the right toner for the warmth actually present. Reading the hair rather than following a remembered formula is the core corrective skill.
  34. The correct answer is D (Graduation and layering share the same underlying technique, elevation and a traveling guideline.): Graduation and layering are both built using elevation and a traveling guideline, which is exactly why students frequently confuse the two and why recognizing which one a described pattern produces is a genuine applied skill rather than rote memorization. The two techniques are not unrelated, since they share this underlying method. The distinction does have real bearing on the finished silhouette, from a defined perimeter to fully textured movement.
  35. The correct answer is B (Check the coloured lengths and new growth separately, since porosity likely differs between them.): Previously coloured hair generally carries different porosity than the newer, unpigmented growth even after several weeks, so checking each section separately during processing gives the stylist real evidence of how each part is actually responding. Treating the whole head as virgin hair ignores the lasting effect colour treatment can have on porosity. There is no fixed rule that new growth always processes slower than treated lengths, and colour history from two months earlier remains relevant to how the hair responds to a new chemical service.
  36. The correct answer is A (Stop at the lowest effective temperature rather than raising the heat further.): When in doubt, the stylist should start at the lowest temperature that will realistically achieve the result and raise it only if needed, rather than starting high. Since the hair already straightened well on the first pass at a moderate setting, the result is achieved without additional heat. Raising the temperature or running extra passes adds thermal stress with no benefit to the outcome.
  37. The correct answer is C (Sanitation removes visible debris first, then disinfection destroys the remaining microorganisms on the surface.): Sanitation physically removes hair, skin cells, and product residue from a tool's surface before any chemical disinfectant is applied. Organic debris left behind can shield microorganisms from the disinfectant and prevent it from working as intended. Disinfection follows sanitation and destroys the bacteria, fungi, and viruses that remain on the cleaned surface. Performing the steps in this order is required before an implement can be considered ready for reuse.
  38. The correct answer is D (Disulfide bonds, the strong sulfur links between cysteine units that reform in a new shape.): A permanent wave or chemical relaxer uses a reducing agent to break disulfide bonds and an oxidizing neutralizer to reform them in a new shape. Hydrogen and salt bonds are physical and break and reform with moisture and heat, so a blown-dry set relaxes in humidity. A style that survives shampooing indicates the stronger disulfide bonds were altered. A set that falls in humidity points only to hydrogen bonds.
  39. The correct answer is A (Block the realistic full service time rather than estimating a shorter slot.): Blocking the realistic time a complex service takes is the most reliable way to protect the rest of the day's schedule from running behind. Treating a lengthy colour service like a basic haircut slot guarantees the appointment will run over. Asking a client to arrive early does not change how long the actual service takes to perform properly. Skipping the consultation removes a step that helps prevent a result the client is unhappy with, which risks a bigger delay later.
  40. The correct answer is B (A demi-permanent colour is the better recommendation for this specific request.): Demi-permanent colour, typically mixed with a low volume developer, deposits richer and longer lasting tone and handles grey blending well without producing the same obvious root regrowth pattern as full permanent colour. This makes it a strong middle option for a client who wants more than semi-permanent but is not ready for permanent commitment. Matching the category to the client's stated priorities is the core of this decision.
  41. The correct answer is C (Both techniques can be combined within the same haircut.): Graduation and layering can be combined within the same haircut, for example graduation through the back combined with layering through the top and crown, since each technique can be applied to different areas as needed. It is not true that the two can never coexist in one service. Neither technique is required to be applied across the entire head every time it is used.
  42. The correct answer is C (Store it in its original, clearly labelled, closed container in a cool area away from light.): Correct storage practice keeps a chemical product in its original, clearly labelled, closed container in a cool area away from direct light, which protects both the product's stability and anyone who might handle it later. Leaving a container uncapped increases fume exposure and spill risk unnecessarily. Transferring product into an unlabelled bottle removes the ability to quickly check timing, concentration, or first aid information. Storing a chemical product under direct heat works against the recommended cool, shaded storage most chemical texture products call for.
  43. The correct answer is D (Perm rods, whose rigid ridged construction gives a firmer, more defined curl.): Perm rods come in the same range of diameters as rollers but have a more rigid, ridged construction, which is why a wet rod set produces clearly separated, springy curls more reliably than a standard roller set. Rollers produce a larger, softer curl depending on their diameter. When the client wants a firm, defined set, perm rods are the better wet setting choice.
  44. The correct answer is C (Report the concern to a supervisor, and an investigation follows if it is not resolved.): The right to refuse unsafe work comes with a defined process: the worker reports the concern to a supervisor, the employer investigates, and if the concern is not resolved a government inspector may be called in to make a final determination. A worker cannot simply walk off the job indefinitely without following this process, and continuing the task while mailing a complaint does not address the immediate safety concern. Quietly having a coworker take over does not resolve the underlying hazard either. Following the reporting and investigation process is what the law actually requires.
  45. The correct answer is A (Begin at the front hairline and work backward toward the crown and nape.): Effective massage movement typically begins at the front hairline and works backward toward the crown and nape, covering the whole scalp systematically. This direction follows the natural sweep of the scalp and makes sure the entire area is treated. Starting at the nape, at the crown, or in a random order can leave areas missed. The sequence is part of a thorough, consistent service.
  46. The correct answer is B (The service is likely to run over time and delay every later appointment.): Booking a complex corrective colour service into a slot sized for a basic trim almost guarantees the appointment will run long and push every later booking behind schedule. Pricing is set by the service actually performed, not by whichever slot length was mistakenly used for booking. There is no legal requirement to cancel the rest of the day over a scheduling error. Retail sales are not directly tied to this particular scheduling mismatch.
  47. The correct answer is B (A demi-permanent or low volume permanent formula with minimal lift is appropriate.): A subtle, one level shift with soft warmth is well suited to a demi-permanent or low volume permanent formula, since only a small amount of lift is needed. Reaching for a high lift or double process service would be an unnecessarily aggressive approach for this modest goal. Matching the service intensity to the requested change is basic professional judgment.
  48. The correct answer is B (Water temporarily elongates the curl pattern, hiding the true resting shape.): Water temporarily relaxes and elongates the curl pattern, so cutting on wet hair can hide how the hair will actually sit once it dries and the curl springs back. Cutting dry lets the stylist see the true curl pattern and plan the cut around actual resting length. This is why dry cutting is common practice on curly and coily textures.
  49. The correct answer is C (Uneven processing and a poorly defined curl at the ends of each section.): Checking that ends are tucked in rather than folded is meant to prevent uneven processing, since folded ends do not sit flat against the rod and the solution cannot reshape them consistently, which typically shows up as a weaker or poorly defined curl at the ends. This check has nothing to do with scalp absorption during neutralizing, rod visibility after styling, or the hair's natural colour. A stylist who checks the ends before applying solution is protecting the finished result from the weak, poorly defined curl that folded ends typically produce.
  50. The correct answer is A (It coats the outside of the shaft, sealing the cuticle and filling surface irregularities.): Silicones coat the outside of the hair shaft, sealing down the cuticle and filling in surface irregularities, which reduces frizz, adds shine, and lowers porosity. They do not penetrate into the cortex, so the effect is a surface smoothing rather than a repair. Because most of these silicones are not water soluble, regular gentle shampooing may not be enough to remove a build-up.
  51. The correct answer is A (Treat the bottle as an unknown hazard and report it rather than using its contents.): An unlabeled container should always be treated as an unknown hazard rather than assumed to be harmless, since WHMIS requires every hazardous product, including those in secondary containers, to carry a proper workplace label. Smelling an unknown chemical, using it on a client, or disposing of it without confirming its contents all introduce unnecessary risk. Reporting the container so it can be identified or safely discarded is the correct and cautious response. This protects both the apprentice and any client whose service might otherwise involve an unverified product.
  52. The correct answer is D (Protein overload, so the treatment should pause and moisture should be added.): Too much protein on hair that is already protein-rich and moisture-poor can leave it stiff and prone to snapping, which is protein overload. The treatment should be paused and the hair given moisture. Coarse, dry hair often needs moisture more than additional protein. This shows why the diagnosis, not the product label, guides conditioning.
  53. The correct answer is B (Restricting system access with passwords to staff who need it for service.): Restricting digital access with passwords to staff who actually need the information for service delivery is a core practice for protecting client privacy. Leaving printed files at an open front desk or sharing login credentials freely defeats the purpose of moving to a secured system. Digital software is fully capable of storing health-related notes securely, so removing them is unnecessary and would reduce the quality of care. Access should be limited, not the recordkeeping itself.
  54. This uneven response means the stylist should watch the strand closely to avoid overprocessing the ends while the roots continue lifting. Recognizing this pattern early protects hair integrity during the service.
  55. The correct answer is D (Sectioning, which organizes the head before cutting starts.): Sectioning is the process of dividing the head into manageable subsections before cutting begins, done with a cutting comb, sectioning clips, and clean partings. Elevation, graduation, and layering are separate concepts that describe angle and resulting shape, not the initial organization step. Sectioning keeps hair under control and lets the stylist work systematically.

Growth rate does not explain localized breakage at a specific point. This pattern is directly tied to chemical service history rather than unrelated to it, and scalp oil production is not an established cause of breakage concentrated at a growth line.
  57. The correct answer is D (The irregular shaft makes sebum travel a harder path down the length.): Because the hair shaft on curly and coily textures is irregular rather than round, natural sebum produced at the scalp has a harder path to travel down the length of the hair. This leaves curly and coily hair naturally drier than straighter textures. Moisture-focused products are therefore a core part of styling these textures.
  58. The correct response is to set the comb aside and take it through the full sanitation and disinfection process before it touches a client's hair or scalp again. Continuing to use it based on a quick visual check skips a required safety step. A spare, already disinfected tool should be used to continue the current service instead.

The scalp is where oil and buildup actually accumulate, so it needs the cleansing first. Nails can scratch a sensitive scalp and cause irritation. The pads of the fingers provide even pressure without scraping.

There is no requirement for the apprentice to pay the employer a registration fee for this approval. A salon does not need to already employ another apprentice to sponsor a new one. The federal government does not conduct this kind of direct employer inspection, since apprenticeship sponsorship is handled provincially.

Foil packets allow more than one formula in the same head and let each packet be checked and removed independently. The cap struggles with this kind of bold, multi formula placement.

Keeping the blade angle shallow controls how much is removed with each pass. This is why a low blade angle is the standard technique for controlled razor work.

Ventilation needs are tied to what a specific formula actually contains rather than being identical regardless of ingredients. Ventilation remains relevant beyond just initial mixing, since heating can release additional fumes later in the service. A flat iron on its own, without a chemical product on the hair, does not generate this kind of hazardous vapour.

There is no single fixed national hour count that applies identically everywhere, since this specific requirement is set provincially. Personal judgment about readiness, or a personal client count, does not substitute for meeting the province's actual training hour requirement. Understanding the home province's specific requirement is what actually determines exam eligibility.

Heat and time allow more of the treatment to reach the inner structure. This is for hair needing more intensive repair than a daily product offers. It does not seal the surface alone or change disulfide bonds.

The stylist does not personally calculate and remit these deductions themselves under this arrangement. Both programs do generally apply to employed workers in the trade, contrary to a blanket exemption. Whether a stylist also sells retail product has no bearing on payroll withholding.

Checking the site immediately would miss a reaction that develops more slowly. This waiting period is a standard safety requirement across the trade.

Cutting line direction and wet versus dry cutting are separate variables from this specific perimeter-connection check.

Dismissing these records as useless ignores their whole purpose. Their value goes well beyond billing, extending directly into chemical planning and client safety. Their usefulness does not depend on the new stylist personally recognizing the client's hair, since the written record itself carries the needed information regardless of familiarity.

This distributes the product evenly and encourages the curl strands within that section to clump into a defined shape. Rubbing vigorously or scrunching roughly tends to disturb the pattern and increase frizz.

Adjusting speed settings does not replace the actual maintenance step the blades need, and switching brands mid-shift does not address the underlying lubrication gap. Allowing clippings to build up is the opposite of good practice, since blades should be brushed free of clippings after every use. Regular oiling throughout a demanding shift is the correct preventive habit here.

Inspectors are specifically concerned with client and worker safety from a sanitation standpoint. Preparing for inspection means reviewing cleaning logs and disposal procedures, not financial or marketing details.

This is a common, deliberate formulation choice rather than an accident. Skipping the warm base risks a visibly mismatched result between grey and pigmented sections.
  76. This produces a haircut where the perimeter length stays consistent, such as a classic one-length bob. A traveling guideline, by contrast, moves forward with the stylist rather than staying fixed.
  77. A slightly duller shine is a much less specific and less serious observation on its own. A lingering faint smell after thorough rinsing is not established as a reliable sign of overprocessing. A minor colour shift is not a recognized indicator of overprocessed hair either.
  78. The correct answer is A (A setting lotion or styling gel applied to damp hair before combing.): In finger waving the stylist applies a setting lotion or styling gel to damp hair, then uses a fine-tooth comb and the fingers of the free hand to direct the hair in opposing directions. Each ridge is shaped by pressing the index and middle finger against the scalp on either side of the section while combing in the opposite direction. The shaped waves are secured with clips until the lotion dries, because disturbing a wet wave before it sets collapses the ridge.
  79. The correct answer is A (It should not change the consistent infection control precautions already used for every client.): Standard precautions are applied consistently to every client rather than selectively based on how well or unwell a client appears, because appearance is not a reliable indicator of bloodborne infection status. Scaling precautions up only for clients who look sick, or down for clients seeking a non chemical service, misunderstands the actual basis for the protocol. The correct approach uses the same infection control standard for this appointment as for any other. Consistency, not personal discretion based on appearance, is what the standard requires.
  80. The correct answer is D (Keratin, and the disulfide bond formed between neighbouring cysteine units.): Hair is made mostly of keratin, a fibrous protein. Keratin is rich in cysteine, an amino acid that contains sulfur. When two cysteine units on neighbouring chains sit close together they form a disulfide bond. These bonds give hair its natural shape and its resistance to stretching and breaking.
  81. Hair condition and product strength can both change between visits, which is exactly why testing exists for each relevant service. Skipping the test because a past service was tolerated ignores that risk changes with formula strength. A verbal confirmation from the client does not substitute for an actual test.

This is a normal characteristic of resistant grey hair rather than a sign of a problem with the formula. Recognizing this pattern helps the stylist plan adequate processing time and monitor coverage carefully.

Leaving the record unchanged loses information that could matter for a future service using the same or a similar product. A verbal mention alone does not create a lasting record another stylist could later reference. Documenting significant events like this should not depend on whether the client happens to request it.

Higher porosity hair would need the opposite adjustment and reduced strength.

Because there is no underlying colour left to blend with, a grey coverage formula must supply its own full deposit. This is why grey coverage formulas typically include a strong warm base tone.

Skipping the step for coarse hair, or tripling the timing for fine hair, are not standard, texture-based adjustments. This reduces the mechanical stress that causes breakage on these textures. It is the first moisture step before heavier curl products are added.
  93. It is not simply a way to fill time before the real work begins, and pricing or staff assignment are separate matters unrelated to its core purpose. This is why the consultation covers texture, density, scalp condition, and prior chemical history rather than only the client's stated preference.   94. The correct answer is C (The cortex, which stores pigment and is altered by permanent chemical services.): The cortex is the middle layer that stores the melanin granules giving hair its natural colour. It is also the layer that permanent colour, relaxing, and permanent waving alter to change structure. This is why timing, strength, and application matter so much with those services. A toner acts more superficially on the cuticle and the outer shaft.

Immediately blaming the client's expectations is defensive and tends to escalate rather than resolve the situation. Offering a refund before understanding the issue skips the chance to correct the actual result, which is usually the better outcome for both the client and the salon's reputation. Telling the client to simply wait months does not address a same-week concern.

  97. Layering or graduation would break up that density and add movement the client did not request. A blunt one-length cut is the correct technique for this outcome.
  98. The correct answer is C (Start timing the full neutralizing period again once the last section is saturated.): Every section needs the full manufacturer-specified contact time with neutralizer for its disulfide bonds to properly reform, so the timing clock should restart once the last section has been fully saturated with fresh solution. Keeping the original timer running through the interruption would shortchange the sections saturated later. Cutting time short or skipping neutralizer on any section leaves those bonds unreformed, which produces a weak or dropped curl in exactly that area.
  99. The correct answer is A (The hydrogen bonds inside the hair shaft, allowing it to reform in a new shape.): Flat irons and curling irons work by using heat to temporarily break and reform the hydrogen bonds inside the hair shaft, which is what allows the hair to be reshaped into a smooth line or a curl. Disulfide bonds are not broken by a thermal tool alone, and pigment is unrelated to shape. The change is temporary, not a lasting restructuring of the protein.
  100. The correct answer is B (Running multiple heat producing tools from one outlet strip raises the risk of an overloaded circuit.): Overloaded outlets running multiple heat styling tools are one of the specific electrical hazards salon fire safety practice addresses, and combining a space heater with two blow dryers and a flat iron on one outlet strip fits this exact concern. Switching individual tools off between uses does not address the risk created when several heat producing devices share the same circuit throughout the day. Outlet strips have real capacity limits and are not designed to safely handle an unlimited number of heat tools at once. The concern applies broadly to this combination of devices, not only when every tool happens to be running at its highest setting simultaneously.
  101. The correct answer is C (Hydrogen bonds, which break and reform temporarily with moisture and heat.): A wet set and a blow-dry style depend on hydrogen bonds, which break when hair is wet and reform as it dries. These are physical bonds that moisture and heat alone can change. When humidity returns, those bonds release and the style falls out. Disulfide bonds are stronger and require a chemical reducing agent to break and a neutralizer to reform.

A signed waiver does not substitute for this safety check. Same-day testing right before the service does not allow enough time for a delayed reaction to show. It generally provides light to medium hold with added volume and lift, particularly when applied at the root. It does not weigh the hair down the way a heavier cream or gel might.

It is not true that elevation has no effect, or that the relationship runs in the opposite direction. Guideline choice and elevation work together, but elevation specifically governs this weight-distribution principle.

Seasonal allergies do not rule out the need for this test, nor do they make performing the test dangerous. Having an unrelated allergy history does not predict how the skin will respond to a specific chemical product, so testing remains the standard step regardless.
  117. The correct answer is D (The diffuser, spreading the airflow across a wide low-velocity area and letting curls clump.): The diffuser spreads the airflow over a wide, low velocity area rather than forcing it through a narrow nozzle. This lets the curls dry in their natural clumped formation, which reduces frizz and preserves the pattern. A direct high velocity nozzle would separate the curl clumps and roughen the cuticle. A vent brush and paddle brush cannot deliver the same clump-friendly result.
  118. The correct answer is C (Remove the gloves used for the bleeding before handling unrelated tasks like sweeping.): Gloves used to manage a blood exposure should be removed before moving on to unrelated tasks, since continuing to wear them risks spreading contamination to other surfaces and tools. Rinsing them briefly or turning them inside out does not make them safe to continue using across different tasks. Wearing the same gloves through every subsequent activity treats a single use protective barrier as though it were reusable equipment. Removing and properly disposing of the gloves, then applying a fresh pair as needed, is the correct sequence.
  119. The correct answer is D (The catagen phase, which is a short transitional stage lasting a few weeks.): The catagen phase is the brief transitional phase after active growth stops. The bulb detaches from its blood supply and the follicle begins to shrink. Only a small percentage of scalp hair is in catagen at any given time. It sits between the longer anagen growth phase and the telogen resting phase.
  120. The correct answer is B (The underlying warm pigment is gradually exposed as melanin breaks down.): The law of colour states that lightening exposes the natural underlying pigment already present in the hair rather than creating new warmth. As melanin is broken down, this warmth becomes progressively visible in a predictable sequence. Understanding this sequence lets a stylist anticipate what a toner will need to neutralize.
  121. The correct answer is A (The exact angle at which the strand is held for cutting.): The cutting comb does more than section hair, since its teeth also distribute hair evenly before a subsection is cut and control the exact angle at which the strand is held. Colour tone and shear brand choice are unrelated to what the comb controls in this moment. Whether the guideline is stationary or traveling is a separate planning decision made before sectioning begins.
  122. The correct answer is C (The dates and results of any predisposition or strand tests performed for this visit.): A complete record includes the dates and results of any predisposition or strand tests performed, since this makes the 24 to 48 hour testing window easier to prove was followed and gives a clear picture of the client's chemical history over time. Testing details are directly relevant to the client's ongoing hair safety record, not a separate, unrelated system. Testing information from a first visit does not cover a new formula or product introduced later, so it should be updated as relevant tests occur. A verbal confirmation of feeling fine is not a substitute for documenting the actual test results themselves.
  123. The correct answer is D (Hold the brush down and close to the head to flatten the root.): Directing the brush down and close to the head flattens the root and produces a smoother silhouette. Lifting the brush up and away from the scalp while drying the base builds volume instead. The client asked for no lift at the roots, so the down and close angle is the right choice.
  124. The correct answer is B (The Workplace Hazardous Materials Information System, known as WHMIS, in its current 2015 version.): WHMIS is the Canadian framework for communicating the hazards of chemical products used on the job, and its current version, WHMIS 2015, aligns with the internationally used Globally Harmonized System. Any salon that stocks hazardous products such as bleach or permanent wave solution is a WHMIS regulated workplace. Employment insurance, pension contributions, and minimum wage rules are unrelated employment matters governed by entirely different legislation. WHMIS specifically covers hazard labels, safety data sheets, and the training a worker must receive before handling these products.
  125. The correct answer is A (The medulla, a loose core that is absent from some finer hair.): The medulla is a loose, sometimes discontinuous core of cells at the centre of some strands. It is not present in all hair, particularly finer types. It plays little to no role in salon services. The cortex and cuticle are the layers that actually matter for styling and chemistry.

Practice Test 5 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (Porous hair absorbs colour unevenly and grabs too fast at the damaged points.): Porous hair absorbs colour unevenly and often too fast, grabbing colour heavily at the most damaged points and releasing it quickly. Skipping the filler step is a common cause of a corrective service turning out patchy or fading unevenly within days, because the target colour has no consistent surface to grab onto. Filling first, then applying the shade over an evened out base, is the standard corrective sequence.
  2. The correct answer is C (The angle at which the section or comb is elevated controls the resulting length.): Elevation angle, whether it is the angle of a shear-cut section or the angle of the comb in clipper-over-comb work, controls how much length is left behind and therefore how graduated the result looks. This shared principle is why clipper-over-comb is described as working the same way finger elevation does in shear cutting. Recognizing this connection helps a stylist move fluidly between both tools within one haircut.
  3. The correct answer is A (A texture change, since new growth typically feels different from chemically straightened hair.): The most reliable way to locate the growth line is to feel for a texture change, since new, unprocessed growth typically has a different feel from hair that has already been chemically straightened. Length alone is not a reliable indicator, since growth rates and prior trims vary. Chemical relaxer does not reliably lighten hair colour, so colour is not the right marker either. Scalp temperature is not an established way to distinguish treated from untreated hair sections.
  4. The correct answer is B (Cross the outer strands alternately over the centre strand.): The three strand braid divides the hair into three even sections, and the outer strands are alternately crossed over the centre strand, right over centre, then left over centre, repeating down the length. This is the foundation of every other braid in the chapter. A Dutch braid crosses the strands underneath instead.
  5. The correct answer is C (Flush the eye continuously with clean, lukewarm water for a full fifteen minutes.): Chemical splash to the eye has a specific, well established response: flush the eye continuously with clean, lukewarm water for a full fifteen minutes, holding the eyelid open so the flow reaches the whole eye. A brief ten second flush does not remove chemical still present on the eye's surface, and covering the eye without flushing first leaves the chemical in place. Implement disinfectant is designed for tools, not skin or eyes, and must never be used on a person. Medical attention should be sought regardless of how the eye looks after the full flush is complete.
  6. The correct answer is D (The porous, over-processed ends are the priority, so the colour formula should be gentler on them.): High porosity hair has a lifted, chemically worn cuticle that absorbs colour quickly and unevenly and can grab too dark, so over-processed ends need a gentler approach. The dry scalp is a product-selection consideration but not the primary risk for the colour result. Density and elasticity are relevant but porosity drives the penetration risk here. Adjusting strength and timing to the ends protects the service outcome.
  7. The correct answer is C (Perform the test again, since sensitivity can develop even after prior tolerance.): Even a client with a history of safe colour services should be retested before each new service, since a person can develop a new sensitivity to an ingredient they previously tolerated without issue. Relying on past results alone does not guarantee safety going forward. Repeating the test consistently protects the client regardless of their history.
  8. The correct answer is B (An inconsistent comb angle or clipper pressure during that part of the section.): A change in the comb's angle or in how firmly the clippers press against it during the pass produces an inconsistent cutting line that shows up as a visible step or ridge. Maintaining steady angle and pressure throughout each pass prevents this. Correcting an existing step requires blending the area with a shorter, even pass.
  9. The correct answer is A (That section likely has different porosity or texture than the rest of the head.): Porosity and texture can vary across a single head, and a section that processes visibly faster than the rest despite even application most likely has different porosity or texture than the surrounding hair. There is no rule that the nape always processes fastest for every client, since this varies by individual. Uneven processing between sections is a real and expected possibility given how porosity and texture can differ across one head, and there is no indication here that rod brand was actually different.
  10. The correct answer is D (Bubble hair, caused by water turning to steam inside the shaft.): Bubble hair forms when water inside the shaft is heated so quickly that it turns to steam and creates small bubbles in the hair structure. These bubbles leave weak points that break easily. The condition reflects rapid, cumulative thermal exposure rather than a single event.
  11. The correct answer is B (Regular fire drills keep evacuation knowledge usable under the real pressure of an actual emergency.): Regular participation in fire drills, where a salon runs them, keeps evacuation knowledge usable under real pressure rather than remaining purely theoretical, and this value applies even to experienced staff. Assuming familiarity with the route removes any benefit from practising it overlooks how differently people can respond under the stress of an actual emergency. Drills are useful for the whole team, not only new staff members encountering the information for the first time. Participating consistently helps ensure the whole salon responds effectively if a real fire occurs.
  12. The correct answer is C (Sebum coats the hair and skin surface to keep them lubricated and protected.): Sebum is an oily secretion from the sebaceous glands that coats the hair shaft and skin surface, keeping them lubricated. Output varies with hormones, genetics, and general health, so some clients look oily soon after shampooing while others feel tight and dry. Surfactants in shampoo trap and rinse away sebum and buildup, which is the job of the cleansing step. Dryness that feels tight may reflect low sebum production rather than a skin condition.
  13. The correct answer is A (Whether the hair lifted high enough to support the intended tone.): Brassiness is the underlying warm pigment showing through a colour that did not fully neutralize it. The stylist has to know whether the hair lifted enough to support the intended tone, because toner deposits colour and does not lift. If the hair never reached the right level, no toner will turn it into a true ash.
  14. The correct answer is A (The perimeter is shortened gradually but stops well short of bare skin.): A taper uses the same graduated guard principle as a fade but stops short of bare skin, shortening the perimeter gradually while keeping some length throughout. This produces the more conservative look the client is asking for compared with a skin fade. Understanding this distinction guides which guard sequence and blending depth to use.
  15. The correct answer is D (Coarse hair has a thicker cortex, giving the solution more protein structure to soften.): Coarse hair has a thicker cortex than fine hair, which means there is more protein structure for the reducing solution to reach and soften, so it generally takes longer to process. Porosity is a separate property from coarseness and is not automatically higher in coarse hair. Coarse hair is not equally resistant to every chemical product, and disulfide bond density relates to protein structure rather than being simply higher in fine hair than coarse hair.
  16. The correct answer is C (Use the lowest temperature range that will still give a smooth result.): Fine, colour-treated hair has already lightened or processed structure and is more porous, so it tolerates the lowest range of thermal styling heat. The stylist should start at the lowest temperature that will realistically achieve the result and raise it only if needed. Working in clean sections and moving smoothly in one pass avoids multiplying damage from repeated passes.
  17. The correct answer is B (Maintaining client confidentiality, which is part of the trade's professional conduct expectations.): Maintaining client confidentiality is part of the professional conduct expectations that flow from workplace norms in this trade, and sharing personal details from one client's conversation with another client is a clear breach of that expectation. This is not simply normal salon conversation, since it involves passing along another person's private information without their knowledge. Confidentiality expectations are not limited only to chemical or medical history, and withholding a name does not resolve the underlying issue if enough identifying detail is shared. Respecting what a client shares during a service is part of professional conduct.
  18. The correct answer is B (A raised cuticle allows faster penetration, while a tight cuticle slows how fast colour enters.): The cuticle controls how easily moisture and chemicals move into the hair shaft, since a raised cuticle allows faster penetration and a tight cuticle slows it down. This is why colour speed varies between clients even with the same formula. Cortex thickness and medulla content are not the main controls on absorption rate.
