Reading the cuticle before a service guides timing and strength.
  19. The correct answer is C (Asking about henna, metallic dye, and any recent chemical texture service.): Asking about henna and metallic dye history, along with any recent chemical texture service, is a safety step, not an optional courtesy. Skipping it risks breakage, scalp injury, and a service that cannot be corrected on the spot. A client who says her hair was just coloured normally still needs to be asked.
  20. The correct answer is D (Graduation, generally built at lower to moderate elevation up to about ninety degrees.): Graduation is generally built at lower to moderate elevation, commonly up to roughly ninety degrees, and still reads as a solid weight line even though it is not perfectly blunt, matching the rounded, beveled description. Layering at higher elevation instead removes that connected weight line. A traveling guideline and a vertical cutting line are tools used within either technique, not a substitute for controlling elevation.
  21. The correct answer is D (To check how the hair actually responds to the chosen solution, rod, and timing.): A pre-perm test curl checks how a specific client's hair actually responds to the chosen solution, rod size, and timing before the whole head is committed to the process, which lets the stylist estimate realistic processing time and confirm the hair can tolerate the chosen chemical strength. It is not a tool for determining natural hair colour or measuring the proportion of grey hair. It also does not test scalp sensitivity to shampoo, which is a separate concern from how the test curl functions.
  22. The correct answer is B (The hair shows cumulative thermal damage and the heat setting should be lowered.): A loss of elasticity and snapping ends are signs of cumulative thermal damage even on coarse, virgin hair, which can normally take a higher heat range. When this damage is present, the stylist should lower the setting and add recovery steps rather than keep the same routine. A clarifying shampoo would not repair the loss of elasticity, and the damage outweighs the hair type advantage.
  23. The correct answer is D (Scalp condition is included because it can affect the safety of a chemical service.): A visual and physical assessment includes scalp condition alongside hair texture, density, and growth pattern, because scalp condition can affect whether a chemical service is safe to perform. Assuming scalp condition is irrelevant to texture services specifically, or that it only matters for colour, overlooks how broadly this part of the consultation applies. Texture and density alone do not provide the complete picture a stylist needs before proceeding. Including scalp condition in the assessment protects the client from an avoidable complication.
  24. The correct answer is B (This is androgenetic alopecia, a genetic, hormone-related pattern that progresses slowly and not from styling.): Androgenetic alopecia is the most common form of hair loss, a genetically influenced, hormone-related pattern of gradual thinning. In men it typically begins at the temples and crown, and it progresses slowly without being contagious or caused by salon work. Alopecia areata is autoimmune with sudden round patches, and traction alopecia follows repeated tension from tight styles. Reassuring the client and noting the pattern is appropriate.
  25. The correct answer is D (Developer strength, lightener formula strength, and processing time together set the lift.): Lift is produced by the reaction between the lightener and developer, not by the powder alone. Developer strength supplies the oxygen that drives the reaction, while the formula strength and processing time add to the effect. These three together determine how many levels of lift the hair reaches.
  26. The correct answer is C (A graduated bob with a stacked perimeter and a shorter nape area.): The stacked perimeter in a graduated bob gives an illusion of thickness even on fine hair, and the shorter nape is easier for the client to maintain between visits. A one-length cut at maximum length would not create the same stacked density effect. Uniform layers distribute volume differently and do not build the same nape stack.
  27. The correct answer is B (Stop and check the product's freshness and storage before applying it to the client.): An unusual consistency or weaker odour can signal that a product has degraded, been stored improperly, or is nearing the end of its shelf life, all of which affect how reliably it will process the hair. The safe response is to verify the product before it touches the client's hair rather than guess at a fix. Simply applying it, extending time blindly, or swapping to an unrelated stronger product all risk an unpredictable or unsafe result instead of addressing the real concern.
  28. The correct answer is A (More volume or the style worn closer to the shoulders around the open line.): An open or strapless neckline can support more volume or a style worn closer to the shoulders, which is why a fuller placement works with that line. A high, structured neckline would instead benefit from a style that keeps the neck and shoulders visible. The placement should complement the openness of the gown rather than compete with it.
  29. The correct answer is A (Decline to use the product until its identity, hazards, and handling information can be confirmed.): A product without a supplier label or an available safety data sheet cannot be safely evaluated for its hazards, correct handling, or first aid response, regardless of how eager the client is to proceed. Client ownership of the product does not satisfy the salon's WHMIS obligations, since the salon is still responsible for how it is used in the workplace. A brief skin test does not substitute for reviewing documented hazard information before use. Declining until the product can be properly identified and documented is the responsible course of action, even if it means a delay or a different plan for the service.
  30. The correct answer is D (Use the pads of the fingers with firm, comfortable pressure in circular glides.): The pads of the fingers, not the nails, should apply firm but comfortable pressure in slow circular or gliding movements. The goal is to move the scalp over the underlying skull rather than sliding over hair. Movement should cover the whole scalp systematically. This technique gives the relaxing and circulatory benefit without irritation.
  31. The correct answer is B (A blue-based toner, because blue sits opposite orange on the wheel.): Blue sits opposite orange on the colour wheel, so a blue-based toner neutralizes the orange cast. Orange is the residual warmth typically seen at the lower to mid lightening levels. Applying a violet toner there would not fully neutralize it, leaving a muddy tone.
  32. The correct answer is D (A horizontal cutting line, used for even, blunt shapes.): A horizontal cutting line runs parallel to the floor and is used to build even, blunt shapes such as a one-length bob or a straight-across fringe. Diagonal forward, diagonal back, and vertical cutting lines each run at a different angle for different purposes. Horizontal keeps weight distributed evenly across the width of the section.
  33. The correct answer is A (That section will process unevenly and may show a weaker, less defined curl.): When hair ends fold over rather than sitting flat against the rod, the solution cannot reach and reshape that section evenly, which typically produces a weaker or poorly defined curl at the ends once the hair is dry. Neutralizer does not correct a wrapping error, since it only locks in whatever shape the hair happens to be in. Ends matter to the finished result just as much as the mid-lengths, and a wrapping problem does not change how much solution reaches the scalp.
  34. The correct answer is C (A foam, an aerated liquid that distributes lightly and evenly through the hair.): Mousse is a foam, a liquid formula that has been aerated so it distributes lightly and evenly through the hair. It generally provides light to medium hold with added volume and lift, particularly when applied at the root, without weighing hair down. A gel would dry into a firm coating, and a cream or serum has a different weight and purpose.
  35. The correct answer is C (Aerosol products carry a flame pictogram and should be stored away from heat sources.): Aerosol products like hairspray commonly carry the flame pictogram under WHMIS 2015, indicating they should be kept away from heat sources due to fire risk. A retail product is not exempt from these hazard communication requirements simply because clients may purchase it, and it is not the corrosion pictogram that applies here. Placing an aerosol display beside a heater is the opposite of the correct storage practice for a flammable product. Moving the display away from the heat source addresses the concern directly.
  36. The correct answer is C (Alopecia areata, an autoimmune condition with sudden patches and an unpredictable course.): Alopecia areata is an autoimmune condition that brings sudden, well-defined round patches of loss. Its course is unpredictable and it is not caused by styling or tension. No styling cause explains this pattern, which separates it from traction alopecia. Referral for a medical opinion is appropriate for an unexplained sudden loss.
  37. The correct answer is B (20 volume developer is a reasonable starting point for one level of lift.): Since 10 volume produces little to no lift and the client needs a full level of change, a slightly stronger option such as 20 volume is a more reasonable starting point to reliably reach the requested lift. Selecting 10 volume alone would risk falling short of the stated goal. Matching developer strength to the actual amount of lift requested, rather than defaulting to the mildest option, avoids an underwhelming result.
  38. The correct answer is B (The shorter, stacked nape is generally easier to maintain between visits.): One of the practical benefits of a graduated bob's shorter, stacked nape is that it is generally easier for the client to maintain between salon visits compared with longer, unlayered styles. This addresses the client's concern directly and accurately. Explaining this benefit can help the client feel confident choosing the design she is considering.
  39. The correct answer is A (A retouch, applying relaxer only to the new growth at the roots.): A retouch is the correct choice here, since the client has previously relaxed lengths and new, unprocessed growth at the roots, and the service should be applied only to that new growth rather than the entire head. Treating the whole head as a virgin application would mean overlapping relaxer onto the already-straightened lengths, risking breakage. Doubling the application on new growth adds unnecessary chemical exposure, and skipping the service entirely ignores the visible new growth that the client is returning to have treated.
  40. The correct answer is D (Coordinating timing with the makeup artist and staying mindful of the photography schedule.): Because bridal work is scheduled tightly around other vendors, coordinating timing with a makeup artist and staying mindful of the photography schedule is part of delivering the service correctly, not a separate concern. This keeps the finished style ready for the photos without the client waiting or being rushed. Treating the vendor schedule as part of the service prevents delays on the day.
  41. The correct answer is C (Contact the supplier for the missing safety data sheet before the product is used.): Employers must keep a safety data sheet accessible to every worker for each hazardous product used in the salon, and a missing sheet is a gap that needs to be resolved before the product is put into service. The supplier label provides only a summary and does not replace the detailed information the full data sheet contains. Discarding a usable product is an unnecessary overreaction when the supplier can simply be asked to provide the missing document. Assuming an older sheet for a different product still applies overlooks that hazard information is specific to each individual product.
  42. The correct answer is A (The anagen phase, which is the growth phase lasting up to several years.): The anagen phase is the active growth phase and the longest part of the cycle. It lasts roughly two to seven years depending on the person. Most of the hair on a healthy scalp, about 85 to 90 percent, is in anagen. The other phases hold only a small fraction of the hair at once.
  43. The correct answer is C (Whether the hair lifted high enough to support the intended tone.): The first question is whether the hair actually lifted to a high enough level to support the intended tone, because no toner can turn under lightened orange hair into a true ash blonde. If the hair lifted adequately and the brassiness is a fading or formulation issue, a blue based or ash based toner is the correction. If the hair did not lift enough, the stylist must re evaluate whether further lightening is safe.
  44. The correct answer is D (Slip, which allows the hair to be combed and controlled precisely.): Wet cutting adds slip, which allows the hair to be combed and sectioned with precision and makes the cutting line easier to see and control. It does not add permanent volume, increase curl pattern, or change the hair's natural texture chemically. Wet hair also lies close to its true, relaxed length for straight and wavy textures.
  45. The correct answer is A (Records should be backed up regularly, since losing them removes their long-term value.): Losing months of client history without a backup highlights that records need to be backed up regularly, since a single technical failure can otherwise erase the long-term value that careful record keeping is meant to provide. Digital records are not inherently safe from loss just because they are digital, which is exactly why a backup plan matters. Losing this history has a real consequence for future planning and safety, regardless of whether clients keep returning. The value of these records exists independent of whether a specific client happens to request to see them.
  46. The correct answer is A (Half off base, which reduces root lift while still creating curl through the length.): Rollers placed half off base or fully off base, angled down and away from the scalp, reduce root lift while still producing curl through the length of the hair. This is useful when a softer, less bouffant result is wanted near the crown. A roller placed directly on its base, wound straight up from the section, produces the maximum volume and lift.
  47. The correct answer is C (Register with the new province's own regulatory or apprenticeship authority before working there.): Each province and territory issues its own licence, so a hairstylist moving to work in a different province still needs to register with that province's own regulatory or apprenticeship authority, even while carrying the Red Seal. Assuming the Red Seal removes the need for provincial registration misunderstands that licensing to practise remains a provincial responsibility. There is no year long grace period before this registration becomes necessary, and contacting only the previous province does not address the requirement in the new one. Registering locally before beginning work is the correct first step.
  48. The correct answer is B (Slide two fingers along a dry strand from ends toward the scalp, feeling the cuticle's surface.): Porosity is checked by sliding two fingers along a dry strand from the ends toward the scalp, feeling whether the cuticle is smooth or rough and raised. A rough, raised cuticle indicates higher porosity, while a smooth, tight one is lower. This is a cuticle condition, separate from texture, which is strand diameter judged by feel. Elasticity is tested separately by wet stretching.
  49. The correct answer is D (Take a fine, tight weave that lifts only a small percentage of the hair.): The weave controls how the finished highlight reads. A fine, tight weave lifts a small percentage of the hair, so the strands are close together and the result stays subtle and blended. A thick, chunky weave lifts more hair and creates bold, distinct streaks.
  50. The correct answer is A (Add light interior texturizing without disturbing the solid perimeter line.): Coarse, thick hair can look overly heavy in a solid one-length shape, and light interior texturizing removes bulk without breaking the outer perimeter line. Shortening the whole perimeter would change the client's requested length rather than solve the density problem. Converting to full layers abandons the one-length design entirely.
  51. The correct answer is B (Repeated bare-handed contact, even in small amounts, can cause skin sensitization over time.): Repeated bare-handed contact with chemical texture products, even in small amounts, can cause skin sensitization and irritation over time in a stylist who has never reacted before, so working bare-handed for small quantities still carries a real risk. Sensitization is not limited to large, prolonged exposures alone, since repeated smaller exposures over time can have the same cumulative effect. There is no established advantage to mixing bare-handed over gloved. Glove use is a standard precaution for chemical handling regardless of how much product is involved in a given task.
  52. The correct answer is C (A ceramic or tourmaline coated surface with an accurate temperature display.): Well-maintained tools with accurate temperature displays and evenly heating plates, such as ceramic or tourmaline coated surfaces, distribute heat more consistently than a worn or lower quality tool. This reduces the hot spots that scorch a section of hair. Even heating supports safer thermal styling.
  53. The correct answer is B (Ask the client directly about their chemical history before proceeding with the service.): When a record is missing key chemical history, the appropriate step is to gather that information directly from the client before proceeding, since combining certain processes without knowing prior treatments can cause serious damage. Proceeding with a standard formula regardless of history ignores exactly the risk that record keeping is meant to prevent. Assuming a blank file means no prior treatment occurred is an unsafe guess rather than a verified fact. Cancelling outright is unnecessary when the missing information can be gathered directly through a proper consultation.
  54. The correct answer is B (The low elasticity and porous ends show over-processing, so she should rebuild before more lightening.): Healthy wet hair stretches and returns, but a strand that snaps with little stretch has compromised elasticity pointing to weakened internal bonds. Combined with rough, porous ends from repeated lightening, this indicates over-processing. Further lightening at this stage risks breakage, so the safe professional judgment is to postpone and rebuild the internal structure. A gentler or stronger formula does not address the underlying weakened cortex.
  55. The correct answer is D (Follow the manufacturer's specified ratio for that particular product line.): High lift blonde formulas often call for extra developer beyond the standard one to one ratio to provide the additional oxidant needed for maximum lift, so the stylist should follow the manufacturer's specific instructions for that product. Applying a generic ratio to a specialty product can produce an unpredictable result. Checking the manufacturer's chart for each specific product line is standard professional practice.
  56. The correct answer is B (Zero degrees of elevation, producing a blunt, weight-heavy line.): Cutting at zero degrees of elevation means the hair is held straight down, against its natural fall with no lift, producing a blunt, weight-heavy line with the ends lining up in one solid plane. Ninety degrees and beyond involve lifting the hair away from the head instead. Forty-five degrees is a moderate lift used for building graduation, not zero lift.
  57. The correct answer is A (This is a normal part of many smoothing treatments, not a sign of error.): Clients undergoing many smoothing treatments can notice odour or mild eye irritation during the flat-ironing step, and this is a normal part of the process rather than a sign of an application error. Telling the client the service must be stopped immediately overstates the situation given how commonly this occurs. It is not, on its own, evidence of a dangerous allergic reaction, and it is a familiar, previously reported experience rather than something entirely unheard of in this category of service.
  58. The correct answer is A (Humidity resistant styling products and a supply of extra pins on hand.): Humidity resistant styling products and a supply of extra pins are reasonable contingencies given that outdoor portions of a wedding day cannot be controlled the way a salon can. A silicone serum is a finishing smoothing product rather than a humidity defense for an updo, and applying a gel over the finished style would disturb it. Being ready with the right products and pins lets the stylist manage the uncontrollable conditions.
  59. The correct answer is B (Raise the styling chair so the client's head is at a comfortable working height.): Raising the styling chair to bring the client's head to a height that allows a neutral wrist and a straight back reduces strain on the neck, shoulders, and lower back, and this applies to tall clients just as much as short ones. Continuing to reach upward and tilt the neck accumulates unnecessary strain over the course of the appointment. Asking a client to slouch is an unreliable and uncomfortable substitute for adjusting the equipment. Using only one hand does not address the underlying posture problem and may create new strain elsewhere.
  60. The correct answer is A (Avoid vigorous manipulation over the tender area and recent service.): The stylist should avoid vigorous manipulation over any area with a scalp condition, recent chemical service, or reported tenderness. Firm massage there could cause discomfort or worsen a reaction. The massage should be adapted around the sensitive spots and the recent treatment. Adjusting pressure is the professional response to the client's report.
  61. The correct answer is C (20 volume, since using the lowest volume that meets the goal limits damage risk.): Selecting 20 volume, the lowest strength that will realistically achieve the requested two levels of lift, follows the professional principle of using no more developer strength than the goal requires. Reaching for 40 volume when it is not needed adds unnecessary risk to hair condition and the scalp without providing any real benefit. Matching volume to the specific goal, rather than defaulting to the strongest option, protects the client's hair.
  62. The correct answer is C (Dry cutting, so the hair's finished fall can be seen directly.): Dry cutting is used for precision detail work, such as texturizing pieces around the face, where the stylist wants to see exactly how the hair falls before removing more length. It is not accurate to say dry cutting is never used for detail work, since this is one of its main uses. Clipper-over-comb and a zero-degree stationary guideline are not the tools suited to this kind of fine detail texturizing.
  63. The correct answer is D (A spiral wrap produces an elongated, uniform curl along the hair's full length.): A spiral wrap winds the hair around the rod at an angle along its full length rather than stacking sections on top of each other, which produces an elongated, uniform curl well suited to longer hair. A basic stacked wrap is not automatically tighter, since curl tightness depends mainly on rod diameter rather than wrap style. Spiral wraps are typically used on longer hair, not short layered cuts, and both wrap types rely on rods rather than finger styling alone.
  64. The correct answer is B (Breakage where the brush grips fragile, chemically treated hair.): Consistent tension turns a wet section into a smooth, controlled one, but excessive tension on fragile or chemically treated hair increases the risk of breakage where the brush grips the section. The concern is physical breakage at the point of grip rather than the cuticle direction issue. Tension should be firm enough to control the hair without overloading a vulnerable shaft.
  65. The correct answer is C (A Drug Identification Number, commonly shown as a DIN, printed on the product label.): A Drug Identification Number confirms that Health Canada has reviewed a disinfectant product for the specific claims made on its label. Salons rely on DIN registered, hospital grade disinfectants rather than general household cleaning products for implement care. A nutrition panel, a business licence, and a warranty card have no bearing on whether a chemical is validated as an effective salon disinfectant. Checking for this number is part of choosing a compliant product before it is ever opened on a workstation.
  66. The correct answer is A (Density, which measures the number of strands growing in an area of scalp.): Density describes the number of hairs per square centimetre of scalp, while texture is the diameter of a single strand. A client can have fine texture and high density at the same time. Density influences how much bulk to remove when cutting. Texture influences how fast a chemical service processes and the strength needed.
  67. The correct answer is D (Developer diffuses natural pigment and helps build the deposited colour molecules.): Hydrogen peroxide developer works alongside an alkalizing agent to diffuse the hair's natural melanin, which is what produces lift. It also helps small colour molecules join into larger molecules that become trapped inside the hair shaft. This combined action is what makes permanent colour both lighten and deposit at the same time.
  68. The correct answer is D (Handle-first, with the blade closed or turned safely away.): Shears and razors should always be passed to another person handle-first, with the blade closed or turned safely away from both people. Passing blade-first, or open and extended, increases the risk of an accidental cut during the exchange. Prioritizing speed over blade position is not consistent with safe tool handling.
  69. The correct answer is D (The client may be sensitized to this product, so the planned service should not proceed.): Visible redness and swelling at a predisposition test site are signs of a possible allergic reaction, which means the client may be sensitized to that product, and the planned full chemical service should not proceed with it. Proceeding anyway ignores a direct warning sign the test exists specifically to catch. Redness and swelling are not signs of normal processing, since the test area receives no further chemical action beyond the initial small application, and reapplying the same product to an already reacting area would only worsen the situation.
  70. The correct answer is B (Work in clean, appropriately sized sections and move smoothly in one pass.): Repeated passes of a hot tool over the same section multiply the risk of damage far more than a single slower pass at a slightly higher temperature. Working in clean, appropriately sized sections and moving smoothly in one pass is the safer technique. Repeated low temperature passes over the same spot still heat the hair over and over, which compounds the stress on the shaft.
  71. The correct answer is C (Improve ventilation and keep product containers closed when they are not actively in use.): Adequate ventilation, along with keeping product containers closed when not in use, reduces exposure to irritating or harmful fumes for both stylist and client. Continuing as usual ignores a genuine occupational exposure concern, and opening containers further would increase rather than decrease the fumes in the room. Air freshener may mask an odour but does nothing to reduce the actual chemical exposure taking place. Addressing ventilation and container handling is the correct way to reduce this specific hazard.
  72. The correct answer is D (About 1.25 centimetres per month, though the actual rate varies by person and area.): Average scalp hair grows roughly 1.25 centimetres per month during anagen, though this varies by individual, scalp area, age, and health. The figure is a monthly average, not a weekly one, and it applies during active growth rather than the resting phase. Telogen is a dormant phase where the old hair is shed. Coarse texture responds to processing differently but does not change the growth-rate figure.
  73. The correct answer is C (An orange or red-orange stage of underlying pigment is likely to be exposed.): The underlying pigment progression moves from red through orange and toward pale yellow as hair lifts higher, so a mid-range target like level 7 typically exposes an orange or red-orange stage. Knowing this in advance lets the stylist plan the toning formula before lightening even begins. This is a direct, practical application of the law of colour.
  74. The correct answer is B (The finished cut may end up far shorter than the client expected once dry.): Cutting curly or coily hair to an intended length while wet and stretched, without accounting for shrinkage, commonly results in a finished cut far shorter than the client expected once the curl springs back. It is not accurate that the length is guaranteed once dry, or that coily hair does not change length at all when drying. This is exactly why many stylists cut at least partially dry for these textures.
  75. The correct answer is A (It can produce an unpredictable result, since the products were not tested to work together.): Combining products from different lines that were not designed to work together can produce an unpredictable and sometimes unsafe result, since manufacturer instructions and testing are specific to that formula's own products used as intended. Assuming all reducing agents and neutralizers work identically across brands ignores real formulation differences between manufacturers. The consequence of this kind of mixing is chemical unpredictability rather than a minor cosmetic difference like scent, and there is no basis for assuming a cross-brand combination reliably produces a stronger or better result.
  76. The correct answer is A (Break up the stiff layer gently by hand once fully dry.): A gel-set style often dries with a stiff outer layer, sometimes called a cast, meant to be broken up gently by hand once fully dry. Styling the hair while it is still wet would disturb the shape. Breaking the cast releases the hold while preserving the defined curl.
  77. The correct answer is B (The original record should stay unaltered after a complaint or adverse reaction has occurred.): A stylist should never destroy or alter a client record after a complaint or an adverse reaction has occurred, since the original record is what shows what was actually documented and done at the time of service. Correcting details after the fact, even with good intentions, undermines the record's reliability during a dispute or investigation. Deleting and replacing the file entirely removes evidence rather than preserving it. Any needed clarification should be added as a new, clearly dated note rather than altering the original entry.
  78. The correct answer is A (Surfactants, which trap oil in micelles that rinse away with water.): Shampoo cleans primarily through surfactants, which are dual-structure molecules with a water-loving end and an oil-loving end. The oil-loving ends surround sebum, buildup, and dirt, forming tiny clusters called micelles. Rinsing carries these micelles away with the water. This is the core mechanism behind shampoo cleansing.
  79. The correct answer is C (The earlier formula may not have included enough deposit for full coverage.): If grey regrowth becomes visible faster than expected, it can indicate the earlier formula did not include enough natural or natural-warm base to achieve solid, lasting coverage. Reviewing and adjusting the base tone percentage for future services can help address this pattern. This kind of observation over multiple visits helps refine a client's ongoing grey coverage formula.
  80. The correct answer is D (Send the shears for professional sharpening before the edge dulls too far.): Shears should be sent for professional sharpening before the edge becomes dull enough to pull or crimp the hair rather than shear through it cleanly, as part of routine maintenance. Fully loosening the pivot tension would reduce control rather than restore cutting quality. Permanently switching tools or waiting for visible failure does not address ordinary blade maintenance needs.
  81. The correct answer is D (Calcium hydroxide and guanidine carbonate, which react together to straighten the hair.): No-lye relaxers use calcium hydroxide combined with guanidine carbonate, which react together to form the active straightening compound, distinguishing them chemically from sodium hydroxide based lye relaxers. Ammonium thioglycolate and hydrogen peroxide describe the reducing and neutralizing pairing used in a permanent wave rather than a no-lye relaxer. Sodium bicarbonate and citric acid, and hydrogen peroxide with ammonia, are unrelated to no-lye relaxer chemistry and describe other product categories entirely.
  82. The correct answer is A (From the roots toward the ends, following the same direction as the cuticle scales.): Directing the nozzle so airflow travels from the roots toward the ends follows the direction of the cuticle scales. This smooths the cuticle down and produces more shine with less frizz. Blowing from the ends back toward the scalp lifts the cuticle and roughens the surface, which adds texture but gives a duller result.
  83. The correct answer is B (Anti-fatigue matting placed under the standing station to reduce stress on the legs and back.): Anti-fatigue matting under a standing station reduces stress on the legs and lower back compared with standing directly on a hard floor for a full shift. Chair height should be set based on the client's own head position, not adjusted uniformly regardless of client height, and heavier shears increase rather than reduce hand and wrist strain. Removing breaks would worsen cumulative fatigue rather than reduce it. Adding supportive matting directly addresses the discomfort this stylist is describing.
  84. The correct answer is B (Hydrogen bonds, which break and reform with moisture and heat.): A blow-dry style or wet set works through hydrogen bonds, which break when hair is wet and reform as it dries. These are physical bonds that moisture and heat can move. The style will release when the hair gets wet or humid again. A chemical change to disulfide bonds would be needed for a lasting structural change.
  85. The correct answer is D (Blue is the complementary colour used to neutralize unwanted orange tones.): Blue sits directly opposite orange on the colour wheel, so a blue based toner is used to cancel unwanted orange warmth in lightened hair. Complementary pairs neutralize each other when combined in the correct proportion. This relationship is the basis for most corrective toning decisions.
  86. The correct answer is A (A horizontal parting pattern kept parallel to the floor.): A horizontal parting pattern that stays parallel to the floor is typically used to build a one-length or blunt shape with a consistent perimeter. A vertical or pivotal pattern is more suited to graduated or layered shapes that follow the head's curve. A diagonal parting pattern serves a different purpose tied to face-framing rather than a uniform perimeter.
  87. The correct answer is D (Move to neutralizer promptly rather than continuing to the full standard processing time.): If a check shows the curl pattern has already formed, continuing to a fixed standard time risks overprocessing hair that clearly processed faster than the manufacturer's typical estimate. The correct response is to move to neutralizer once the curl has actually developed, since a check like this is exactly why progress is monitored directly rather than by the clock alone. Extending time further or restarting with different rods ignores what the check has already shown and adds unnecessary risk.
  88. The correct answer is C (Place each roller directly on its base and wind it straight up.): A roller placed directly on its base and wound straight up from the section produces maximum volume and lift at the scalp. Rollers placed half off base or fully off base reduce root lift while still producing curl through the length. This distinction matters near the crown when a softer result is wanted.
  89. The correct answer is B (Explain the risk in plain language and propose a safer, multi-visit path to the same goal.): Setting realistic expectations means explaining the risk in plain language and describing a safer path to the result, then getting the client's informed agreement before proceeding, rather than avoiding the conversation or overriding the client's stated goal outright. Simply attempting the unsafe plan as requested ignores the assessment findings that led to the concern in the first place. Declaring the goal impossible without explanation leaves the client without the information needed to understand or agree to an alternative. Clear, specific communication about both the risk and the alternative is what this part of the consultation requires.
  90. The correct answer is D (Dryness from low sebum, rather than a skin condition or dandruff.): A scalp that produces very little sebum may feel tight or flaky from dryness rather than from a skin condition. Low sebum means less natural lubrication on the skin and hair. A stylist should tell dryness from a condition before recommending a product. Dandruff and dermatitis present with flakes or scaling along with other signs.
  91. The correct answer is D (Stronger developer than needed adds damage risk without any real benefit.): Using the strongest developer available regardless of the client's actual goal adds unnecessary risk of damage and scalp irritation without providing any benefit when less lift was requested. Professional formulation matches developer strength to the specific target rather than defaulting to maximum strength. This explanation helps the client understand why volume selection is a deliberate, goal based decision.
  92. The correct answer is C (Apply whichever technique fits the shape rather than the client's gender.): Men's and women's cutting tendencies are general patterns rather than fixed rules, and a short pixie cut may use as much clipper-over-comb work as a typical men's cut. The stylist should choose the technique that fits the desired shape and the client's hair rather than assuming a technique based on gender. This flexibility is part of applying cutting principles correctly.
  93. The correct answer is A (A leave-in conditioner or protein treatment suited to newly relaxed, more porous hair.): After neutralizing shampoo, a leave-in conditioner or protein treatment is an appropriate next step, since it helps replace moisture and support the structure of hair that has just become more porous from the relaxer service. A second relaxer application at this point adds unnecessary chemical exposure. A strong clarifying shampoo would strip the hair rather than support it right after a chemical service, and moving straight to heat styling without any supportive product skips a widely used part of a complete relaxer service.
  94. The correct answer is C (Straight or chemically relaxed hair, which will hold the molded shape predictably.): Finger waving works best on hair with enough length and a texture that will hold the S-shape, typically straight or chemically relaxed hair. Naturally curly or coily hair will not mold into the flat wave pattern in the same predictable way. The technique is a tested fundamental because it develops finger control, directional combing, and the ability to build a style by hand.
  95. The correct answer is C (The right to know, which underlies the worker's other health and safety rights.): The right to know requires an employer to inform workers of known workplace hazards and provide the training needed to work safely around them. This right underlies the other two fundamental worker rights, since a worker cannot participate in safety decisions or judge a task unsafe without first knowing what the hazards are. Promotion, overtime pay, and vacation entitlement are employment standards matters governed by different legislation entirely. The right to know is specifically about hazard information and safety training.
  96. The correct answer is D (This is traction alopecia, so reducing sustained tension along the hairline can help if caught early.): Traction alopecia is caused by sustained tension from tight braids, ponytails, or extensions worn repeatedly over the same area, most often along the front hairline. It is the form a stylist can directly cause or prevent through styling choices. If caught before the follicle scars, reducing tension can often let the hair recover. The pattern here follows her styling rather than a hormonal or autoimmune course.
  97. The correct answer is A (A muddy tone, because violet is not complementary to orange.): A violet based toner neutralises yellow, which is the residual warmth left at the highest levels of lift. If the hair is still strongly orange, violet is not complementary to orange, so the result is a muddy tone rather than a clean neutral. The stylist must read the pigment actually present in the hair rather than follow a formula from memory.
  98. The correct answer is C (A stationary guide stays fixed while a travelling guide moves with each section.): A stationary guide is one fixed length that every section is cut to match, while a travelling guide is cut to match the length of the section immediately before it, moving progressively around the head. Both are used to establish a perimeter before internal layering begins. Choosing between them depends on the desired outline consistency.
  99. The correct answer is C (Follow the exact ratio specified and order more product rather than adjusting the mix.): Manufacturer mixing ratios reflect how a specific formula was tested to perform, so the correct response to running low on supply is to follow the exact ratio and order more product rather than adjusting the mix, which can produce an unpredictable result. Substituting a component from a different product line risks combining chemistries that were never designed to work together. Skipping a required component entirely and applying the rest as a single part abandons the formula's tested chemistry altogether rather than preserving it.
  100. The correct answer is A (On base, which produces maximum volume and lift at the scalp.): A roller placed directly on its base and wound straight up from the section produces the maximum volume and lift at the scalp. Rollers placed half off base or fully off base, angled down and away from the scalp, reduce root lift while still producing curl through the length. Since this stylist specifically wants volume at the scalp, the on-base placement is correct.
  101. The correct answer is B (Employers must retrain workers whenever a new hazardous product is introduced into the workplace.): WHMIS training is not a one time event, and employers must retrain workers whenever a new hazardous product is introduced, even if general training was completed previously. Training needs to cover the specific products actually used in that salon, not WHMIS in general terms. Assuming last year's training is automatically sufficient overlooks this requirement and leaves staff without product specific handling and first aid information. Whether or not a worker files a written request, or whether the pictogram has changed, is not what determines when retraining is owed.
  102. The correct answer is A (Residue can cause dullness or interfere with how the chemical service works on the hair.): Thorough rinsing is essential because any residue left in the hair can cause dullness or interfere with a chemical service performed afterward. A clean cuticle allows chemical solutions to work as intended. Leaving shampoo behind can block consistent penetration and lead to an uneven result. Rinsing until no suds remain is the correct standard.
  103. The correct answer is B (Explain that a professional predisposition test is still required regardless.): Even if a client has used a colour product at home without incident, a professional predisposition test is still required before a professional oxidative colour service, since formulas, concentrations, and ingredients can differ between products and sensitivities can develop over time. Relying on a client's informal home experience is not a substitute for the required test. Explaining this clearly protects both the client and the stylist.
  104. The correct answer is A (Clipper work, including clipper-over-comb and guard-based fades or tapers.): Men's cutting typically leans more heavily on clipper work, including clipper-over-comb and guard-based fades and tapers, with more emphasis on close, defined lines at the hairline and neckline. Women's cutting relies more on shear work across a wider range of lengths and elevations. These are general tendencies rather than fixed rules for every client.
  105. The correct answer is A (Section carefully by growth amount, applying relaxer precisely to each area's new growth.): Uneven growth calls for careful sectioning by growth amount, so relaxer reaches each area's actual new growth precisely without spilling onto the previously treated hair in areas where less new growth has come in. Applying one uniform amount across the whole head ignores the uneven pattern and risks overlap in the areas with less new growth. Treating the whole head as virgin hair guarantees overlap everywhere previously relaxed hair exists. Skipping the areas with less visible growth would leave some new growth untreated rather than solving the underlying overlap risk.
  106. The correct answer is B (Regular deep conditioning to replace moisture and support integrity.): Regular deep conditioning helps replace lost moisture and support the hair's structural integrity between services. Giving hair recovery time between a chemical service and heavy thermal styling reduces the combined stress on a shaft that is already vulnerable after processing. Combined with heat protectant it supports recovery.
  107. The correct answer is C (The shears have dulled and are crushing the hair shaft rather than shearing it cleanly.): A dull shear forces the stylist to apply more pressure and can bend or crush the hair shaft rather than shearing it, which produces exactly the crushed, uneven ends described here. A permanent change in the client's hair type is a far less likely explanation for a sudden shift during a single service. A brand new, properly sharpened pair of shears would not be expected to produce this result. Recognizing this pattern points toward professional sharpening as the next needed step rather than assuming the cause lies elsewhere.
  108. The correct answer is B (Refer the client and postpone the service, since live insects indicate a possible contagious condition.): Signs of a possible contagious condition, such as unusual scaling with visible movement or live insects at the hair shaft base, call for referral rather than continuing the service. A stylist should not continue with a chemical service in this situation, because it could spread the condition and is outside salon scope. Postponing and tactfully referring is the professional response and protects both client and salon. Dandruff does not show moving insects at the hair shaft base.
  109. The correct answer is C (Dilute a little powder with developer and shampoo, then work it through like a shampoo.): A bleach bath is made by mixing a small amount of lightening powder with developer and shampoo. The shampoo base dilutes the active lightening agents, so the lift is softer and slower. Working it through like a shampoo, rather than painting a thick paste, is what lets it gently remove old colour buildup.
  110. The correct answer is A (A stationary or low-traveling guideline in the back with a higher-traveling guideline through the top.): A connected weight line in the back is consistent with a stationary or lower-elevation traveling guideline, while independently moving, textured pieces on top require the higher-elevation traveling guideline associated with layering. Applying one single guideline approach identically across the whole head would not let each area serve its own distinct goal. Guidelines and elevation work together, so removing the guideline entirely is not a workable substitute for elevation alone.
  111. The correct answer is B (Keratin and chemical smoothing treatments, due to fumes released during flat-ironing.): Keratin and chemical smoothing treatments most consistently require ventilation attention tied specifically to a heat-based step, since flat-ironing can release formaldehyde or other irritating fumes from the product already on the hair. Rod placement during basic perm wrapping is a mechanical step that does not generate fumes. Shampoo lathering during a neutralizing step is not identified as a significant fume source. A small predisposition test patch does not involve a heat-based step and is not associated with generating significant fumes.
  112. The correct answer is D (An updo that keeps the neck and shoulders visible for a balanced line.): For bridal clients, the shape and height of the chosen updo should suit the neckline of the gown. A high, structured neckline generally pairs well with an updo that keeps the neck and shoulders visible, while a more open or strapless neckline can support more volume or a style worn closer to the shoulders. This keeps the overall line balanced rather than competing with the gown.
  113. The correct answer is C (The stylist should have followed up to fully understand this recent chemical history before proceeding.): Checking a client's record and history for any relevant chemical treatment, including services performed elsewhere, is part of a proper consultation before a new chemical or colour service. Combining certain processes without knowing the client's recent history can cause serious damage, so a passing mention of a relaxer service should prompt clear follow up questions rather than being set aside. Where the previous service took place does not make it any less relevant to planning a safe new service. Fully understanding recent chemical history, regardless of source, is part of the stylist's responsibility here.
  114. The correct answer is C (Slide two fingers along a dry strand from the ends toward the scalp.): Porosity is checked by sliding two fingers along a dry strand from the ends toward the scalp. This lets the stylist feel whether the cuticle is smooth or raised and rough. A smooth slide suggests low porosity and a rough, raised feel suggests high porosity. The result guides processing time and product strength.
  115. The correct answer is D (An on scalp lightener that is creamier and buffered for direct scalp contact.): An on scalp formula is a gentler, creamier lightener specifically buffered for direct scalp contact, used for a full head blonding or a root to end retouch. Every professional lightener package states whether it is rated for on scalp use. Assuming any powder is safe for scalp contact is a real safety error.
  116. The correct answer is A (A horizontal line at the nape transitioning into a vertical line.): A single haircut often combines more than one cutting line across different areas, such as a horizontal line at the nape transitioning into a vertical line through the sides, since each line produces a different shape suited to a different area. Using only a diagonal forward line, only a vertical line, or only a horizontal line throughout would not match both stated goals. Recognizing which line produces which shape is a frequently tested application skill.
  117. The correct answer is D (The hair likely has raised cuticle and higher porosity, suggesting a shorter processing estimate.): A rough feel with a raised texture when stroked against the direction of growth is a common way to judge that the cuticle is lifted, which points to higher porosity and generally a faster-processing, shorter-timing plan to avoid overprocessing. This texture does not indicate the hair is broadly resistant to chemicals, and it is directly relevant to timing rather than something to disregard. Roughness reflects cuticle condition rather than coarseness, which is a separate property describing strand thickness.
  118. The correct answer is B (Pressing the index and middle finger on either side while combing in the opposite direction.): The ridge is shaped by pressing the index and middle finger against the scalp on either side of the section while combing the hair in the opposite direction. This pushes a crest of hair up between the fingers, which forms the ridge. A wave is not built by winding around a rod, applying heat, or backcombing, which are separate techniques.
  119. The correct answer is C (Treat it as contaminated and launder and disinfect it before it returns to use.): Any reusable item that contacts blood, including a cape, is treated as contaminated and must be laundered and properly disinfected before it can be used again. Shaking it out, wiping the visible spot, or masking the area with an unrelated product does not remove the actual contamination risk. This standard applies regardless of how small or minor the exposure appeared to be. Following this step protects the next client who sits in that cape.
  120. The correct answer is D (Modify or postpone the service on the affected part of the scalp.): The bumps suggest folliculitis, an inflammation or infection of the hair follicle. It can be caused by bacteria, tight styles, or ingrown hairs. Active folliculitis is a reason to modify or postpone service on the affected area. Continuing to work there could worsen the irritation or the infection.
  121. The correct answer is A (A backing paper or plastic board held behind the section while it is painted.): Balayage paints product directly onto the surface of the hair without a foil boundary. A backing paper or plastic board behind the section keeps the lightener off the hair underneath it. This is what protects the underlying strands from accidental lightening during freehand work.
  122. The correct answer is D (Leave the front longer while keeping the same overall graduation principle at the back.): The graduated bob technique allows the front to be left longer for a face-framing angle while the back maintains its typical stacked, weighted graduation. This is a common variation rather than an exception to the technique. Refusing the request would misunderstand how flexible the graduated bob design actually is.
  123. The correct answer is B (Mixing concentrated chemicals generally calls for added protection like eye protection where recommended.): Mixing a concentrated chemical component, rather than using a ready-to-use formula, generally calls for added protection such as eye protection where recommended, since concentrated chemicals carry a higher risk during handling than a pre-diluted product. Ruling out eye protection for every chemical texture service overlooks situations exactly like this one. This kind of protection is meant for the stylist performing the mixing and application, not only for the client. Experience level does not change the physical risk that handling a concentrated chemical component presents.
  124. The correct answer is B (The Dutch braid, where outer strands are crossed underneath the centre strand.): A Dutch braid uses the same principle of picking up new hair from the sides, but the outer strands are crossed underneath the centre strand instead of over it. Crossing underneath causes the braid to sit up and stand out from the head rather than lying flat, which is why it is sometimes taught as an inverted or reverse French braid. Crossing over the centre gives the flatter French braid.
  125. The correct answer is D (Tools that puncture skin or carry a real risk of contact with a client's blood.): Sterilization is reserved for implements that puncture skin or carry a genuine risk of blood contact, because it destroys all microbial life including bacterial spores. An autoclave using pressurized steam at a controlled temperature achieves this level of pathogen destruction in a salon setting. Tools that only ever touch dry hair or are used purely for mixing do not carry the same blood exposure risk and are managed through routine sanitation and disinfection instead. Knowing which category a tool falls into is what determines the correct standard to apply.

Practice Test 6 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (The telogen phase, during which the follicle rests and the old hair is released.): Shed hairs in an ordinary daily pattern come from follicles in the telogen phase. In telogen the follicle rests and then releases the old hair to make room for a new one. A steady, modest amount of shedding is normal rather than pathological. Heavier sustained loss that continues over time is the signal that referral may be appropriate.
  2. The correct answer is B (Remove the excess artificial pigment with a colour reducer first.): A too dark result comes from excess artificial pigment, so it is a colour removal case first. A colour reducer breaks down the artificial colour molecules so they can be rinsed out, exposing the natural level underneath. Once that base is visible, the stylist can formulate the intended shade onto it.
  3. The correct answer is C (The area cannot be fully corrected this visit and will need time to grow out.): Over-thinning close to the scalp cannot be corrected in the same visit, since the short regrowth simply has to grow out before it blends with the surrounding hair. Being honest with the client about this limitation is more appropriate than suggesting a quick fix that will not actually resolve it. This is why texturizing is always worked well away from the root in the first place.
  4. The correct answer is D (Address the ventilation problem first, since good airflow is a standard precaution for this service.): Because good ventilation is a standard precaution for chemical smoothing treatments, particularly during the fume-producing flat-ironing step, a broken fan should be addressed before proceeding rather than treated as optional. Proceeding as scheduled without functioning ventilation increases exposure risk for both stylist and client. Skipping the flat-ironing step is not a realistic option, since that step is central to how the treatment actually smooths the hair. Simply shortening the appointment does not resolve the underlying ventilation problem during whatever time the service still takes.
  5. The correct answer is A (An updo that keeps the neck and shoulders visible.): For bridal clients the shape and height of the chosen updo should suit the neckline of the gown. A high, structured neckline generally pairs well with an updo that keeps the neck and shoulders visible. A more open or strapless neckline can support more volume or a style worn closer to the shoulders.
  6. The correct answer is B (It keeps the client's full history accurate and available for any future service planning.): Keeping a complete and current record after every visit, including routine non chemical appointments, ensures the client's full history is accurate and available whenever it is needed for future service planning. Limiting record updates to only complex chemical visits leaves gaps that could matter later, since even a simple haircut visit can include useful notes about condition or preference. Treating routine documentation as unnecessary paperwork overlooks its value as an ongoing, cumulative record. Consistent record keeping, not just occasional record keeping, is what makes the file genuinely useful over time.
  7. The correct answer is C (A moisturizing shampoo with gentler surfactants for daily use on treated hair.): Moisturizing shampoo uses gentler surfactants and conditioning agents for regular use on dry or chemically treated hair. Using clarifying shampoo daily on colour-treated, dry hair can worsen dryness and irritation over time. The clarifying formula should be kept for occasional deep cleaning. A moisturizing routine better protects the colour and the cuticle.
  8. The correct answer is A (Root smudging with a shade close to the surrounding colour.): Correcting banding usually means softening that hard line rather than erasing it in one step. Root smudging with a shade close to the surrounding colour blends the transition zone. In more stubborn cases the stylist works down from the root in smaller sections, feathering the timing so each band processes for a shorter time.
  9. The correct answer is C (Moderate elevation, roughly in the forty-five to ninety degree range, rather than pushing to the extremes.): Moderate elevation in the forty-five to ninety degree range removes meaningful interior weight while still generally preserving a defined, connected perimeter line, balancing both of the client's stated goals. Zero degrees would preserve too much weight and fail the client's request for reduction. Pushing well past ninety degrees, or alternating unpredictably between extremes, risks disconnecting the perimeter entirely rather than keeping it defined.
  10. The correct answer is D (A thio relaxer, which uses the same gentler reducing chemistry found in a perm.): Thio relaxers are built on the same reducing and oxidizing chemistry used in a permanent wave rather than a strongly alkaline hydroxide reaction, which makes them generally milder than lye or no-lye formulas, matching what this client is asking for. A stronger sodium hydroxide relaxer moves in the opposite direction toward greater chemical intensity. Doubling a standard no-lye application or extending its time past the recommended window increases chemical exposure rather than offering a genuinely gentler alternative.
  11. The correct answer is D (A loss of elasticity from cumulative thermal damage to the hair structure.): Loss of elasticity is a subtle but important sign of thermal damage. Healthy hair will stretch slightly under gentle tension and spring back, while heat-damaged hair is more likely to simply snap or to stretch and stay stretched without recovering its shape. This client's hair is showing the stretch-and-stay response rather than recovery.
  12. The correct answer is C (Run the shear through the full sanitation and disinfection process before using it today.): A disinfected implement does not remain disinfected indefinitely simply because it was stored untouched, since dust and airborne contaminants can settle on an exposed surface over time. The safest practice treats any implement due for use as needing a fresh sanitation and disinfection cycle before it touches a client. Assuming visible cleanliness or a quiet storage period preserves the earlier disinfection status is not a reliable trade standard. Running the shear through the full process again removes any doubt before the appointment begins.
  13. The correct answer is B (High density means more hairs per area, so bulk removal is judged separately from texture.): Density is how many individual hairs grow per square centimetre of scalp, while texture is the diameter of an individual strand. The two are assessed separately because they affect different decisions. A high-density client needs bulk removal judged by the overall amount of hair, not by the fineness of each strand. Assuming texture controls density would lead to the wrong cutting plan.
  14. The correct answer is A (More porous ends absorbed and then released the toner's cool pigment faster.): Ends are typically more porous than regrowth, so they can take up a cool toner quickly and then fade unevenly as the deposited pigment washes out faster than at the less porous root area. This uneven fade is a porosity and pigment retention issue layered on top of tone theory. Recognizing the pattern helps the stylist plan a more even future toning strategy.
  15. The correct answer is D (The beveled edge, the standard grind for precision cutting.): The beveled edge is ground on both sides to a fine angle, producing a clean, precise cutting line suited to everyday haircutting. It is the standard grind found on most general-purpose shears. Serrated, convex, and notched edges serve different, more specialized cutting purposes.
  16. The correct answer is A (That row will show uneven processing and a curl pattern that looks inconsistent.): Uneven tension changes how consistently the solution can penetrate and reshape the hair, so a row wrapped much tighter than the rest of the head is likely to process differently and produce a curl pattern that looks inconsistent once dry. Drying time and colour are not affected by wrapping tension. Rod diameter does control overall curl size, but tension still plays its own separate role in how evenly a given rod's curl actually develops.
  17. The correct answer is D (Hydrogen bonds, which let the hair be reshaped into a smooth line or a curl.): Flat irons and curling irons work by using heat to temporarily break and reform the hydrogen bonds inside the hair shaft, which allows the hair to be reshaped into a smooth line or a curl. This is a temporary change that the hair reverses over time, unlike a chemical service. Disulphide bonds are altered by chemical services rather than by heat styling.
  18. The correct answer is B (Apply direct pressure with a clean dressing until bleeding stops, clean the area, and bandage it.): For a minor cut, the correct response is direct pressure with a clean dressing until bleeding stops, followed by cleaning the area and applying a bandage. Continuing to work without addressing an active cut, or waiting until the end of the shift, delays proper first aid unnecessarily. A reusable cloth is not a clean, single use dressing appropriate for an open wound, and implement disinfectant is formulated for tools, not for direct application to skin. Prompt, correct first aid protects the stylist and avoids further contamination of the wound.
  19. The correct answer is A (Switch to a moisture treatment, since too much protein on dry hair can leave it stiff.): Protein-based treatments add strength and firmness but are for hair with weakened bonds, while moisture-based treatments add and hold water within the cortex. Hair that is dry, stiff, and brittle but still returns to length is moisture-poor. Too much protein on protein-rich, moisture-poor hair causes protein overload, leaving it stiff and prone to snapping. A moisture treatment is the correct adjustment here.
  20. The correct answer is C (Metallic salts can react with peroxide and generate heat at the scalp.): Metallic dyes deposit metallic salts onto the hair shaft, and these salts can react with the hydrogen peroxide in permanent colour or lightener, generating significant heat at the scalp, discolouring unpredictably, or causing severe breakage. Because metallic dye is not always disclosed, a metallic salt test is a standard precaution. A strand containing metallic salts will discolour, heat up, or bubble within a short time.
  21. The correct answer is C (A softer, less blunt line than a straight-edge shear produces.): A serrated or micro-serrated edge grips the hair slightly during the cut and is used where a softer, less blunt line is wanted compared to a straight-edge shear. It does not produce a perfectly blunt result, since that is the opposite of its intended effect. It also does not permanently alter the structure of the hair's cuticle.
  22. The correct answer is B (A predisposition test checks for allergic reaction, while a strand test checks the hair's response.): A predisposition test checks whether a client will have an allergic reaction to a chemical product, applied to a small area of skin, while a strand test checks how the hair itself, rather than the skin, will respond to the planned chemical process. Reversing these two purposes misstates what each test is actually for. Neither test exists solely to determine processing time, though a strand test can help inform that estimate, and neither is aimed at confirming the client's satisfaction with a planned look.
  23. The correct answer is D (Dry the sections fully before backcombing for a stable foundation.): Backcombing should always be done on completely dry hair, since wet hair is more elastic and more easily damaged and does not hold the cushion of volume. A dry foundation supports the smoother top layer and the finished shape. Backcombing damp hair is a common cause of a style that will not hold.
  24. The correct answer is A (The right to participate, which is best used by formally reporting the concern.): The right to participate is best exercised by raising a concern through the salon's proper reporting channel, such as a supervisor or a health and safety committee, rather than only mentioning it informally to a coworker. An empty first aid kit is a genuine workplace safety concern, and informal mention alone does not ensure it actually gets addressed. This situation is not limited to the right to refuse, since no task is currently being performed that would trigger that specific right. Formally reporting the gap is what turns awareness into an actual fix.
  25. The correct answer is B (The sebaceous gland, which secretes sebum and coats the hair and skin.): Sebaceous glands attach to the hair follicle and release sebum, an oily secretion. Sebum coats the hair shaft and skin and keeps both lubricated. Output varies by person and responds to hormones, genetics, and general health. A scalp that looks oily quickly simply produces sebum faster.
  26. The correct answer is B (Demi-permanent colour deposits colour with little to no lift of the level.): Demi-permanent formulas are designed to deposit colour and tone with little to no lift of the natural level, which is why the client's overall lightness will not change significantly. This makes demi-permanent a common choice for toning and grey blending rather than dramatic lightening. Setting this expectation clearly during consultation avoids a disappointed client after the service.
  27. The correct answer is C (Texturizing, applied as a finishing step to reduce bulk and soften movement.): Texturizing removes bulk and softens how hair moves and sits, refining the finished result rather than establishing or altering the underlying shape the way graduation or layering does. It is applied as a finishing step after the base cut and its elevation pattern are already set. This is why texturizing is the appropriate choice for the stylist's specific goal here.
  28. The correct answer is C (Reassess processing time and consider moving to the neutralizing steps sooner than planned.): Noticing unexpected weakness partway through processing is an early warning sign, so the correct response is to reassess processing time and consider moving to the neutralizing steps sooner than originally planned, rather than waiting out a fixed schedule. Early checks during a chemical service are specifically valuable because they provide real evidence about how that hair is responding. Adding more relaxer to an already weakening section would worsen the situation. Waiting for the full scheduled time despite this early warning increases the risk of further damage.
  29. The correct answer is D (To allow air to pass directly through the section for faster rough-drying.): This is a vent brush, which has widely spaced bristles and open slots in its base. The slots allow air to pass directly through the section for faster rough-drying while preserving the hair's natural texture and movement rather than producing a fully smooth finish. A solid brush would push the hair against the dryer and flatten its natural pattern.
  30. The correct answer is A (The province's own apprenticeship or licensing authority, distinct from the national Red Seal standard itself.): Each province and territory runs its own apprenticeship and licensing system through its own provincial regulatory authority, which sets training hour requirements and issues the actual provincial licence, even though the exam content itself is developed to a common national standard. This role is not filled by a private trade association without regulatory authority, and it is not something a salon owner or manager oversees independently. There is also no single federal licence that applies identically across every province. Recognizing that licensing responsibility sits at the provincial level clarifies how the overall Red Seal system actually works.
  31. The correct answer is D (Choosing the lowest developer volume that can still realistically achieve the goal.): For a client with a known sensitive scalp, selecting the lowest developer volume capable of achieving the requested lift helps limit potential irritation while still meeting the client's goal. Reaching for the strongest available volume would increase, not reduce, the risk to an already sensitive scalp. This kind of judgment call is part of adapting standard formulation to an individual client's needs.
  32. The correct answer is C (An increased risk of strain injury to the hand and wrist.): A dull shear forces the stylist to apply more pressure to cut through the hair, and that added pressure over repeated services increases the risk of strain injury to the hand and wrist. It does not improve precision and has no effect on hair growth patterns. A dull shear also needs sharpening sooner, not less often.
  33. The correct answer is A (A curl pattern that looks inconsistent between the front and back of the head.): Uneven tension changes how consistently the solution can penetrate and reshape different sections, so wrapping the front hairline much tighter than the back is likely to produce a curl pattern that looks inconsistent between the two areas once dry. Neutralizer reforms bonds in whatever shape the hair is already in and does not correct wrapping errors. Tension differences of this kind do not cause hair loss or a change in natural colour.
  34. The correct answer is A (New hair from each side is gradually added into the outer strands as it descends.): A French braid follows the same crossing pattern as a three-strand braid but begins close to the scalp and gradually incorporates new hair from each side into the outer strands as it progresses downward. This makes the braid appear to grow directly out of the scalp along its entire path. Crossing the strands underneath creates a Dutch braid, and using two sections is a fishtail.
  35. The correct answer is B (Chronic delays create a ripple effect that pressures later steps toward being rushed.): A stylist who underestimates a service's length creates a ripple effect through the rest of the day, delaying every client booked afterward and increasing the temptation to skip a step, such as a proper patch test or a full consultation, to catch up. This consequence extends well beyond the single appointment where the underestimate occurred. Later clients are directly affected by the earlier delay, contrary to assuming the impact stays contained. Recognizing this connection is part of why realistic service timing matters for more than just punctuality alone.
  36. The correct answer is D (Add the corrective pigment in small amounts and check the strand partway through.): Adding the complementary corrector gradually and checking a test strand partway through processing allows the stylist to stop once the unwanted tone is neutralized. This measured approach prevents pushing the hair into the opposite unwanted cast. Rushing to the maximum amount without checking removes the stylist's ability to control the outcome.
  37. The correct answer is A (Progressively longer guards are used, blending upward toward the longer top.): A skin fade builds upward from bare skin through a sequence of increasingly longer guards until it meets the longer length on top of the head. Using the same bare length throughout would not produce a graduated fade at all. Moving to shorter guards toward the crown would reverse the intended graduation.
  38. The correct answer is B (A smaller rod diameter produces a tighter curl, and a larger rod produces a looser wave.): Rod diameter directly and predictably controls curl size, because it sets the arc the hair is held in while the disulfide bonds reform. A small-diameter rod wraps hair through a tight arc, producing a small, tight curl, while a large-diameter rod produces a loose, wide wave. Rod diameter is not merely about processing speed, and the direction described in the alternative option is reversed from how rod size actually works. Chemical strength affects processing, not the geometry of the curl itself.
  39. The correct answer is B (A curl-defining cream, which coats the clumps and holds them together with light hold.): A curl-defining cream is generally heavier and richer than a leave-in conditioner and carries some light styling hold, so it coats individual curl clumps, encourages them to stay grouped together, and reduces frizz along the surface of the curl. Mousse is a lighter foam for volume at the root, and a firm hairspray is a finishing product used at the end. The cream is the right next step after the leave-in when definition and grouping are the goal.
  40. The correct answer is D (Prolonged skin contact with lightening product raises the risk of contact dermatitis without gloves.): Gloves protect the stylist's skin from prolonged chemical contact during mixing and application, reducing the risk of contact dermatitis, which is one of the most common occupational health problems among hairstylists. Working quickly does not eliminate exposure, since even brief, repeated contact over many services adds up over a career. Waiting until after application to put on gloves misses the mixing stage, where exposure risk is often highest. Washing hands afterward does not replace the protective barrier gloves provide during the task itself.
  41. The correct answer is A (Apply a blue-green corrective toner at a similar level using a low volume developer.): Correcting an orange cast calls for a blue-green complementary toner, which also happens to suit the client's cool skin undertone rather than working against it. Because the client wants to preserve the current level, the correction should use a low volume developer focused on depositing the corrective tone rather than lifting the hair any further. Combining the correct complementary colour, the client's undertone preference, and the level constraint together is what separates a well reasoned corrective plan from a formula that solves only one part of the problem.
  42. The correct answer is C (Graduation, since a connected weight line remains at the perimeter.): A solid, connected perimeter weight line with a shorter interior is the defining outcome of graduation, since graduation still reads as having a solid, connected weight line even though the interior is shorter.
